Contracts those chunks build to: icons carry meta `"icon"` = id on a `TextureRect` with a texture (`Icons.rect` does this); the shop's grid is `ShopWindow._cards`, each card with meta `"item_id"` and that item's own `Icons.for_shop_item` icon under it, and `brand_homie_depot` and `cash` icons in the window, which fits 1280x720 less 16 px; `JobBoardWindow._board_cards` children each hold a `task_*` icon; every phone `_tabs` button has `.icon`; the crew app shows a `rank_*` icon and the map one `hood_*` icon per hood; a text bubble is a node with meta `"text_bubble"` and an icon under it; `DayHud._rank_badge` is a `TextureRect`; `UIRoot._hud_rows` holds a `cash` icon; `task_list._task_line(task, done)` returns a Control holding a `task_<task>` icon (locked rows too); `HeatMeter` holds a `heat` icon; `UIRoot/InteractionPrompt` is a Control with a RichTextLabel.
- **Considered:** hand-drawn or bought icons (no artist, and they'd drift from the models; revisit if a hand-drawn set arrives: it replaces recipes one id at a time), rendering icons at runtime (slow startup, and impossible headless), and SVG (no 3D).

### 2026-11-02: The catalog windows' icon look lives in ui/catalog_look.gd (icons chunk D)

- **What:** the job board, payout receipt and day summary show icons (docs/GAME.md, "the catalog windows wear their icons"). The look they share is one preloaded static helper, `ui/catalog_look.gd`: `brand_title`, `wallet`, `shelf(icon, tint, owned, locked, tag)` (the item icon at `Icons.SIZE_CARD` on a tinted shelf with OWNED/lock stamps and an optional `price_tag`), `price_row`, `task_badges(tasks, names, lead)`, `task_line(task, label, missing_tool)`, `stars(rating)`, `hover(card, shelf)`, `pop(icon)` and `sfx(from, id)`. Every icon goes through `Icons.rect`, so it carries meta `"icon"` for the icons test.
- **The shop is shops:4's.** critic.md's W5 order (it overrides the icons spec) gives icons:D the job board, payout and day summary only, and the shop window to shops:4's tabbed rewrite, so icons:D leaves `ui/shop_window.gd` alone and the `shop` check stays `PENDING` under shops:4. `brand_title`, `wallet`, `shelf`, `price_tag`, `hover` and `pop` exist for those cards, so the shop and the board keep one look. `ui/job_board_window.gd` extends the shop window, so it preloads the icons and the look under its own names (`_BoardIcons`, `_BoardLook`): a subclass can't redeclare a parent's constant, and shops:4 may add `_Icons`/`_Look` to its parent. Notes for shops:4 from a trial of the old two-row grid: nine cards with a 92 px shelf, a price row and a header on two lines came to 872 px tall, over 1280x720; a one-line header and the price as a tag on the shelf brought it to about 645. Keep the "Unlocks: ..." words (icons spec chunk D) with the badge row under them. A confirmed buy should pop the icon only on the host's `Shops.result("ok")`, and the sale also redraws the cards, so remember the bought id for a moment and pop the redrawn card.
- **Fit:** the day summary's lines sit in a ScrollContainer capped at `ROWS_MAX_HEIGHT` (520), since a rank-up badge, a season and a town morning together outgrew the screen.
- **Sounds:** no `ka_ching`: critic C11/C12 made the purchase sound `register`, rung by `audio/cue_hooks.gd` on the host-confirmed `Shops.result("ok")`. Hover plays the existing `ui_tick`. `ui_pop` already exists (settings), for the phone in icons chunk E. Motion numbers are `Constants.ICONS_*`.
- **Test:** `--icons-test` (port 7810): `board` is live (its `PENDING` line is gone) and also wants a cash icon on every offer. Two new live checks draw made-up data into this peer's own windows (nothing is paid or banked): `receipt` (bag, task, mood, hose and medical bills, water, cash, star, bonus, xp and split icons, and the receipt fits) and `summary` (every line's icon, the rank badge and sparkle on a RANK UP, the unlock's, season's and hood's icons, and the card fits; it is hidden directly so its `closed` signal, the morning stinger, never fires). The pending `shop` check now checks each card by itself: the card's meta `"item_id"` and that item's own `Icons.for_shop_item` icon under it, since counting every icon under the grid let price-tag cash icons and task badges stand in for missing item icons.
- **W5 integration:** with shops:4 merged, its cards also carry meta `"item_id"` (beside its own `"item"`), the tools tab passes the per-card check, and the `shop` and `prompt_format` `PENDING` lines are gone, so both checks gate the run. The shop does not use `catalog_look.gd` yet (shelf, price tag, hover, pop); moving its cards onto those helpers is open for shops:7 or icons:E.
- **Considered:** putting the look in `shop_window.gd` as instance helpers (the payout and summary windows don't extend it, and shops:4 replaces it), and building the shop here and rebasing shops:4 onto it (the critic's wave order keeps one owner per window).

### 2026-11-02: The phone and the HUD show icons; the HUD rows are a VBox (icons chunk E)

- **What:** `ui/phone.gd`, `ui/day_hud.gd`, `ui/ui_root.gd`, `ui/task_list.gd` and `ui/heat_meter.gd` show icons (docs/GAME.md, "the phone and the HUD wear their icons"). Local presentation only: no RPC, replicated property, save format or yard/task generation changed, so no BUILD_VERSION bump.
- **HUD rows:** `ui_root._cleanup` (one Label of joined lines) is gone. `_hud_rows` (a VBoxContainer named `HudRows` at 16,16) holds three `Icons.row` rows and the `_status` Label. The rows read critic C7's three amount keys: `hud_wallet_amount` ("$%d"), `hud_cleanup_amount` and `hud_chefty_amount`. `hud_wallet_amount` matches `cash_amount` (the homeowner's cash Label3D) word for word but is its own key, so the HUD and the world label can change apart and a C7 reader finds it. The words-in-a-line keys it replaced (`hud_wallet`, `hud_cleanup_row`, `hud_chefty_row`, and their `FORMAT_ARGS` entries) are deleted. The status Label goes through `Icons.plain` (keycaps there are icons:F's or later). It is built before the automated-mode return, so `_hud_rows` always exists. The Chefty row uses `brand_chefty`, not the spec's `item_chefty`: the side-on truck is a sliver at 22 px. `ui_root` moves `TaskList` to sit `TASK_LIST_GAP` under the rows' combined height (never above its old y of 104), gliding at `Constants.ICONS_HUD_FOLLOW_RATE`; before, a NICE BOYS or hiding line drew under the job list.
- **Task list:** rows are kept and updated in place, rebuilt only when the task set changes (they were rebuilt every 0.25 s), so a check can animate in. `_task_line(task, done)` returns the row Control (the icons test's contract). Gotcha: a Container resets a child's scale and rotation when it sorts, and a child that just became visible makes it sort, so the check's grow-in tween uses `.from(Vector2.ZERO)` instead of setting the scale first.
- **Day HUD:** the clock is an HBox pushed right ([season] words [sun or moon]); `_rank_badge` is a TextureRect at (12,26), 64 px, set only when the tier changes; `_note` is a RichTextLabel through `Icons.rich`, set only when its words or color change, colored by `default_color` (not modulate, which would tint the keycaps). `hud_unread` gained an `{icon:unread}` prefix. The rank block fades out (`ICONS_HUD_FADE_SECONDS`) while the phone is up: the phone, anchored right, covers it.
- **Phone:** the dock is icon tiles (`expand_icon`, icon on top, `icon_max_width` = `Icons.SIZE_DOCK`); a tapped tile bounces (`ICONS_DOCK_*`) and plays `ui_pop`. Text bubbles carry meta `"text_bubble"` and an avatar from `Icons.TEXT_AVATARS`. Lines that wrap beside an icon leave room for it, so the phone keeps its 380 px width. `APPS` is unchanged (texts, clients, map, crew).
- **Test:** `--icons-test`'s `phone` and `hud` checks are live (their `PENDING` lines are gone). Only `prompt` (icons:F) and `catalog` (icons:catalog) are still pending.
- **Not done here:** the shop's cards still don't use `catalog_look.gd` (open for shops:7); the phone still appears and disappears in one frame (no slide).

### 2026-11-03: The interaction prompt is a screen-space pill; the money coin is a shader quad (icons chunk F)

- **What:** `ui/interaction_prompt.gd` (UIRoot/InteractionPrompt) is a full-rect `Control` (mouse ignored) holding a `PanelContainer` "Pill" with a `RichTextLabel` "Text"; the world `Label3D` and `PIXEL_SIZE` are gone. Each frame (`process_priority` 1000, after the cameras move) it projects the hint's `"at"` through `get_viewport().get_camera_3d()` and centers the pill there. `text_for(hint)` (plain, `Strings.keyed`) stays for settings_test; `rich_for(hint)` is the same through `Icons.rich(..., SIZE_PROMPT_KEY)`, stripped so a bare key has no trailing gap. `current_text` is what the prompt offers (the hint, not the fade or whether it's on screen); `hud_test._prompt_drawn` reads it now instead of the old Label3D. `present(hint, camera, delta)` is one frame of it, so the test can drive the pill with processing off. Local presentation only: no RPC, replicated property, save format or yard/task generation changed.
- **UI scale: no division.** critic.md C8 said to divide `unproject_position()` by `content_scale_factor`. Measured on 4.7.2 (a bare project at canvas_items/expand, `content_scale_factor` 1.25, window 1280x720): `unproject_position` returned a point in the 1024x576 canvas (`get_visible_rect()`) and a CanvasLayer dot drawn there sat on the box; dividing would have put it 20% off. So the point is used as is, and `--icons-test` checks a point dead ahead lands mid-canvas at UI scales 0.8 and 1.25.
- **Motion** (constants `ICONS_PROMPT_*`): fade in 0.12 s with an ease-out-back grow from 0.82, fade out 0.1 s in place; a new anchor more than 0.5 m away in one frame starts a screen-space glide from where the pill was (exponential, 16/s), so following a moving thing is exact while switching things is never a jump; new words bump the scale by 0.07. The pill is clamped 12 px inside the screen only while its point is within half a pill of the screen; past that it fades (a first take pinned it into the top-left HUD rows when the player turned away, `build/play/rec_pill_1p` frames 104-112; fixed in `rec_pill2_1p` frames 14-18).
- **Coin:** the money popup's coin is a `MeshInstance3D` "Coin" (a `QuadMesh` with a `ShaderMaterial`) under the popup's Label3D: its vertex shader does the billboard, the parent's pop scale and BaseMaterial3D's fixed-size math, and moves the quad beside the text (`shift`) and squeezes it for the spin (`spin`), both per-instance shader parameters. A `Sprite3D` was tried first: spinning by `scale.x` and holding it beside the text with `offset / scale.x` swung it out and back every half turn, because a Sprite3D's new offset reaches the screen a frame after its new scale (`build/play/rec_coin_1p` frames 70-72). `extra_cull_margin` covers the shift the AABB doesn't know about.
- **Test:** `--icons-test`'s `prompt` check is live (its `PENDING` line is gone) and drives the pill: keycaps (the hint's own key too), a bare key, escaped names, centered within 1.5 px, a retarget that moves under 60% of the way on its first frame and settles in a second, hidden behind the camera and far off screen, mid-canvas at UI scales 0.8 and 1.25, and down while the shop is open. A new `coin` check wants the cash texture, a constant leftward `shift` and a varying `spin`. Only `catalog` (icons:catalog) is still pending.
- **Review fixes (same chunk, 2026-11-03).**
  - **Where the Hide pill sits:** a hide hint's `at` was the spot's origin + 2.2 m, which from where you stand to dive in projects at or above the top of the screen, so the pill rode the top edge beside the day line and, a step closer, didn't show at all. `HideSpot.prompt_point(from)` (net/components/hide_spot.gd) puts it `ICONS_PROMPT_HIDE_ABOVE` (0.12 m) over the box's top, above the point of the box nearest the player (`ICONS_PROMPT_HIDE_INSET` in from its edges), capped at `ICONS_PROMPT_HIDE_MAX_Y` (1.9 m, a shed's door). Seen in `build/play/rec_pill6_1p`: frames 0 and 16 (stepped closer) and 66-86 the pill sits just over the hedge's top; 88-92 it fades in place on the dive; `rec_pill4_1p` frames 24-54 it walks along the hedge with the player and fades once out of reach.
  - **Retarget by identity:** "the anchor moved more than 0.5 m" both missed two things closer than that (two bags, a clump of weeds: the pill jumped) and restarted the glide every frame for a thing moving faster (Chefty at 19 m/s at 30 fps, a hitch frame: the pill trailed). A hint may now carry `"source"` (a node, or any value naming the thing: a hand task is `"<task>:<index>"`); `identity_of` is [source, text]. When both the last hint and this one have one, the pill glides exactly when the identity changes and otherwise follows the point exactly, however far it moved; a hint without one keeps the distance rule. player.gd's hints over a nearest-of-several thing (pickups, barrows, hand tasks, hide spots, ladders, buckets, water, the gas can's target, a grabbable player) carry their source. Prompt sources aren't sourced automatically: one source can offer several places with the same words (the Annex's windows), which must still glide.
  - **Out of the way of words:** the pill eases (at the glide rate) out of the NICE BOYS' bubbles (`Overheads.last_rects`) and any visible Label3D in group `prompt_avoid` (the keepers' and homeowners' captions and name tags): up above one it would touch, or below when above would leave the screen. `rec_store2_1p` frame 80: "[E] Shop" 6 px above Marty's "Morning. Your cots are where you left them." (it sat on his line in the first take, `rec_store_1p` frame 66).
  - **Edges:** while fading out the pill is no longer pushed inside the screen (it keeps the last push and goes with its point), so turning away doesn't slide it along the edge into the HUD corners. A window opening hides it at once rather than fading for 0.1 s.
  - **Tests:** `--icons-test` `prompt` adds two things 0.3 m apart with different sources (must glide), one source moving 0.6 m a frame (followed within 1.5 px), a keeper's line on the pill's spot (it must end clear above it, without jumping there in one frame), and the shop step now first waits for a live prompt (a prompt source in front of the camera) to be up and visible before opening the store; the yard's own `prompt_avoid` labels are taken out of the group while the pill is driven by hand. `hud_test`'s "prompt shown while free" half wants the pill visible and faded in (`_prompt_shown`), not just offered, with its fake source in front of the client's camera; the "held, so no prompt" half still reads `current_text`.
- **Not done here:** `ui/drive_hud.gd`'s key legend and `ui/job_goal.gd`'s goal line are still Labels through `Strings.keyed` (plain "[W]" text), and the rake pile's `Label3D` stays plain; they can become RichTextLabels through `Icons.rich` the same way. The rake hint is the hotbar's hint line (critic C6), already rich.

### 2026-11-01: Road markings are painted by the surface shader; seasons change the look through shader globals and a "seasonal" group

- **Roads:** the surface shader (`props/surfaces.gd`) gained a `road` pattern that paints the lines on the asphalt's top face in mesh-local space (along = local x, across = local z), and `Surfaces.material(pattern, color, accent, params)` takes a `params` Vector4 (for roads: line style, half width, dash length, dash period). The job street, `Neighborhood.road()` (the store's roads) and the country road are one mesh each instead of a box per dash, about 300 fewer MeshInstances. `LowPoly.paint(node, surface, params, accent)` stores metas `surface_params` and `surface_accent`, which `levels/shared_look.gd` passes on. Supersedes the dash boxes. Twelve more patterns came with it (stucco, stone, roof_tiles, corrugated, shakes, board_batten, gravel, pavers, lawn_stripes, cinderblock, tile_floor, road) for the neighborhood looks (art spec).
- **Seasons:** `Seasons.apply_look(day)` (already called on every peer by the day clock, from the replicated day) also sets the shader globals `season_snow` and `season_bloom` (declared in `project.godot` beside `season_foliage`; a global used before it is declared breaks every material, so they land together). Every surface lays snow on upward faces (world-space normal), slush only on roads and gravel, never on skin; lawns get flower dots. When the season actually changes, or on the first call, it calls `apply_season(season)` on group `seasonal`, so things built per season can rebuild locally; `Seasons.current()` and `Seasons.first_day_of(season)` are there for builders, the studio and tests.
- **Snow is opt-in for vertex-color meshes:** `Stylized.material` options `snow` (0-1, default 0) and `seasonal` (default true; false for meshes built per season, which skip the foliage tint). Characters, the first-person arms and held things use the default, so they never turn white. No Stylized prop opts in yet (YardModels' fences, hedges, sheds and cars stay clear until a later art chunk passes `snow: 1.0`).
- **Surfaces snow is opt-out:** `Surfaces.material(pattern, color, accent, params, snow = 1.0)` gains a fifth optional argument (part of the cache key; uniform `snow_cover`), fed from mesh meta `surface_snow` and `LowPoly.paint(node, surface, params, accent, snow = 1.0)`. Indoor meshes pass 0; `tile_floor` and `skin` never take snow regardless. Existing four-argument callers are unchanged.
- **Road contract extension (art spec C1):** the spec's road styles 0-5 stand, plus style 6 `single_yellow` (one dashed yellow centre line, used by the country road instead of style 1 with long dashes) and `params.w` = dash period (0 = twice the dash length). `Surfaces.ROAD_LINES` and `Surfaces.road_params(style, half_width, dash, period)` are the source of truth; later chunks build against them.
- **Networking:** none. Everything is a pure function of the replicated day and constant data; no RPC, replicated property, save key, or yard or task generation changed.
- **Verification:** shader changes are checked with windowed studio renders (`--subject=surfaces --season=0..3`), since headless runs use the dummy renderer and compile no shaders. The studio gained `--season`, `--hood`, `--size`, `--seed`, `--archetype` and a per-subject `views` meta.

### 2026-11-01: The balance lab is code; spine tuning stays in constants.gd

- **What:** `tools/balance_sim.py` (stdlib Python, gap balance:C) is the balance lab. It reads every tuning number straight out of `core/constants.gd`, `core/balance.gd`, `core/ranks.gd`, `core/seasons.gd`, `net/town/neighborhoods.gd` and `core/catalog.gd` through `tools/balance/gdconst.py` (a reader for top-level GDScript `const` declarations), mirrors `core/economy.gd` exactly and the town, job board, homeowner, campaign, hires, day clock and run director by hand, and plays crews of 1 to 4 through the campaign over 40 seeds in about 4 seconds. Its checks (`--check`) hold the arc to `Constants.ARC_HOURS`, `RANK_PACE_HOURS` and `ARC_SOLO_RATIO_MAX` (a new `# ---- balance targets` section the game itself never reads), plus price-to-income, hiring, days-per-tier and, later, endless-loop rules. Sim-only assumptions (rake speed, task minutes, how players choose) live in `tools/balance/model.json`, each with a source; money sinks in `tools/balance/sinks.json`, whose prices are `const:` paths into the constants, never numbers. `docs/BALANCE.md` is the manual and holds the baseline.
- **Why:** agents can't edit the workbook, and its Balance Lab still models the old eight-lot game (BAL-01/02 say 5 to 10 hours, CLAUDE.md says 10 to 20); `design/export_balance.py` only goes one way. A simulator that reads the source of truth can't drift from it, runs in CI, and gives the retune numbers instead of guesses. `core/balance.gd` stays generated and keeps only the workbook's values; the spine's tuning stays in `constants.gd` and `ranks.gd`.
- **Rules:** a change to a formula in `core/economy.gd`, or to the rules of `net/town/town.gd`, `levels/job_board.gd` `choose_tasks`, `npcs/homeowner.gd` `_judge`, `Campaign.finish_job`, `hires.gd` `pay_wages`, `day_clock.gd` or `director.gd` `_consider_roll`, changes its mirror in `balance_sim.py` in the same commit. A new constant the simulator reads goes in its `NEEDED` list, so a misparse stops the run (exit 2) and the engine parity check (`--engine-dump`, fed by balance:D's `--balance-test`) compares it with the engine. A new money sink gets a `sinks.json` entry. `--propose` only prints and writes `build/balance/proposal.json`; values reach the game by hand, in a commit that bumps `BUILD_VERSION` when they change what peers compute.
- **Until the retune:** today's numbers fail the blocking checks (the arc is 54 h solo and 29 h for four, raids run 4 to 5 per job, tools cost a fraction of a day's pay), so `--check --baseline` runs the checks without failing the exit code on the tuning checks; balance:E drops `--baseline`. `--baseline` never excuses the integrity checks (engine parity (f), the sink-price self-test): those say the simulator is wrong, not the numbers, and must block CI from balance:D on. `--set` refuses names the game doesn't have (the crew-scale hooks excepted), so a typo can't pass for a what-if.
- **Considered:** a "Spine Tuning" sheet in the workbook with a second generator (splits the source of truth and blocks agents; Taylan can add a read-only "Balance Sim" sheet from `report.json`), a GDScript simulator run headless (slower, and CI already has Python), and tuning by bot soaks alone (minutes per job and no picture of a 15-hour arc).

### 2026-11-01: The engine checks the balance lab in CI; saves remember their XP curve

- **What:** `--balance-test` (`net/dev/balance_test.gd`, port 7819, host only, done in about a second) dumps `get_script_constant_map()` of Constants, Balance, Ranks, Seasons, the neighborhoods and Catalog, runs a fixed grid of about 1,170 calls (`Ranks` tier math and `pay_bonus`/`tier_of`, `Seasons.of_day`/`year_of`, every `Catalog.price`, and every `core/economy.gd` function over its inputs), and checks invariants of the tables: every `shop:` unlock in `UNLOCKS` and `EXTRA_UNLOCKS` has a `Campaign.price_of`, every `TOOL_PRICES` tool unlocks by tier 25, every `hood:` unlock is in `HOODS`, the crew-scale tables (once balance:E adds them) have `MAX_PLAYERS` entries above 0, `RANK_PACE_HOURS` has a target per rank, rises, and ends inside `ARC_HOURS`, `tier_for_xp(xp_to_reach(k)) == k`, and the XP-curve migration keeps every tier. Its result is the probe's `balance_test` key `{ok, done, constants, cases, invariants}`; `ok` is the invariants. CI's "Balance simulator" step and `ci_local`'s `balance-sim` step (Python only; `-Only balance-sim` needs no Godot) then run `balance_sim.py --check --baseline --engine-dump <host.json>`, which fails on any constant or case that differs, on a case for a function the sim has no mirror of, and on a failed engine invariant, `--baseline` or not.
- **`constants` is a JSON string** inside `host.json`, not an object: `JUNK_PER_HOOD` has a `""` key, and PowerShell 5.1's `ConvertFrom-Json` (which `ci_local` runs on every host report) rejects empty keys and folds case. `balance_sim.py` decodes it; the cases and invariants stay plain JSON.
- **`crew.curve`:** `Campaign._save_data` writes `Ranks.CURVE_ID` ("first/step/endless") next to the crew's XP. `_load` passes the section through `Campaign.crew_xp_from_save`, which carries the XP from the saved curve (a save without the key is `Ranks.LEGACY_CURVE_ID`, "150/25/1200", never to be changed) onto today's with `Economy.migrate_xp`: same tier, same fraction into it, floored. Pending XP (today's, not yet banked) is kept as is. The key is additive, `SAVE_VERSION` stays 1, and while `CURVE_ID` is still "150/25/1200" no save changes, so play is identical; the first curve retune (balance:E) is when it starts moving XP. This is a save-format addition, so the wave's `BUILD_VERSION` bump covers it.
- **Why:** the sim's value is that it reads the game rather than a copy; this is the proof, every run, that its parser and its mirror still agree with the engine, and the migration is what lets balance:E retune the rank curve without costing any crew a rank.
- **Considered:** comparing only a hash of the constants (says something drifted, not what), and writing the dump to a file of its own (another path for CI and `ci_local` to agree on, when the probe report already carries every test's result).

### 2026-11-01: Houses are batched by HouseKit, described by a HouseSpec, and each neighborhood's look is data

- **HouseKit (`props/house_kit.gd`)** builds architecture (box, prism, hip, cylinder, window, glow) into one mesh per material (surface, color, accent, params, snow), painted by the surface shader; glass and glows keep their own materials, and glass joins group `window_glass`. A house is 10 to 20 draw calls instead of 60 to 80 MeshInstance3Ds; the committed holder carries meta `triangles` and `draw_calls`. Faces are flat and wound clockwise from outside by construction (each face is oriented away from an inside point its shape passes), and `tools/mesh_check.gd` checks that independently: with `HouseKit.audit` on, every shape's triangles are tested against the centroid of that shape's own vertices, so a wrong inside point shows up as inward faces (a deliberately wrong box proves the audit can fail). A winding-vs-stored-normal test would prove nothing, since the kit writes the normal from the winding. Patterns are measured in the holder's space, so bricks run continuously across neighboring boxes.
- **HouseBuilder (`levels/town/house_builder.gd`)** builds any archetype from a req (archetype, width, depth, stories, colors and surfaces, garage, seed, detail) at any point, facing +Z with the origin at the footprint's ground center, and returns root `House` with **HouseSpec** metas (archetype, stories, windows `[[pos, normal, size]]`, roof `{kind, center, width, half_run, rise, top}`, roof_edge_y, gutters, solids, roof_solids, roof_blockers, porch_depth, door, spigot, eaves, footprint, triangles, draw_calls, and all of it as `house_spec`). **`roof_blockers`** are boxes `[center, size]` for whatever stands on the climbable roof or across its eaves (dormers, cross-gables like the cottage's bay, the lake house's glass gable and the colonial's garage, door hoods, porticos and pediments, chimneys): art chunk 6 skips clear-roof spots inside them and gives them colliders. **Gutters and eaves are split** around every solid and roof blocker they would run through (clearance `ART_HOUSE_LINE_CLEARANCE` 0.1 m, pieces shorter than `ART_HOUSE_MIN_LINE` 0.4 m dropped), so one eave can be several pieces and no gutter spot, gutter mesh or string light lands inside a bay wall or a hood; `check()` samples every gutter and eave against the solids and blockers and requires a gutter piece on both the front and back eave. The estate's portico is a little narrower than first built (just past the outer capitals) so even a 5 m core keeps some front gutter. Detail `far` is the solids as boxes, the main roof, and two glass panes (about 100 triangles). A pure function of the req: no global randomness, no colliders, nothing networked.
- **Footprint contract:** x within +-(w/2 + `ART_HOUSE_SIDE_REACH` 0.6), z within -d/2 - `ART_HOUSE_BACK_REACH` (0.7, the roof overhang) to d/2 + `ART_HOUSE_FRONT_REACH` (2.6, porch and steps). The spec said back 0.6 and front 2.4; today's classic house reaches 0.7 and 2.6 and has to stay identical, and the lot generator reserves -0.6 .. +3.0 on the ground (the 0.7 is roof overhang, overhead). Every job archetype's main roof is a climbable gable along x (`roof.kind == "gable_x"`) with gutters on its front and back eaves; only `a_frame` and `modern_glass` (`HouseArchetypes.NEIGHBOR_ONLY`) break that, and no style offers them for a job house. `HouseBuilder.check(house)` lists contract breaks.
- **15 archetypes** (`levels/town/house_archetypes.gd`): classic (today's house, shape for shape), bungalow, cottage, foursquare, single_wide, double_wide, colonial_new, split_level, hoa_standard, lake_cabin, lake_house, a_frame, estate, modern_glass, farmhouse. New roofs are two slabs with a gable-end wall prism tucked under them (so eaves have a soffit and the gable end is wall, not roof); the classic keeps its solid shingled prism.
- **Each neighborhood's look is data:** `levels/town/hood_styles.gd`, one dictionary per `net/town/neighborhoods.gd` id (archetypes by stories, neighbor-only archetypes, uniformity, frontage, palettes with surfaces, fences, beds, trees, ground, street, mailbox, cars, yard props, backyard, set pieces, sign keys, art-test signatures, decor style, chalk, back boundary), falling back to maple_row (`style()` reports `fallback`). Mansion Hill's frontage is 60, not 88 (gap-specs/critic.md C25). `net/town/neighborhoods.gd` keeps its unused `look` entries until the yards switch over (art chunk 6).
- **`Neighborhood.house()`** is now a thin wrapper over `HouseBuilder.build(... "classic")`, so the job yard's house, the street's neighbors and the countryside's farmhouses keep their shapes, colors and triangle counts, batched; its metas gain the size as a third element of each `windows` entry (callers read the first two). Window order, roof, gutters and the yard's colliders are unchanged. It is not pixel-identical, on purpose: besides the pattern phase (patterns now run in house space), the classic roof's shingle courses now run parallel to the eaves, as real shingles do (the old PrismMesh painted them in its own rotated space, so they ran down the slope); the stoop under the porch roof takes no snow; and window mullions are painted planks like the frames (they used to pick up the house's siding).
- **Budgets** live in `core/constants.gd` (`# ---- art`): ART_HOUSE_MAX_TRIANGLES 6000 (the largest archetype measures about 3,500), plus the prop, tree, LOD, batching and visual budgets the later art chunks enforce.
- **Not yet:** `HouseBuilder.request_for(hood, seed, size, spec)` (critic C25) comes with `LotBuilder` in art chunk 5; job yards switch to their neighborhood's archetypes in art chunk 6.
- **Networking:** none. No RPC, replicated property, save key, or yard or task generation changed; the job yard builds the same house, spots and colliders as before.

### 2026-11-01: The NICE BOYS' look is built in code from one dress helper; the cartoon body is over its triangle budget

- **One dress helper:** `CrewMember.dress(body, index)` (static, `npcs/crew_member.gd`) dresses an unbuilt `player/cartoon_body.gd` as NICE BOY number `index` (1 is the head NICE BOY). The crew's `_dress_up`, `tools/studio` (`nice_boys`, `nice_boy`) and `--cast-test` all use it, so the studio and the test see exactly what the game shows. The look is a pure function of the node name (`Crew<N>`), and the name tag (`Strings.crew_tag(index, day)`) of that and the replicated day: no RPC, no replicated property, no save key. `crew_member.tscn`'s label default is now empty and its synchronizer is untouched.
- **CartoonBody gains** `outfit` ("", "nice_boy", "nice_boy_lead"), `grin`, `shades_style` ("aviator" or "plain"), hat style "visor", and gestures "point" and "scribble". An outfit's parts are named MeshInstance3D nodes (`CartoonBody.OUTFIT_PARTS`: Socks, Collar, Lanyard, Badge, Insignia, FannyPack, VisorSeal, Clipboard, or Megaphone and Whistle for the lead) on the existing `_torso`, `_hips`, `_legs`, `_arms` and head pivots; a feature that rebuilds the body (shops' `rebuild()`, hotbar's `set_hold`) must keep those pivots and re-run `_build_outfit()`. Everyone else is unchanged: the defaults are off and `dress_from_seed` never picks a visor or lavender.
- **Gesture direction:** a positive x turn swings a hanging arm forward (-Z, where the body faces), checked in studio renders. "point" and "scribble" use positive turns. Several older gestures ("offer", "punch", "watch", "rake") use negative turns, which swing the arm backward; not changed here, flagged for whoever next touches the body's animation.
- **The Daisy Seal** is `props/nice_seal.gd`: `add(kit, center, radius, facing, detail, two_sided)` draws flat stacked layers into an existing MeshKit (so a patch merges into one mesh), facing any direction; `node(radius, facing)` returns a two-sided stylized MeshInstance3D for the beacon and studio. About 100 triangles at chest size. `MeshKit` gained `triangle(a, b, c, normal, color)` for flat decals.
- **Triangle budget finding:** a plain cartoon body measures **8,082 triangles** (`--cast-test` step 9, `dress_from_seed(3)`), against docs/ART.md's 5,000 per character, before any outfit (the detail-3 head and hair and the detail-2 eyes, pupils, brows, mouth and shoes). The test therefore reports the total and budgets only the outfit's extra over a plain body: `Constants.CREW_OUTFIT_TRIANGLE_BUDGET` (1,200). Measured: +967 for the head NICE BOY (9,049 in all), +895 for a NICE BOY (8,977). Outfit parts use blob detail 0 or 1, boxes and tubes of 8 sides or fewer (the cast spec's rule), never `rbox` (detail 3, 1,280 triangles). **Deviation from the cast spec:** tubes that cover a body part match its sides, or the part pokes through between facets (an 8-sided collar's flat faces sit 7.6% inside its radius, into the 12-sided shirt collar). The shorts, socks and sock stripe are 10-sided like the plain leg, the collar and visor band 12, and the megaphone's horn, rim and bell 10. That costs 48 triangles on a NICE BOY and 62 on the head NICE BOY over 8 sides, inside the 1,200 outfit budget. Bringing the base body under 5,000 is a separate task (the critic's gap list, "Character art").
- **Test:** `--cast-test` now has a client (port 7812; ci_local `Client = 40`, the netcode.yml step starts a Target client). Phase B (steps 9-10) checks the outfit parts, visor, grin and triangle budget on scratch bodies; phase C (steps 13-16) adds full heat and calls a raid (all `HEAT_MAX_CREW` NICE BOYS, so plain NICE BOYS reach the client too) on day 7 (`--start-day=7` in ci_local and netcode.yml; the test fails on day 1, the client's default before the host's state arrives, so the tags prove the day replicated), freezes the crew, and checks each NICE BOY's outfit, name tag and hidden capsule; CI requires the host's and client's `crew_cast` probe snapshots to match. Steps 11-12 (van, beacon) are behind `PHASE_B_VAN` for cast:5 and step 17 (barks) behind `PHASE_C_BARKS` for cast:6.
- **Considered:** a separate crew body scene (two bodies to keep animating alike), and a total-triangle assertion (it would fail before the outfit adds anything).

### 2026-11-02: The NICE BOYS' van lives in its own file and moves itself; it pulls up on every peer from the director's phases (cast chunk 5)

- **One file for the van:** `props/nice_van.gd` (`NiceVan.build(parent, paint)`) builds the Courtesy Shuttle's "VanLook": `Shell` (the cached body mesh, a cached see-through `Glass` mesh with its own transparent material, the emissive `Dome` with a `DomeLight`, a `CabinLight`, the glows), `Livery` (the Label3D lettering from `Strings.UI` van_side, van_motto, van_rear), `Wheels` (four MeshInstance3Ds that roll), `Solid` (the same five collision boxes as before) and `Motion`. `Vehicles.build_van(parent, color)` keeps its signature and hands over; `Vehicles.VAN_PAINT`, `VAN_BAND` and `BEACON_AMBER` are the cast spec's constants. R2 (the catch and the ride redo) reuses the file as is. Meshes are cached per paint color; side-profile shapes (the cab doors follow the windshield's slant) are extruded convex prisms, so window holes are real holes the glass sits in.
- **The van animates itself:** `NiceVan.Motion` (an inner Node class under VanLook) reads only how the VanLook moves on physics ticks: it rolls the wheels by the distance driven, sways the `Shell` and `Livery` on a spring by the acceleration (nose down braking, up pulling away, out of turns; `Constants.VAN_SWAY_*`), flares the brake lights below `VAN_BRAKE_LIGHT_ACCEL`, and pulses the dome. Jumps over `VAN_TELEPORT_METERS` per tick are placements, ignored. So whoever moves the van (the ride, the arrival, a test, the studio) just moves it. Riders are seated on the ride's `Seats`, not under the Shell, so they don't sway (the sway is at most 2°).
- **The pull-up is local and timed from shared state:** `net/run_events/van_arrival.gd`, a child the run director adds on every peer, reads the director's replicated phase, `arrival_time()` and `phase_started_at()` and `Net.server_time()`. During the warning's last `VAN_ARRIVE_SECONDS` it drives a cosmetic van along `NiceVan.arrival_curve(director.van_park_point())` (cruise, then even braking, `arrive_progress`), parks for the chase (solid only while parked, so it never shoves anyone while driving), and when the phase ends either steps aside for the ride's van (`Rides/Van` is riding, or stands at the park spot) or drives off down the street (`VAN_LEAVE_*`). Its sounds play once each at their server-time moments, and not at all when that moment is more than 0.5 s past (a hitch, a phase change seen late): `van_honk` (new in `tools/make_sfx.py`, the van's honk-honk without the `van` one-shot's door bonk) as it starts down the street, `skid` as it brakes, `door_slam` as it parks, and `van` (bonk, honk, putt-putt) when it pulls away from parked. The first placement of each new van snaps (resets physics interpolation and `Motion.settle()`), whatever point of the pull-up it starts at, because the reused holder still stands where the last van left. **Late joiners don't see it:** the director's phase reaches peers only at its transitions (`_rpc_phase`), with no state sync on join, so a client that joins mid-raid sees QUIET and no van until the next phase change. Cosmetic only; fixing it is a director state request on join, an RPC change. No RPC, no replicated property.
- **The swap can't be seen:** `director.van_park_point()` (lot + `VAN_PARK_OFFSET`, the old inline start) is where the van parks and where `_build_van` starts the ride. The ride's road is `NiceVan.departure_curve(from, to)`: straight along the street for `VAN_LEAD_OUT`, then the old lifted curve to the border; it starts at the same point and heading the arrival curve ends with (`--cast-test` step 11 checks the poses match to 1 cm and 1°). `ride.gd` places a van with `NiceVan.depart_progress(share, duration, length)`: a real pull-out (`VAN_PULL_ACCEL` for `VAN_PULL_SECONDS`) and then a cubic that makes up the fixed ride time and stops at rest at the border, instead of the old constant 177 m/s from a standstill. Chefty's legacy ride is unchanged. The ride's duration, riders and timing (the `_rpc_depart` message) are unchanged.
- **The beacon:** `npcs/arrival_beacon.gd` keeps `set_urgency()` and the amber column, and adds `Seal`, `Cones`, `Notices` and `Label` children, `dismiss()` (folds away over `BEACON_DISMISS_SECONDS`, then frees itself; `ui/run_cues.gd` calls it instead of `queue_free`) and `advance(seconds)` for the studio. **Deviation from the cast spec:** the seal floats at column height + 1.9 m (spec: + 1.0) and the label at the seal + 1.9 m (spec: column + 2.4), because at + 1.0 the seal's ribbon tails sank into the column and read pink through its transparent amber; and the small THE NICE BOYS on the right is on the parked-open sliding door rather than the cab's quarter panel, which carries the seal.
- **Test:** `--cast-test` `PHASE_B_VAN` is on. Step 11: the livery's lettering equals the UI strings and passes the guardrail, `Solid` keeps 5 boxes, 4 wheels, glass that is see-through, the dome and Motion exist; the parked pose equals the ride's start; the pull-up runs 0 to 1 and creeps the last bit; the ride pulls out like a van and never backs up on a long and a short road. Step 12: the beacon's four parts, a spinning seal, eight notices, the label's text, and a dismissed beacon freed within its fold time. Step 14b (phase C, a real raid): the van is parked at `van_park_point()` when the crew arrives.
- **Networking:** none. No RPC, replicated property, save key, or yard or task generation changed; BUILD_VERSION needs no bump for this chunk.
- **Considered:** keeping the van in `props/vehicles.gd` (R2 and driving:F need it alone, and it outgrew a helper); a host-replicated van pose (a synchronizer or RPC stream for something every peer can compute from the phase and the server clock, and a BUILD_VERSION bump); spawning the van only at capture time as before (it popped in beside the yard, and the NICE BOYS arrived with no vehicle); animating the wheels and sway from the arrival and ride code (each mover would repeat it, and they'd drift apart).
- **Revisit:** when R2 redoes the catch and the ride (the NICE BOYS should climb out of the parked van instead of spawning at `CREW_ARRIVAL_OFFSET`, about 30 m away, and the fixed 8 s ride should become a real drive); when the director gets a late-join state sync (the van then shows for late joiners for free); when driving:H places the lot in the town (the van parks on bare grass with `--town-map --start-zone=lot`); and if the 60 m arrival pop-in (`VAN_ARRIVE_DISTANCE`) shows on a map with longer sight lines.

### 2026-11-01: The Settings window: a shared UI kit, input caught before the game, buttons added in code

- **UI kit:** `ui/ui_style.gd` (static, no class_name) holds the Homie Depot catalog look: `PAPER`, `INK`, `ORANGE`, `CARD`, `SOFT`, and `box`, `text`, `button`, `paint_button`, `pill`/`set_pill`, `card_button`/`paint_card`, `slider`, `check`, `choice`. Slider grabbers and the on/off switch are small textures drawn in code and cached, so they read on paper (Godot's default switch is white on white). New windows use it; `shop_window.gd` and the others keep their private copies until a global Theme is built from it (critic.md gap 4).
- **The window** is `ui/settings_window.gd`, a PanelContainer in group `settings_window`, built in code. `ui/ui_root.gd` adds it last (with a full-screen dim `ColorRect` behind it), so it draws over the HUD and its `_input` runs before the phone's and the game's (Godot calls `_input` last-in-tree first). API: `ui_root.open_settings(from_pause := false, tab := "sound")`, `close_settings()`; the window's `show_tab`, `listen(action, slot)`, `binding_text(action, slot)`, `current_tab`, `row_count`, `row_labels`, `is_listening`, and the `closed` signal. It counts in `any_window_open()`. While open and not listening, its `_input` swallows every key event that is not a GUI navigation action (`GUI_ACTIONS`: ui_accept, ui_up, ui_focus_next and so on), so the world's `_unhandled_input` handlers (clerk, job board, job post, zone gate, cot, homeowner, the player's camera, interact and take) never see game keys behind the form; this is done once in the window instead of gating each handler on `any_window_open()`. `open()` moves keyboard focus onto the open tab's pill and `close_window()` gives it back; a key that arrives while focus has wandered outside the window (arrow navigation can reach the menu buttons behind the backdrop) only pulls focus back. While listening, a left click binds Left click only when it lands on the waiting slot; elsewhere it cancels and passes through. The main-menu, lobby and pause buttons are made in code, not in `ui_root.tscn`, to keep scene merges simple; the pause button sits at -208..-168 (critic C7) and the FPS label at the top center.
- **Input:** while a slot listens, every key and mouse event is taken in `_input` and marked handled, so nothing reaches the GUI, the phone or the player. Esc (cancel) and Backspace (clear) are hard-wired to the physical keys, so a rebound pause can never trap a player in the window; otherwise the `pause` action closes the window before `ui_root` can toggle pause, and `phone` is swallowed. The Input singleton still sees the press (gap-specs/settings.json risks), and `Settings.bind` releases the actions it touches. A refusal (the wheel on a held action, pause's last key) keeps the slot listening and says why in the footer.
- **Controls never write back on redraw:** each row redraws from `Settings.changed` with `set_value_no_signal`, `set_pressed_no_signal` and `OptionButton.select`, so presets, resets and reloads move the controls without echoing a write. UI scale applies when its slider is let go, not while dragging, or the window would rescale under the mouse. The display mode is set with `persist false` and committed by "Keep" (or reverted by "Revert", closing the window, or `Constants.DISPLAY_REVERT_SECONDS`).
- **Sounds** `ui_tick`, `ui_pop` and `ui_stamp` are synthesized by `tools/make_sfx.py`, registered in `audio/sound_bank.gd` on the UI bus, and played through the `sfx` group node (ticks at most every `Constants.SETTINGS_TICK_GAP_MS`), so headless runs log them for the test without making players.
- **Keys tab groups** come from `Constants.KEYBIND_GROUPS`, now with a `pockets` group for `slot_1..5`, `slot_next` and `slot_prev` (critic C15); anything else lands under "Other".
- **Dev flag** `--open-settings=<tab>` (plus `keys:listen` and `keys:swap`) opens it for windowed screenshots. `--settings-test` gained its `window` check (spec check 15).
- **Networking:** none. No RPC, replicated property, save format, or yard or task generation changed.

### 2026-11-01: One grip table drives every hold, first and third person (hotbar chunk C)

- **What:** `props/prop_looks.gd` owns how things are held. `TOOL_GRIPS` (moved from `player/first_person_arms.gd`, same values) gives the aimed tools' hand spots; `HOLDS` gives every other carried kind `{at, yaw, roll, tilt, pivot, grips}` in the holder's body space (origin at the feet, -Z ahead). `pose_held(prop, holder)` places the gnome, trash can, TurfMasters sign and wheelbarrow from the holder's smoothed Visual; `pose_tool` uses the same table for tools that are carried rather than aimed (the shouldered ladder, an unhooked hose). `grip_points(prop, holder)` returns world `[right, left]` (`Vector3.INF` for a free hand) for every kind: rake (its Pose), leaf bag (`BAG_RIM`), aimed tools, the pressure washer's wand (`WAND_GRIPS`) and `HOLDS`. Both `first_person_arms.gd` (`_on_item`) and `CartoonBody.set_hold` read it, so your own arms and everyone's view of you can't disagree.
- **CartoonBody** gains `set_hold(right, left)`, `shoulder_position(side)` and `arm_direction(side)` (side 0 is the right, the order of `grip_points`). `_apply_hold` runs after the gestures each tick: it turns each arm pivot's -Y onto its target (`Quaternion(DOWN, dir)`), blended over `Constants.HOLD_BLEND_SECONDS`, and scales the arm's length by `clamp(distance / HOLD_ARM_REACH, HOLD_ARM_SHORTEST, HOLD_ARM_LONGEST)` so a single-segment arm lands its mitten on a close or slightly far grip. Off while fallen. Targets are stored relative to the body's parent (the Visual), set from `player.gd _process` every frame on every peer (INF when nothing is held, flat on the back, or carried), so the frame between setting and aiming can't drag them. NPCs never call it. Each tick the arms' y turn and scale are reset before the walk and gestures, so a hold leaves nothing behind.
- **Looks in world space:** a held look (HOLDS or an aimed tool) is made `top_level` and placed in world space, like the rake's Pose, so the prop's own smoothed Visual (or the holder's client moving it to `_hold_position`) can't drag it off the hands; letting go puts it back under the Visual at rest. The host also places the held body of a HOLDS kind at `at` and turns it to the holder's yaw plus `yaw` (`net_prop._hold_position`, `_host_step`), so a set-down starts from the hands and lands upright.
- **Proof on the wire:** `Hotbar.snapshot`'s `grip_ok` is now `PropLooks.grips_ok`: the held thing's grips resolve on this peer, within `HOLD_GRIP_REACH` of the holder's shoulders. CI's `Same(host.hotbar, client.hotbar)` therefore proves the client resolves the host's held rake from its own replicated view. `--hotbar-test` step 9 checks the sign (one hand, on the model, in reach), the gnome (upright, facing ahead, both hands), the trash can, the wheelbarrow (base within 0.15 m of the ground, handles 0.65 to 0.95 m up), the ladder and mower, and that the right arm's -Y points at its grip (dot > 0.9). Windowed with `--screenshot`, step 9 lingers 4 s on each hold, on a clear spot of its own, for first-person and selfie shots. `tools/studio` subjects `holds` (`--kinds=gnome,trash` closes in on some) and `gestures` render them.
- **Gesture direction fixed:** "offer", "punch" (and its shoulder twist), "watch" and "rake" now use positive x turns (forward), per the rule recorded with the NICE BOYS' look; "rake" leans the torso forward (negative x, as walking does). The hired hand's own rake (`CrewHand.add_hand_rake`, `HAND_RAKE_AT`/`HAND_RAKE_TURN`, also drawn by the studio) moved from behind the hand to the ground in front of them to match.
- **TurfMasters sign labels** are one-sided and a little smaller (`pixel_size` 0.003), so the far face's text no longer shows through mirrored past the panel's edges now that signs are held up in people's faces.
- **Networking:** none changed. No RPC, replicated property, save key, or yard/task generation; holds are a pure function of replicated state (held_by, kind, the holder's transform and head pitch) on every peer. The held body's position on the host moved for the gnome, trash can, sign and wheelbarrow (it replicates as before; collision is off while held).
- **Considered:** two-bone third-person arms (a bigger change to a body already over its triangle budget, and every gesture would need rewriting); grip targets in world space computed at aim time (one frame of drift at a walk); keeping the hat-in-your-face gnome hold from the spec (it covered the middle of the view up to the horizon, so the gnome sits 12 cm lower and nods forward).

### 2026-11-02: The hotbar HUD is a pure view of the props; it owns the bottom band (hotbar chunk B)

- **What:** `ui/hotbar_hud.gd` (UIRoot/HotbarHud, after ThrowMeter) draws the bar with `_draw` from `static func model(tree, peer_id)`: the slots (`Hotbar.slots_of`, each `slot_view`: kind, count, name, icon, abbr, tier, paint, gas, hooked), the selection (`Player/Pockets.selected`, predicted), what's in hand, its name and hint, and the hands card (`hands_view`). It sends nothing; everything comes from replicated prop fields and Pockets' signals (`refused` shakes it and toasts the reason, `notice` toasts the emptied-pockets line). Its `last_model`, `toast_text` and `shake_left` are public, so `--hotbar-test` step 13 and remote play read what's on screen without pixels.
- **Visibility** is one pure rule, `shown_when({in_session, zone, window_open, seated, carrying})`: off with any window (`Ui.any_window_open`, which covers the pause buttons and Settings opened from them), in a Chefty seat, in Border Return; on in the LOT zone; elsewhere only while carrying. A toast draws even with the bar down (a capture's line arrives on the way to Border Return), never over a window.
- **Icons** come from `ui/icons.gd` (`Icons.for_kind`, critic C9) behind `Icons.has`, so a kind without art (the gas can today) gets a colored chip with `Strings.UI["hotbar_abbr_<kind>"]` instead of a warning. The hint line is a RichTextLabel through `Icons.rich` (critic C6), so `{key:}` tokens become keycaps once settings:4 tokenizes `hud_rake_hint`; the card's G keys already do. The slot's key label reads `Icons.key_label("slot_N")`, so it follows rebinding.
- **Layout band** (critic C7), all in `Constants` under `# ---- hotbar hud`: slots 68 px with 6 px gaps, 8 px off the bottom edge (the spec's 12 px left no room for the name and hint lines under the goal line), the raised selection up to about 86 px, the name and hint lines above that, the job goal line's bottom at `HOTBAR_GOAL_LINE_Y` (122 px, and it now grows upward so a long goal can't run into the bar), the throw meter at `HOTBAR_THROW_METER_Y` (168 px). The separate `_rake_hint` label in ui_root is gone; its text is the rake's hint line.
- **Sounds:** `slot_tick` (UI bus), `pocket`, `draw` (Sfx) and `jangle` (Sfx, played by chunk D) are in `audio/sound_bank.gd`, made by `tools/make_sfx.py`. Pockets plays `pocket` when what was in hand went into a pocket and `draw` only when what's in hand now was in a pocket last frame: a pickup off the ground isn't a draw (critic C12).
- **Dev:** `--fill-pockets` (host) picks up the rake and every pocketable job thing once the yard is up, for screenshots; remote play's result has `hotbar` {selected, slots, hands}.
- **Networking:** none. No RPC, replicated property, save key, or yard/task generation changed.
- **Considered:** building the bar from Control nodes (a dozen nodes per slot re-laid out every frame for the raise and shrink; `_draw` is simpler and cheaper); reading `shop_window.TOOL_COLORS` for chips (shops:4 rewrites that window, so the HUD keeps its own copy); keeping the rake hint as its own always-on label (it would sit under the bar).

### 2026-11-01: Props come from one catalog and are drawn in batches; water has its own shaders

- **PropCatalog (`props/prop_catalog.gd`)** is the one registry for neighborhood props: `mesh(kind, color, size)` (cached by kind, color and, for sized kinds only, size snapped to 0.1 m), `node()`, `place(parent, kind, xform, color, opts)`, `kinds()`, `options(kind)`, `SIMPLE`, `SIGNATURE`. It asks four builder files in turn (`props/street_props.gd` with the vehicles, `props/yard_extras.gd`, `props/estate_props.gd`, `props/season_props.gd`; each a `KINDS` list, a `SIZED` list and `build(kind, kit, color, size) -> bool`), then falls back to `props/yard_models.gd`. 137 kinds. The builders share shorthand shapes in `props/prop_parts.gd` (posts, bars, slabs, soft boxes at detail 1, rings, cheap wheels, two-sided cards and leaves, `poly()` for hand-built faces such as boat hulls). Meshes carry metas `triangles`, `aabb` and `height`, recorded at build time so the headless art test can read them. No `class_name`: the props layer is preloaded, like HouseKit.
- **Frame and size conventions:** origin at the ground center, the front facing -Z (sign faces, car noses, lamp arms; a Label3D reads from -Z when turned PI). `color` Color.WHITE and `size` Vector3.ONE mean "the kind's own default"; only the kinds in a builder's `SIZED` read size (fence, wall and snowbank runs are size.x long; a pool's water is size.x by size.z; a dock reaches size.z out toward -Z from its shore end). Things that hang (icicles, string lights, a wreath) hang from their origin.
- **SIMPLE vs place():** a kind is SIMPLE (batchable) unless its look needs something a vertex-colored mesh can't carry; those are `COMPLEX` and only come through `place()`: lamp lenses (emissive, group `street_lamps`), pool, hot tub and fountain water and the fountain's falling sheets, the jack-o'-lantern's lit face, string-light bulbs, boat names, and required words (stop, speed, for-sale, monument, arch, gatehouse, lemonade). Optional words (a violation flag's NOTICE OF VIOLATION, a bus bench ad) are added by `place()` when asked, so the flags still batch and only the nearest few get a Label3D (critic risk 3).
- **PropBatch (`props/prop_batch.gd`)**: `new(root, chunk)`, `add(kind, xform, color, size)`, `commit() -> int`, `instance_count()`, `hash()`, `views()`. One MultiMeshInstance3D per 32 m chunk (`ART_PROP_CHUNK`) and mesh, positioned at its chunk's center so the visibility range is measured from there, `use_colors` off (color is in the cached mesh). Fading and shadows are one rule shared with `node()`, `PropCatalog.fade()`: shorter than `ART_SMALL_PROP_HEIGHT` fades at `ART_SMALL_PROP_RANGE` and casts no shadow, the rest at `ART_MEDIUM_PROP_RANGE`, both over `ART_PROP_FADE_MARGIN` (new, 6 m); anything `ART_LARGE_PROP_SIZE` (new, 8 m) long never fades (barns, docks, tennis courts), but the shadow rule goes by height alone, so a long, low run (a road-wide speed bump, a snowbank, icicle or string-light run, a tulip bed) never fades and still casts no shadow. Batches carry metas `prop_kind` and `prop_instances` and join `art_signature:<id>` (`PropCatalog.SIGNATURE`), so the art test counts batched props by summing `prop_instances` over a group. `hash()` digests every add (kind, color, size, transform snapped to 1 cm) in order for the art hash; it uses `Array.hash()` because the method name shadows the global.
- **Materials:** catalog props use `props/stylized.gd` with `snow: 1.0` (snow settles on their upward faces) and `seasonal: false` (props are built per season, so swaying flowers must not take the autumn foliage tint); vehicles and metal are glossier; laundry, flags, flowers and the inflatable snowman sway (`WIND` 0.06).
- **Water (`props/water_surface.gd`):** four shared ShaderMaterials, `pool` (turquoise, drifting caustic bands), `lake` (vertex waves, ripple normals, a fresnel fade to the horizon), `fountain` (a transparent sheet scrolling down a ribbon with UVs, `sheet()`), `frozen` (opaque ice with Voronoi cracks; `lake_kind(season)` picks it in winter, and `place()` freezes pools and stops fountains in winter). Animated from TIME on each peer; nothing feeds back. `task_looks.add_pool` keeps its StandardMaterial.
- **Deviation from the art spec:** string-light bulbs are one MultiMesh per run with per-bulb colors and a tiny emissive shader reading the instance color, not one StandardMaterial per color (one draw call per run instead of five). The light styles are `classic` (cycling colors), `approved` (warm white, identical), `homemade` (mismatched, about one bulb in eight dead) and `pro` (bigger bulbs).
- **Legacy kinds over budget:** the YardModels kinds the catalog falls back to (`FALLBACK_KINDS`: shed 12,808 triangles, wheelbarrow 9,944, dumpster 7,296, trash 4,304, ...) predate the budget and are listed, not failed, by `tools/prop_check.gd`; a block should not batch them by the hundred. The catalog has its own light `gnome` (about 450 triangles; YardModels' is 3,270).
- **Checked by** `tools/prop_check.gd` (also run by `tools/mesh_check.gd`): every kind under `ART_PROP_MAX_TRIANGLES` (heaviest: the holiday tree lot, 1,506), winding agrees with stored normals (it caught MeshKit's `leaf_card`, whose back faces carry the front normal, so props use `PropParts.leaf()`), `place()` gives every labeled kind its words and every lamp a `street_lamps` lens, every kind and sign a hood style names exists, SIGNS and BOAT_NAMES pass `net/dev/guardrail.gd` and the art word rule, PropBatch chunks, fades and hashes the same twice, and the water materials build. The CI art test with these budgets (A7) is art chunk 7.
- **Networking:** none. No RPC, replicated property, save key, or yard or task generation changed; nothing places these props in the game yet (art chunks 5 and 6 do).

### 2026-11-01: Trees are built per season, keep their limbs all year, and draw a far mesh past 55 m

- **Seasonal trees (`props/trees.gd`, art chunk 4):** `Trees.seasonal_form(kind, crown, season) -> [kind, crown shown, form]` decides what a tree wears: autumn as asked; winter `bare` for broadleaf, birch and willow (limbs, twigs, snow tufts) and `snowy` for pine and cypress (snow caps); spring `blossom_pink` for a red broadleaf, `blossom_white` for a yellow one, `spring_green` for everything else (birches and willows don't blossom); summer `deep_green` for red, rust and orange crowns, `green` for the rest. A tree's shape comes from (kind, autumn crown, variant) only, the same seed as before, and only its leaves change, so the maple on the corner keeps its limbs from autumn to winter. Crown meshes carry their season's colors, so the tree material sets `seasonal: false` (no `season_foliage` tint) and `snow: 1.0` (snow settles on bare limbs and pine tiers).
- **`Trees.spawn()` keeps its signature** and now returns a `props/tree_node.gd` root (name `Tree`, metas `kind`, `form`, `crown`) in group `seasonal`; `apply_season(season)` swaps both meshes from the cache. Every caller (store, surroundings, countryside, job yards, town, fallback houses) picks this up with no change. A tree added to the scene in a different season than it was spawned in catches up in `_ready`.
- **LOD:** each tree has `Look` (near) and `LookFar` (the trunk, the main limbs and three or four detail-1 blobs over the crown's clumps, no leaf cards; willows get one stick per strand). `LookFar` draws from `Constants.ART_TREE_LOD_DISTANCE` (55 m, hysteresis `ART_TREE_LOD_MARGIN` 6 m) and `Look` has `visibility_parent = ../LookFar` (Godot's hierarchical LOD) and no range of its own, so exactly one of them draws at any distance. Two independent ranges were rejected: Godot measures each from its own AABB center, so a near and far mesh of slightly different bounds would leave a ring where neither draws. No fade: a fading instance goes through the transparent pipeline.
- **Budgets:** near meshes stay inside `ART_TREE_MAX_TRIANGLES` (7,000) and far inside `ART_TREE_FAR_MAX_TRIANGLES` (900), recorded as mesh meta `triangles`. The old broadleaf measured up to 10,680, so two tip clumps closer than 0.65 of a clump share one (their random draws still happen, so every other clump keeps its color and size) and the leaf cards per clump (at most 40, the last thing drawn from the RNG) are cut to fit the budget. Measured now: broadleaf 6,788, birch 6,582, willow 6,072, cypress 3,347, pine 287 near; at most 624 far. `tools/mesh_check.gd` checks every kind x crown x variant x season, near and far (budgets, far cheaper than near, finite positions, normals and colors), the season rules, and a spawned tree's LOD wiring and season swap.
- **Stylized shader fix:** `to_linear()` clamps the vertex color at zero first. MSAA extrapolates vertex colors a little past a triangle's edge, a dark channel went negative, `pow()` of a negative is NaN, and glow smeared the NaN pixel into a white flare (seen on an autumn red maple from across a lot). Same clamp in the cloud shader. `tools/studio/icon_studio.gd` has the same function; not touched here (icons chunk in flight).
- **Networking:** none. Trees are cosmetic and local, a pure function of the replicated day; no RPC, replicated property, save key, or yard or task generation changed (the lot generator's tree calls and RNG draws are untouched).
- **Review fixes (same day):** `tools/mesh_check.gd` is now a CI step (`meshcheck` in `ci_local`, "Mesh check" in `netcode.yml`; headless, exit code only, no port, so the CI ports table is unchanged). It covers variants -3..3 (store and town dressing pass negative variants, and `variant % VARIANTS` keeps the sign, so -3..-1 are shapes of their own; `posmod` was rejected because it would re-roll existing trees), every crown for evergreens (the crown is part of a pine's seed), and asserts the exact season mapping instead of "any blossom" or "either green". A season turn used to build every distinct tree's meshes inside DayClock's day RPC (about 12 ms per broadleaf, near and far; a few hundred ms at the store or in town, host included), so the day before a turn `Seasons.apply_look` calls `prewarm_season(next)` on `seasonal` nodes that have it and trees build tomorrow's meshes into the shared cache within `Constants.ART_TREE_PREWARM_MS_PER_FRAME` (4 ms) a frame; the turn then only swaps. Rendering the studio's `trees_seasons` through `spawn` + `apply_season` (as the game does) showed `ERROR: Condition "dep_instance->array_index == -1"` from `instance_set_base`: changing LookFar's mesh walks its visibility dependents, and the renderer rejects one whose own mesh changed (the old Look-first order, so every real turn) or that was spawned the same frame. `apply_season` now clears Look's `visibility_parent` across the swap and restores it.

### 2026-11-01: The cast's lines are wired in without new traffic; text lines get their own rngs; one day-tally entry point

- **Homeowners:** the host picks the pool id ("<mood>", "bill_<reason>", or "<personality>/<mood>") with the static `homeowner.gd choose_pool(mood, bills, water_fee, personality, rng)` (the chances are `Constants.HOMEOWNER_BILL_LINE_CHANCE`, `HOMEOWNER_WATER_LINE_CHANCE` and `HOMEOWNER_PERSONALITY_LINE_CHANCE`; the rng draws are in the same order as before) and the line index, and the existing `_rpc_react(mood, line_index, amount, pool)` carries both; every peer resolves the pool with `Strings.homeowner_pool()`. The RPC's signature is unchanged but its pool argument can now name a personality pool, so host and client must run the same build (the integrator's BUILD_VERSION bump covers it). The paid line is `pick_seeded(paid, amount)`, the same everywhere with no traffic.
- **Phone texts** (town, hires, TurfMasters™) stay host-composed final strings in the existing `_rpc_text` and `_rpc_state`. Each line is picked by a fresh rng seeded `hash([town_seed, day, <what it's about>, "line"])` (`town.gd _line_rng`), or a host-local randomized rng in `hires.gd`, never the town's gameplay rngs. Removing the old inline line draws from those rngs changes nothing they decide: in every caller the line draw was the last use of the rng.
- **New save key:** `town.rival_announced` (bool, read with `false` as the default; host-only, not in `_rpc_state`). An old save past Mulch Mule III gets TurfMasters™'s hello once on its next morning.
- **Day tallies:** `Campaign.note_day(key, amount)` is the one public, host-only entry point to the day summary's counters (critic C18). The run director adds `raids` (each arrival) and `caught` (each cohort's size); they ride in the existing `_rpc_day` summary. Every peer computes the quip and rank blurbs from the summary itself.
- **Marty** stays local and cosmetic. His trigger logic (`store_clerk.gd greet_trigger()` and `open_trigger()`) reads the replicated day clock, the Shops node's hours and the local player's zone changes; his "buy" line hangs off the shop window's confirmed `bought` signal (which already waits for `Shops.result` "ok"), so it fires once per confirmed purchase. The after-raid flag clears only when the after-raid line is actually said, so a "morning" greet (which wins before `Constants.CLERK_MORNING_UNTIL_HOUR`) pushes it to the next greet instead of losing it. His caption honors `Settings` interface captions and caption size.
- **Tuning vs. presentation:** anything that decides what is said or for how long is a `Constants` tuning number (`CLERK_CAPTION_SECONDS`, `CLERK_MORNING_UNTIL_HOUR`, `HOMEOWNER_PAID_CAPTION_SECONDS`, the line chances). A caption's look (pixel size, font size, outline, offset) stays a const or literal in its script next to the Label3D it styles, like the homeowner's caption always has.
- **Test:** `--cast-test` gains host steps W1-W7 (Marty's triggers and caption, including a morning greet after a raid, the town's texts against their pools, seeded run cues, the summary card's quip and blurb, the hire names, the raid tally, and the homeowner's pool choice rolled through `choose_pool` with a seeded rng) and uses only `Guardrail.check()` (`net/dev/guardrail.gd`, critic C31): its own pattern list is gone. `--rival-test` checks the sore-loser text and the one-time hello and its save key.

### 2026-11-01: Tool tier and fuel are replicated prop properties; every tier rule is one pure helper; bags are spent where they're bagged

- **Replicated:** `props/yard_prop.tscn` gains `tier` (property /15) and `fuel` (/16), both spawn plus on-change (critic C16; hotbar holds /13 and /14). The host sets a job tool's tier from `Campaign.crew_tool_tier` when it spawns it, and its replicated `label` names the tier ("Leaf blower Mk II"). Fuel only changes while a gas tool runs, like `water`. The look rebuilds when the tier changes (`prop_looks.dress` keys on kind and tier).
- **One helper:** `core/tool_tiers.gd` (preloaded, no class_name) holds every tier and fuel rule as pure static functions over `Constants` (`TOOL_TIER_*`, `MK3_PERKS`, `GAS_TOOLS`, `FUEL_BURN_PER_SECOND`, the new `TOOL_TIER_PITCH`): speed, reach, noise, fuel use, start chance, hose length, washer and roller use, trimmer spots, climb speed, blower cells, names, and gas-can gallons. Heat (`Heat.noise_for(kind, tier)`, static), job_tasks (speed, reach, the Mk III perks, no work on an empty tank), leaf_field (the blower), net_prop (burn, pull cord, hose length), player (ladder climb speed, prompts) and tool_sound (volume and pitch) all read it, so a tier means the same everywhere and a balance pass changes only constants.
- **Gas:** a running gas tool burns its tank on the host (`net_prop._step_fuel`); at 0 it stops and `_rpc_mower_fx("sputter")` plays; the holder's own peer shows a notice. `request_refuel()` / `_rpc_request_refuel()` (any_peer, host checks the held gas can and finds the nearest gas tool that isn't full within `PICKUP_REACH`) pours what the tank needs from the can (the can's `water` is its fill as a share of `GAS_CAN_CARRY`). Chefty takes `GAS_PER_TANK` from the crew's gas per gas tool and the spare (up to `GAS_CAN_CARRY`) into the can when the yard's tools spawn; `world._return_job_gas()` puts what's left in the tanks and the can back before the job tools are freed (`_free_lot` and the yard swap), so the crew pays only for gas burned. Until then that gas is still the crew's in the save: `Shops.supplies_to_save()` (what `_save_section` writes) adds `ToolTiers.gas_out()` of the live `job_tools` to the supply's gas, so a save made mid-job or on the ride home (`Campaign.finish_job` saves before Chefty gets back) keeps it if the host quits before `_return_job_gas` runs. Job tools are never saved, so it can't be counted twice; `_return_job_gas` needs no save of its own because the saved number doesn't change. The gas can only rides along with at least `GAS_CAN_MIN_GALLONS` spare.
- **Bags:** the host takes one bag per pile in `leaf_field._handle_collect` and `host_hand_collect` (hands use the crew's bags too, so hires never charge twice): `big_bags` first, which scoop `BIG_BAG_RADIUS_SCALE` wider, then `lawn_bags`, via `Shops.consume`. `_rpc_bag_refused(reason)` and the `bag_refused(peer, reason)` signal now say why, "thin" or "no_bags"; a client that already sees no bags doesn't predict the bag. `TEST_STARTER_SUPPLIES` has no contractor bags, so older tests keep measuring plain lawn-bag piles.
- **Notices:** `player.flash_hint(text)` shows a plain notice over your head for `SUPPLY_NOTICE_SECONDS`, ahead of every prompt; the out-of-gas, empty-can and out-of-bags hints also show as plain hints while you hold the tool and E has nothing else to do.
- **Protocol:** RPCs changed (`_rpc_bag_refused` gains its reason; new `_rpc_request_refuel`) and two replicated properties were added, so the integrator's BUILD_VERSION bump covers this chunk. The save format is unchanged (gas and bags were already in the `shops` section).
- **Test:** `--shop-test` gains `bags` (a bag uses one, none left refuses with "no_bags" and leaves the leaves, the hint, contractor bags first and wider), `gas` (tanks filled and the spare in the can, run dry, won't start empty, the refuel prompt, the can fills it), and `tier_math` (the tier tables through `noise_for` and `tool_tiers.gd`, and a Mk II look). The probe's `shops` block gains each yard tool's tier and fuel, so CI's host-equals-client check proves both replicate.
- **Considered:** fuel and tier inside the hotbar's per-player state (rejected: the crew shares one set of tools, so it belongs on the prop); charging a full tank per ride (rejected: it punishes a crew for owning more tools than a job uses); a separate notice HUD (the interaction prompt already floats plain notices).

### 2026-11-02: The shop you can see is local and derived; one generic keeper; one site builder (shops chunk 4)

- **Nothing new on the wire.** The catalog window (`ui/shop_window.gd`), the shopkeepers (`npcs/store_clerk.gd`), the shelves (`props/shelf_stock.gd`), the hours signs and site dressing (`levels/dressing/shop_sites.gd`) are local cosmetic nodes on every peer, built from state each peer already has: Shops' replicated snapshot (stock, supplies, looks, truck), Campaign's unlocks and rank, and the day clock. Buying still goes only through `Shops.request_buy`, and the window's toasts, the keeper's "buy" line, the register (`audio/cue_hooks.gd`) and the cash drawer all hang off `Shops.result`, which only the host sends. No RPC, replicated property, save key or yard/task generation changed.
- **The window:** `open_shop(shop, tab, focus)` (ui_root's `open_shop` and the old `open_store()`) builds a header, a tab bar from `Catalog.SHOPS[shop].tabs`, and cards from `Catalog.items_for` (the Tools tab is one card per tool showing its next tier, `next_tier(owned)`). A card's reason it can't be bought (`_blocker`) is the first refusal in the host's `_check_buy` order (closed, season, sold out, rank, needs; cash last, as the charge), so what it says is what the host would answer; owned things skip it for their own buttons. The header shows the keeper's line only while its caption is up (`current_line()`), and the counter says its line before opening the window, so it opens on that line and never on an old one. Pictures are `Icons.for_shop_item(id)` when `Icons.has()` it, else a color swatch (the catalog's icons are icons:catalog, W10). The window keeps `_cards` and `_items()` for icons_test. `ui/job_board_window.gd` extends this script for the catalog look, so `open()` (no arguments) stays the shared entry point and touches the shop's own nodes only when they were built; `_box` and `_button` keep their old padding, and the shop uses `_tight` / `_small_button`.
- **One keeper script:** `store_clerk.gd` takes a `shop_id`; `Catalog.SHOPS[shop].keeper` picks the lines (`Strings.KEEPER_LINES[keeper]`), and its `KEEPERS` table the look and whether they stay after hours (only Marty). Groups: `shopkeepers` for all, `store_clerk` still only Marty (cast_test and `character_life` find him by it). Reach is `Constants.SHOP_REACH` from the keeper or the site's counter, the same point the host checks; the counter's prompt is plain while the shop is closed.
- **One site builder:** `shop_sites.dress(world)` walks `Constants.SHOP_SITES` and calls `build_site(zone, shop_id, site)` for each, so the drivable town (critic C28) moves a shop by changing data or by calling `build_site` under another zone. `world._dress` calls it in place of placing the clerk (Marty keeps the node name `StoreZone/StoreClerk`).
- **Shelves:** `shelf_stock.gd` fills its `SHELF_SLOTS` (the garden center's three units) in catalog order from the shop's shelf tabs, redraws only slots whose state (`slot_state(item)`) changed, scales each model to fit its slot, and shows a tarp for a rank-locked item. A unit's three levels share one spot in front of it, so the slot you look at is the nearest stack's level that your head's pitch points at most nearly (`pitch_to(slot)`; stacks within `SHELF_STACK_TOLERANCE`): look up for the top shelf, down for the bottom. Considered: one spot per level along the shelf (rejected: the levels are stacked, you'd stand beside what you look at) and E cycling the stack (rejected: the prompt couldn't say which one E opens until you pressed it). `units_by_item()` is the same on every peer, so the probe's `shops.shelf_units` lets CI prove a client's shelves match the host's. Models for what shops sell (bag boxes, sacks, gas can, crate, pallet, tarp) are `props/shop_models.gd`; `yard_models.gd` hands its `gas_can` kind to it (the tier trim stays in yard_models).
- **Ladder reach per tier:** gutter work reaches `ToolTiers.ladder_reach(tier)` = `LADDER_TOP_REACH` x `TOOL_TIER_REACH[tier]`, where the tier is the set-up ladder whose top is nearest the worker (`job_tasks.ladder_tier_near`), since a ladder you're standing on isn't held.
- **Sounds:** `register` (critic C11/C12's id for the ka-ching) and `cash_drawer` are synthesized by `tools/make_sfx.py` and registered in `audio/sound_bank.gd`; `register` is the buyer's alone on the UI bus, `cash_drawer` is positional at the keeper, `SHOP_DRAWER_DELAY_SECONDS` after it.
- **Test:** `--shop-test` gains `shelves` (sold-out bags, a box per gas can, the blower's locked Mk III with its rank tag, the trimmer's Mk II price, the "Look at" prompt on a top, middle and bottom slot with the head pitched at each), `window` (opened on the gas can: Supplies tab, the card picked out, its x1 button buys at the counter during open hours, cash, supply, stock, shelf and toast all move; the Tools tab's seven cards and the locked Mk III button), `keepers` (at 10 PM and noon: each keeper there exactly while open, Marty always, the counter prompt and its closed form, which keeps its key since Marty still answers E, the window's shutter, the hours sign), and in `tier_math` the ladder reach and `ladder_tier_near` with a one-frame stand-in ladder.
- **Considered:** host-placed keepers (rejected: nothing about a keeper is shared, like the clerk always was); hiding Marty after closing too (rejected: the crew sleeps at his store and the cast wrote him as never leaving, so his night and closed lines would never play); a shelf per catalog item (rejected: 57 items; workwear and truck paint get the hat rack and swatch counter in shops chunk 7).

### 2026-11-03: Rusty's junk is props on the ground and models in the bed; selling is one host path (shops chunk 5)

- **Junk is a yard prop** (`props/yard_prop.tscn`, kind `junk`, its type in `label`, its base appraisal in `value`), host-spawned by `world.spawn_junk(type, at, value := -1, on_yard := false)` into `%RuntimeProps` (group `junk`, plus `job_tools` on a yard so it goes with the yard). `world._spawn_junk(lot, offer)` places a yard's pieces from the offer's seed on the lot's `free_spots(count, seed)` when the lot has it (the lot agent's contract), else on the front-lawn band the TurfMasters signs use, `JUNK_SPACING` apart. Yards only get junk once `feature:salvage` is open, and timed tests other than `--shop-test` get none (the same rule as Shops' unlimited supplies), so no older test sees a new prop. Each type's model, collision box (`ShopModels.JUNK_BOX`, set on the prop's own Collision by `prop_looks.dress`) and hold (`ShopModels.JUNK_HOLDS`, read through `prop_looks.hold_of(prop)`, which every HOLDS reader now uses) live in `props/shop_models.gd`; weight is `Constants.JUNK_TYPES[type].kg` in `net_prop.weight_kg`. The pickup prompt and the hotbar name it through `net_prop.display_name()` ("Toilet"). A set-down eases the look from the arms onto the body over `JUNK_SET_DOWN_SECONDS` (junk only), and a landing plays `_rpc_junk_land` (a thud by weight).
- **Chefty's bed:** both Chefties (`ride.gd`, `chefty_drive.gd`) make a `Body/JunkBed` (`net/components/junk_bed.gd`) and forward `accept_junk`, `junk_count`, `unload_junk` to it (critic C22's contract). A loose junk prop that has settled inside the bed (`net_prop._try_load_junk`, under `JUNK_BED_REST_SPEED`) is taken in: freed, and drawn at exactly its pose in the bed's space, so nothing moves on screen. The whole list goes out with `JunkBed._rpc_junk(list, hopped_at)` on every change (eight entries at most) and to a late joiner from its Chefty state request (`send_to`), so neither Chefty's existing RPC signature changed. Unloading (legacy: `world.on_ride_arrived(STORE)`; town: Chefty parking inside `STORE_AREA`) spawns each piece as a prop at its drawn pose with a hop velocity over the tailgate (`JunkBed.hop_velocity`) and a short collision exception with the truck's Solid, one every `JUNK_UNLOAD_GAP_SECONDS`, back row first. E by the bed with junk in your arms releases it with `JunkBed.heave_velocity` onto the bed floor. Considered: a bed slot layout like the bags' (rejected: junk is all shapes, and a fixed slot meant a visible jump from where it landed).
