- **Selling** is `Shops.request_sell(prop_path, haggle)` → `_rpc_request_sell` → `host_sell`: Rusty's unlocked, open, the seller at his counter or in his yard (`_near_site`), the prop loose junk on the scale (`on_scale`: its base over the 1.24 m platform, within `JUNK_SCALE_HALF` of `SHOP_SITES.rustys.scale` along both sides in StoreZone space, and resting on it, at least `JUNK_SCALE_TOP` less `JUNK_SCALE_TOP_SLACK` up). A haggle rolls on the host RNG, stores the factor in `haggled[prop name]` (one per piece, replicated in the snapshot's extra; it is never cleared by the morning, only forgotten once the piece is gone, and it rides with the piece through Chefty's bed and the save: `host_take_haggle`/`host_keep_haggle`, the bed entry's and `junk_left` entry's `haggle`) and answers `haggle_won`/`haggle_lost` with the new offer; a sale pays `appraise(prop)` (value × `rusty_mood` × haggle factor) through `Campaign.pay`, frees the prop and answers `sold` (item `junk_<type>`, or `junk_flamingo_vintage` for the jackpot). Refusals: `locked`, `closed`, `far`, `off_scale`, `haggled`, `unknown`. `audio/cue_hooks.gd` rings the register on `sold` too. `rusty_mood` and `used_today` (tool ids, one per pallet; a used tool bought off the rack leaves `""` in its place, so the other pallets keep their tools, and a buy needs it there) roll in `_restock` from `hash([town_seed, day, "rusty"])`. Rusty's welcome text goes out when `Campaign.rank_changed` finds `feature:salvage` newly open. The save's `junk_left` is now computed when saving: everything in Chefty's bed (`"bed": true`) and up to `JUNK_LEFT_MAX` unsold junk props inside `STORE_AREA`, each with its haggle; on load the bed's go back in the bed (`JunkBed.restore`, laid in `JUNK_BED_SPOTS`) and the rest lie in a grid (`Shops.junk_drop_spot`) inside a new `STORE_LAYOUT` reservation, `junk_drop`, so they never land on the mulch bin or Lunch Money's stall (the first cut's unbounded row from (5.6, 11.2) started inside the mulch bin).
- **What's seen, all local:** the yard (`shop_sites._rustys_yard`: fence runs from `yard_extras`' chainlink, the gate `RustysGate`, the shack, `MoodBoard`, `ScaleDial`, the pallets as a `shelf_stock` with `used_rack = true`, group `rusty_rack`, slots `used_slot_<n>` resolving to `used_today[n]`), the keeper's beard and overalls (`store_clerk._dress_extras`, blob detail 1 per critic C20), and the window's Sell tab (polls `junk_on_scale()` since junk lands by physics, not by a Shops change; a haggle holds the old price and disables the buttons for `RUSTY_HAGGLE_THINK_SECONDS` while Rusty hums `haggle_hmm`, then his verdict line and the new price land on the same frame). The scale moved to (14.4, 0.5): next to the counter, E there grabbed the junk off the scale instead of opening the window.
- **Review round (2026-11-03), from the takes:**
  - **Set-down and throw:** junk passes through whoever let go of it (`add_collision_exception_with`) until its box is clear of their capsule (`net_prop._clear_of`: the box's half diagonal plus `JUNK_CLEAR_MARGIN`), at least `JUNK_CLEAR_MIN_SECONDS` and at most `JUNK_CLEAR_MAX_SECONDS`. The wide junk box turning solid against the holder's legs shoved them 0.17 m back on the release frame (`rec_setdown_1p` 13).
  - **E-heave:** `JunkBed.heave_velocity` now picks the arc by height, its top `JUNK_HEAVE_CLEARANCE` over `BED_CATCH_RAIL_Y`, and the piece passes through the truck's walls until that top (`let_through`, `heave_rise_seconds`). A heave starts a fresh flight (`_thrown_at`, `_bonked` with the heaver in it): the stale `_thrown_at` made the heaved piece bonk its own heaver on the spot (`rec_heave2_1p` 11), and the flat 0.55 s arc hit the tailgate from right behind it and fell back.
  - **Unload junk at the tailgate** (critic C22): `JunkBed.can_unload_from(player)` (junk in the bed, Chefty standing still per its `junk_unload_allowed()`, the player within `JUNK_TAILGATE_REACH` of `tailgate_point()`), `request_unload` → `_rpc_request_unload` (any_peer, host-checked) → `unload`. `player.gd` puts it after picking things up and before getting in, and its hint is `SHOP_UI.prompt_unload_junk` (an action you can't see, so it gets words). Pieces land `JUNK_UNLOAD_SIDE` over to the side away from the nearest player behind the tailgate (`_landing_side`); the one who pressed E stood where they landed.
  - **Rusty's gate** (`RustysGate.someone_inside`): open while any visible player is in the yard or within `RUSTY_GATE_HOLD_MARGIN` of it, so nobody is fenced in overnight (the fence is 1.8 m, a jump about 1 m). Each peer decides from the replicated players, like the clock half. Considered: a one-way gate (rejected: needs a second collider and still traps someone at the far fence), teleporting stragglers out (rejected: a cut).
  - **Tests:** `--shop-test` adds `yard_junk` (this yard's count from its seed, and a fixed-seed Dandelion Acres yard: count, lawn band, spacing), `scale_edge`, `haggle_sticks` (through the bed and `_forget_gone_haggles`), `heave` (E by the bed, the heaver not stunned), `welcome` (one text), `junk_left` (bed and lot entries saved, laid back out); `used_buy` checks the next pallet keeps its tool, `client_rusty` checks the client's copy of the bed, and `junk_bed` must land within `JUNK_LAND_TOLERANCE` (1.2 m) of the predicted spot (was anywhere within 3 m). Not covered: the town-mode unload on parking (the shop test runs the legacy world) and a late joiner's bed (`send_to`).
- **Protocol:** new RPCs (`Shops._rpc_request_sell`, `JunkBed._rpc_junk`, `net_prop._rpc_junk_land`); no replicated property, save key or yard/task generation changed except that yards now spawn junk props (gated as above). The review round adds `JunkBed._rpc_request_unload`, `junk_left` entries gain optional `bed` and `haggle` fields (same key), and `STORE_LAYOUT` gains `junk_drop`. The integrator bumps BUILD_VERSION.
- **Test:** `--shop-test` gains `rusty_day`, `junk_bed` (a toilet dropped in the bed is loaded where it lands and hops out at the tailgate), `sell` (the needle reads 28 kg, the offer is value × mood, the sale pays it), `haggle` (the factor sticks, a second haggle is refused, a tire off the scale isn't bought) and `used_buy` (the host's used tool is "owned"; the client sells a boombox and buys the used tool through the RPCs, `client_rusty`). The probe's `shops.rusty` (mood, rack, what the pallets show, junk in the bed) is compared host vs client. `tools/play.ps1` passes `--rank=` to the observer and friend too, so a client sees the shops the host's dev rank opened; scenario `rustys` (needs `-Extra '--rank=5'`) puts junk in Chefty's bed on the TruckPad and unloads it 1.5 s later.

### 2026-11-02: Chefty is a host-simulated arcade vehicle with driver prediction (driving chunk C1)

- **The vehicle:** in the connected town `World/Rides/Chefty` is `net/components/chefty_drive.gd`, made in code on every peer by `levels/world.gd _add_chefty()` (the legacy world makes `ride.gd` the same way; the static node left `world.tscn`). Nodes: `Chassis` (a CharacterBody3D box on physics layer 5, `Constants.VEHICLE_LAYER = 16`, masking the world and other chassis, held 0.45 m off the ground in `MOTION_MODE_FLOATING`), `Body` (the rendered pose: `TruckLook`, `Seats` x8, `Label`; probe and riders rely on the name) and `View` (`vehicle_view.gd`). Players (layer 2) never touch the chassis; on their own peer they bump `TruckLook/Solid` as before.
- **The model** (`net/components/vehicle_sim.gd`, `class_name VehicleSim`, static): `step(chassis, state, input, params, dt)` turns first (speed-ramped, weaker at top speed, more with the handbrake), then splits the velocity along and across the new heading: throttle, brakes, reverse, coast and air drag along it; grip (or drift grip under the handbrake) bleeds the sideways part. `move_and_slide` meets only walls; rigid bodies and characters are soft (90% speed kept, a 0.3 s collision exception). Four rays under the hubs (excluding its own Solid) set the ride height and the visual tilt, with gravity when none hits; the body leans with the throttle and the turn. All numbers are `CHEFTY_*` in `core/constants.gd`; `chefty_params(dents, tuneup)` applies dents and `perk:chefty_tuneup` (+8%, so the perk finally does something). The NICE BOYS' van will use the same model (chunk F).
- **Netcode:** see docs/NETCODE.md, "Drivable Chefty". The driver's peer sends one input per physics tick (`_rpc_input`, channel 1, unreliable_ordered); the host simulates and sends 30 Hz snapshots with an input ack (`_rpc_snap`, channel 1); the driver predicts and reconciles by replay; everyone else draws 100 ms behind on physics ticks. Seats, horn, bags and the late-join state are reliable host RPCs. Getting in is host-validated: within `CHEFTY_ENTER_RADIUS` of the driver's door, the shotgun door or the tailgate, a free seat, not captured, carried, hidden or asleep, the driver's seat only under `CHEFTY_ENTER_MAX_SPEED`; hands are stowed (`hotbar.host_stow_hands`, critic C23). Getting out teleports you to a clear spot by your door (host-checked with a shape query) through `player.send_teleport`, and above `CHEFTY_EXIT_MAX_SPEED` it's a bail: flung out with `CHEFTY_BAIL_SHARE` of Chefty's velocity through `_rpc_thrown`.
- **Seated players:** `player/seated.gd` holds the logic, so `player.gd` only gains a few hooks: `_physics_process` runs `Seated.step` instead of walking when in a vehicle seat (look, and the driver's controls into `chefty.local_drive_input`), `_unhandled_input` lets the seat take E (out), F (`take`, swap seats) and the camera key, and the interaction prompt offers Drive Chefty / Ride shotgun / Hop in the bed (after any pickup, so bags by the tailgate still come first). A vehicle's riders face its way on every peer; the owner turns only the head. The driver gets `player/vehicle_camera.gd` (a chase camera, FOV widening with speed), riders their own eyes; the camera key swaps. Hands go on the wheel in first and third person (`vehicles.wheel_grips`). `CartoonBody.sit(pose)`: 1 sits in the cab, 2 stands still in the bed (no walk cycle at 19 m/s).
- **The truck:** `vehicles.build_truck(parent, opts)` is the one signature (critic C21): `{color, decal, dents, interior}`, all in the cache key, a plain Color still accepted. `interior: true` is the drivable Chefty: an open cab (no glass, so you see who's driving), a roof on four pillars, door panels, a dash with gauges, a bench and a `SteeringWheel` node (`set_steering`). Seats (`CHEFTY_SEATS`): driver and shotgun sitting in the cab, six in the bed (two across, three deep; the first row moved back to z -0.05 to clear the water tank).
- **Horn and input:** the new action `honk` (H; also left click for the driver, critic C14) is in `KEYBIND_GROUPS` "crew" and `HOLD_ACTIONS`. Sounds: `truck_horn`, `horn_loop`, `door_slam`, `engine_idle`, `skid` (critic C12's ids). `skid` is audio's one-shot (critic C12 folds driving's skid_loop into it), made by `tools/make_sfx.py`: Chefty fires it when a slide starts and again every `CHEFTY_SKID_REPEAT` while it lasts, and audio:7 keeps that one recipe. Chefty implements `engine_state()` for audio's `audio/vehicle_sound.gd` and attaches it under `Body` when it exists, so it moves with the drawn truck; `Body` is `net/components/vehicle_body.gd`, which hands `engine_state()` through to Chefty, so `parent.engine_state()` (critic C13) works, and the `state_source` Callable is set before it enters the tree too. Until then Chefty plays its own engine loop pitched by speed.
- **depart() until the autopilot:** `chefty.depart(peers, zone)` seats them and hops: after `CHEFTY_HOP_SECONDS` Chefty parks at the stop (the yard's curb, or the TruckPad) and the riders arrive like the legacy ride. Driving chunk C2 replaces the hop with the autopilot and warp mode.
- **Considered:** VehicleBody3D (Jolt wheel instability, and it can't be replayed for prediction); a driver-authoritative pose (the truck carries other players and the bags: shared state); a MultiplayerSynchronizer (Chefty isn't spawned, and the snapshot needs its timestamp and ack). Prediction error is measured as the distance between where the driver first predicted Chefty after input N (made once, when N was applied; later replays never rewrite it) and the host's pose after the same input, which ignores the ~one-way latency the driver is always ahead by. Measuring against the replayed predictions instead only saw the last two ticks' drift. The honest metric found that the replay ran in the RPC callback, where `move_and_slide` uses the render delta, so every prediction fell about 0.09 s of travel short (mean 0.68 m at speed); reconciling on the next physics tick took the mean to 0.02 m.
- **Test:** `--drive-test` (`net/dev/drive_test.gd`, port 7815, `--town-map`, `--sim-lag=120` on both and `--sim-loss=1` on the host): seats through the real request paths (the client's from `_rpc_dev_script`, test-only, accepted only with `--run-seconds`), 4 s full throttle (25 m, 12 m/s), steering, a stop, the horn, getting out; then the host drives with the client in the bed, both bail at speed (each must land at least 2 m from its exit spot), and the client is caught sitting in the bed (out of its seat). CI also checks the client's `drive.prediction_error_max_m` < 1.5 and `prediction_error_mean_m` < 0.25 (measured 0.18 and 0.004), both peers' riders, the client's snapshot gaps (`vehicle_view.max_gap_ms` < 400), and `ride_wobble_m` < 0.05 with `ride_frames` > 0 on both peers: the host's bed rider, and the client riding a Chefty it draws through `vehicle_view` (measured 0.009). The probe skips the driver's chase camera, which trails on purpose. The client must end in `TOWN`.
- **Review fixes (same chunk):** tuning numbers that were inline (steering speed, the brake-or-reverse switch, landing thresholds, lean rates, bail lift, input queue cap, exit spots and capsule, engine and horn volumes) are `CHEFTY_*` constants. `perk:chefty_tuneup` is re-applied on every peer on `Campaign.rank_changed` and `campaign_loaded`, so ranking up mid-session speeds Chefty up for host and driver alike. The drive HUD shows the seat's keys as they'd go right now: F reads "Slow down first" in the cab above `CHEFTY_SEAT_SWAP_MAX_SPEED`, and E reads "Bail out!" above `CHEFTY_EXIT_MAX_SPEED`.
- **Networking:** new RPCs on Chefty in the town only (`_rpc_input`, `_rpc_snap`, `_rpc_seats`, `_rpc_horn`, `_rpc_bags` with three args, `_rpc_request_enter/exit/swap`, `_rpc_hop`, `_rpc_hop_arrived`, `_rpc_placed`, `_rpc_request_state`, `_rpc_state`, `_rpc_dev_script`), and the new input action; no replicated property, save key or yard generation changed. The legacy Chefty's RPCs are untouched.

### 2026-11-02: The autopilot drives depart() trips and the chauffeur; tests warp (driving chunk C2)

- **The autopilot** (`net/components/vehicle_autopilot.gd`, `class_name VehicleAutopilot`, a RefCounted) only makes inputs for `VehicleSim.step`: the host feeds them to Chefty's chassis instead of a driver's, so the pose goes out in the normal `_rpc_snap` stream and nothing about the autopilot itself is networked. `plan(from, to)` is `TownMap.route` from the truck onto the nearest road, junction to junction, with the last road point pulled into the stop's lane `AUTOPILOT_LANE_LEAD` before it (so it pulls in straight instead of swerving across the centerline at the end). `next_input(state, pos, dt)` is pure pursuit (aim `AUTOPILOT_LOOKAHEAD` ahead along the route, steering proportional to the heading error), cruising at `AUTOPILOT_CRUISE`, slowing to `AUTOPILOT_CORNER_SPEED` ahead of corners sharper than `AUTOPILOT_CORNER_ANGLE` and to a stop at the end (both planned at `AUTOPILOT_BRAKE_DECEL`), braking with a negative throttle except near standstill (where that would reverse). A target behind the nose, or pushing at nothing for `AUTOPILOT_STUCK_SECONDS`, backs up with the wheel turned so the nose swings toward the target: U-turns and three-point turns come out of that one rule. Parked = stopped within `AUTOPILOT_STOP_RADIUS`, or within `AUTOPILOT_SETTLE_RADIUS` for `AUTOPILOT_SETTLE_SECONDS`. All numbers are `AUTOPILOT_*` in `core/constants.gd`. The NICE BOYS' van (driving chunk F) uses the same class.
- **Warp mode:** with `Net.is_automated()` (the smoke test) or the new `--warp-rides`, the host moves the chassis along the planned route at `AUTOPILOT_TEST_WARP` times cruise (kinematic, no collisions) and parks it exactly on the stop, so job, board, hire and smoke timings stay near the old ride's when the town becomes the default (chunk H). Only `--drive-test` (and later `--pursuit-test`) drive for real. A real trip still going after its route's length at `AUTOPILOT_GIVE_UP_SPEED` plus `AUTOPILOT_GIVE_UP_EXTRA` seconds warps the rest of the way with a warning, so nobody is ever stranded on a bad route.
- **depart() keeps the ride contract** (job_test semantics): the party is seated (shotgun and the bed first; the driver's seat is the autopilot's), their zone is `TRANSIT` while it drives, and at the stop everyone in a seat is let off into the destination zone with the usual host-ordered teleport, then `world.on_ride_arrived(zone)`. The stop is `LotZone * TownMap.CURB_POINT` (wherever the job site stands; chunk D places it at the house's yard) or the TruckPad. `is_riding()` is true only for a depart() trip; `is_autopiloting()` also covers the chauffeur. The hop C1 used in its place, with `_rpc_hop` / `_rpc_hop_arrived` and `CHEFTY_HOP_SECONDS`, is gone.
- **The chauffeur:** in a cab seat, with a hired hand aboard (a hand is aboard while none are spawned on a yard) and the driver's seat empty, G (the `drop` action, unused while seated) asks `_rpc_request_chauffeur()`: the host starts an autopilot trip of kind CHAUFFEUR to the loaded yard's curb (if Chefty isn't already parked by it) or home. Nobody can take the driver's seat or slide into it while the autopilot drives; riders may still get out or bail. G again pulls over (the autopilot lets go; with nobody driving Chefty brakes). F stays the seat swap, so shotgun can always slide over to an empty driver's seat with a hand aboard (a first version put the chauffeur on F, which took the slide-over away whenever a hand was hired). The hand at the wheel is the one the trip named (`_trip_label`, sent once in `_rpc_autopilot`): `trip_label()` and the hand drawn at the wheel both match that name against the live hires list, so a hand fired or paid off mid-trip stops being drawn or named on every peer at once, and the host ends a CHAUFFEUR trip whose hand is gone (Chefty pulls over). A chauffeur's riders stay seated at the end and their zones follow where Chefty stopped. `chauffeur_action(peer)` ("start", "stop", "") decides what G does, on every peer, from replicated state only (the seats, the hires list, the hands' spawned bodies, the trip), so `player/seated.gd` and the drive HUD agree with the host.
- **Arrival detection:** when Chefty parks (the `parked` signal) within `CHEFTY_ARRIVE_RADIUS` of the loaded yard's curb point, however it was driven there, the host calls the new `world.on_crew_arrived()`: the homeowner's clock starts and the hands hop off, once per yard (a meta on the lot). In the town a depart() trip's arrival at the yard goes through the same function; the legacy world's `on_ride_arrived(LOT)` is unchanged.
- **Hands ride along, cosmetically:** `cosmetic_riders()` places the hands aboard in the free bed seats (back row first) and the trip's hand at the wheel while the autopilot drives with the driver's seat empty. Every peer computes it from replicated state and draws crew-orange cartoon bodies with their names on the seat markers (rebuilt only when the placement changes; nothing headless); the bed ones wave every `CHEFTY_HAND_WAVE_SECONDS`. No new networked nodes.
- **Networking:** Chefty's RPCs changed: `_rpc_hop` and `_rpc_hop_arrived` removed; `_rpc_autopilot(kind, zone, label, quip)` (authority, call_local, reliable: who's drawn driving and the quip index, which the host picks) and `_rpc_request_chauffeur()` (any_peer) added; `_rpc_state` carries the trip (kind, zone, label) instead of the hop. No replicated property, save key or yard generation changed. `hires.gd` gains `dev_add_hand(name)` (host, tests only).
- **Test:** `--drive-test` steps j to l: a real depart() drive from the TruckPad to Maple Row's first yard (the site placed on the host only, until chunk D) arrives in about 45 s at up to 13 m/s, parks 0.5 m from the curb point, lets the host off into `LOT` and starts the job clock; a hand (Big Sal) rides in the bed, the client takes shotgun and its own scripted G (`_rpc_request_chauffeur`) starts the trip, Big Sal takes the wheel and drives about 40 m, the client's G stops it and Chefty pulls over, F then slides the client into the driver's seat, and the client gets out on the street (`TOWN`); then (k2) the client lets Big Sal drive again, the host fires him mid-trip, and Chefty pulls over with nobody named or drawn at the wheel (he's hired back for the last step); a warped depart() home takes about 9 s, parks on the TruckPad, lets the host off into `STORE`, frees the yard and puts Big Sal back in the bed. CI also checks that the client saw all three trips (`drive.trips_seen`), draws the same hands (`drive.cosmetic`), and that its own `chauffeur_action` offered "start" then "stop" (`drive.chauffeur_seen`), the same the host's verdict saw. The ceilings went to 160/155 s (the run takes about 95 s). A `--smoke-test --town-map` run passes with the warped trip.
- **Considered:** a navmesh (the town is roads; the road graph and A* were already there), a spline follower that sets the pose directly outside warp mode (it can't hit anything, so it would drive through a parked car the players see), and steering by curvature (`2 sin(a) / L`; the simple proportional gain behaves the same at these speeds and is easier to tune).

### 2026-11-02: Audio: a local music director, a deterministic schedule, and game cues

- **One pure rule function.** `audio/music_director.gd` (`UIRoot/Audio/MusicDirector`, group `music_director`, alive in the menu too) asks the static `pick_mood(state, current)` every `MUSIC_TICK_SECONDS`. State keys: `net_state`, `zone`, `hour`, `heat`, `phase`, `sleeping`, `summary_open`, all from things every peer already has (`Net` zones, the day clock's hour and sleepers, the replicated heat, the run director's phase, the summary card). Priority order: not in a session -> title; the border -> crossing; a raid's chase or warning on the yard; asleep or the summary card -> silence; transit keeps the current mood; quiet hours -> night; a hot yard (enter `MUSIC_HOT_HEAT`, leave below `MUSIC_HOT_HEAT_EXIT`) -> hot; the store; from `MUSIC_GOLDEN_HOUR` -> work_evening; else work_morning (the drivable town's streets included). A calm change waits `MUSIC_MOOD_HOLD_SECONDS` and crossfades (equal power) over `MUSIC_FADE_SECONDS`; an urgent mood switches on the next tick over `MUSIC_FADE_URGENT_SECONDS`. No RPC, no replicated property: peers in different places hear different music, on purpose.
- **A deterministic schedule.** `audio/music_schedule.gd` (RefCounted, seeded rng) turns moods into play/keep/rest/stop: calm moods play a track `MUSIC_LOOPS_BEFORE_REST` loops (the first pass includes the intro, later ones start at the loop), rest a random `MUSIC_REST_SECONDS` with ambience only, then play the mood's next track in turn; urgent moods never rest; a mood that lists the track already playing keeps it; the Settings "Breaks between songs" (`audio/music_rests`) turns rests off. Headless tests drive it with simulated time.
- **Data:** `audio/music_tracks.gd` `TRACKS` (file, bpm, bars, intro bars, loop, stinger, title key, a dB trim) and `MOODS` (tracks, rests, urgent, priority, stand_in), `STINGER_IDS` (all nine, `st_lost_em` included), `seconds()`, `loop_offset()` (intro plus `MUSIC_ENCODE_LEAD_SECONDS` for .ogg) and `play_seconds()`. Only tracks that exist are listed; until chunks 4a/4b add the rest, the old 8-bar loop (`old_tune`, `assets/audio/sfx/music_loop.wav` at its old -15 dB) stands in for title, store, evening and night, hot plays the work music (`stand_in`), road keeps whatever is on (`stand_in: hold`), and warning, chase and crossing cut the music. Chunk 4 removes `old_tune` from `MOODS`; chunk 8 deletes the file. Titles are `ui/strings.gd` `MUSIC_TITLES` (`track_<id>`, the whole planned soundtrack), shown by the Settings window's "Now playing" row (`now_playing_title()`).
- **Stingers and overrides:** `stinger(id)` logs every fire (`static stinger_log`, also `stingers()`), even when the file doesn't exist yet or the music is off, and queues the ones that do on a third player; the decks duck `MUSIC_STINGER_DUCK_DB` over `MUSIC_DUCK_FADE_SECONDS` under it. `push_mood(source, mood, priority)` / `pop_mood(source)`: the highest push wins when its priority is at least the picked mood's `MOODS` priority (work 10, night 20, hot 30, road 50, silence 55, warning 60, chase 70, crossing 80), never over the menu.
- **M and muffle:** M (and the Settings checkbox) is `Settings` `audio/music`; off fades the director's own players out instead of muting the Music bus, because `core/settings.gd` owns the bus mute for the Music volume slider and the two would fight. The phone or the pause menu (`ui_root.pause_open()`: the pause buttons, or the Settings window opened from them) turn on the Music bus low-pass. `--audio-test`'s moods check fails any trackless mood that its `MOODS_WAITING` list doesn't name; chunks 4a and 4b shrink that list as the tracks land. The music left `audio/ambience.gd`, which keeps only the wind, birds, dog and cars.
- **Cues:** `audio/cue_hooks.gd` (`UIRoot/Audio/CueHooks`) hooks nodes by name as they appear, like the day summary does: `DayClock.day_ended` -> Clock Out, Promoted! (rank_ups), New Season; the summary card's new `closed` signal -> Rise and Rake plus one buzz for the night's texts; `Town.texted` (texts wait `CUE_TEXT_SETTLE_SECONDS`, so the ones that arrive with the card are held until it closes) -> New Client for referral/lead/won_back, `text_bad` for poached, `phone_buzz` (one per `PHONE_BUZZ_GAP_SECONDS`) or `text_pop` with the phone out; `Shops.result("ok")` -> `register` (critic C11; a refusal and Pay a crewmate stay quiet); `Campaign.payout_ready` -> Paid; a task crossing `TASK_DONE_PERCENT` on the yard -> `task_done` (ui/task_list.gd uses the same constant now); quiet hours and closing edges -> `quiet_chime`, `closing_yawn`; the local player taken to transit while captured -> Busted. The sound ids aren't synthesized yet (audio chunk 5), so `_sound(id)` logs every cue in `static cue_log` and plays only ids `audio/sound_bank.gd` has; chunk 5 registers them (and can add `_sound` to the audio test's source scan). Border Return's stinger and sounds stay in chunk 4b.
- **Dry mode:** headless and automated runs make no players or streams, but every decision and stinger is taken and logged (`mood_log`, `stinger_log`), so `--audio-test` checks the real logic on host and client.
- **Test:** `--audio-test` (port 7811) now runs with a Target client and adds `moods`, `picker_table` (24 cases plus pushes), `schedule_sim` (900 simulated seconds), `store_morning`, `now_playing` (the Settings row, and in a windowed run that a deck is really playing), `register`, `ride_to_lot`, `hot`, `raid` and `day_end` (and the stinger player playing, windowed); its source scan accepts stinger ids. CI also requires the client's probe `audio.stingers` to include `st_day_end` and `st_rank_up`.
- **Considered:** an `AudioStreamInteractive` for the transitions (the rules would live in a resource the tests can't drive with simulated time, and rests and stand-ins don't fit its model); host-decided music (a peer in the store would hear the yard's raid); muting the Music bus for M (fights the volume slider).

### 2026-11-02: Audio: the tense soundtrack, an escape mood with aliases, song books (audio chunk 4b)

- **Escape, not crossing.** Border Return's lane crossing is being redesigned (wave R2: a bureaucratic escape from the NICE BOYS' Field Office), so its music is the general escape/end-sequence cue: track `escape_run` ("Clean Getaway", title key `track_escape_run`), mood `escape` (urgent, priority 80, no rests). `pick_mood` returns `escape` for `Zone.BORDER`. The old id still works: `MOODS["crossing"] = {"alias": "escape"}`, and `MusicTracks.canonical()` / `is_alias()` resolve it, so every MusicTracks query (`tracks_for`, `priority`, `is_urgent`, `rests`, `stand_in`, `has_mood`) answers for the mood it names, and `push_mood(&"crossing", ...)` pushes `escape`. The director stores and logs the canonical id. `track_lane_change` stays in `MUSIC_TITLES` with the new title. The Field Office escape can `push_mood(&"<source>", &"escape", 80)` or add a `pick_mood` rule for its zone; the sound ids `crossing_go` (the start) and `crossing_bonk` (thrown back) and the stinger `st_made_it` (you're out) are generic enough to keep.
- **Tracks:** `hot_yard` (hot), `nice_boys_incoming` (warning), `nice_boys_chase` (chase), `escape_run` (escape), and the stingers `st_rank_up`, `st_season`, `st_morning`, `st_new_client`, `st_job_paid`, `st_busted`, `st_made_it`. Hot loses its `stand_in`; `--audio-test`'s `MOODS_WAITING` is down to `road`. A loop's `bpm` in `TRACKS` is the sidecar's `bpm_exact` (make_music.py nudges the tempo so a loop is whole 20 ms frames), because `loop_offset()` must land on the sample where the intro ends.
- **Song books.** `tools/make_music.py` loads `SONG_BOOKS` (here `tools/music_tension.py`) in `main()`: each book's `register(module, SONGS)` adds its songs and any instruments or drums only it uses (here a xylophone, a plunger trombone, a spring boing, a doorbell, chip kick/snare/hat, a register ka-ching, and `kick_seam`, make_music's kick with its click faded in over 1.5 ms so a loop that goes back to a downbeat kick has no jump at the seam). Books receive the running module, so `--preview`'s sample rate reaches them. This keeps chunks composing at the same time out of each other's lines; make_music.py's own instruments and songs are unchanged, so `rake_it_easy` and `st_day_end` render identically.
- **Border Return's cues** are local presentation inside the existing RPC handlers (no RPC, property or save change): `_rpc_go` plays `crossing_go` (2D, UI bus) for the local player in the roster; a local hit (`_throw_local`) or a host-sent `_rpc_throwback` plays `crossing_bonk` with `play_on` the flying body and no pitch wobble (`Sfx.play_on` gained a `wobble` argument), timed to `BORDER_THROWBACK_SECONDS` (boing on contact, slide whistle through the flight, pomf on landing), so the camera that follows you hears the whole flight at one level and the pomf lands on the landing; `_rpc_clear` for the local peer fires `st_made_it`.
- **Checks:** every new loop and stinger passes `tools/audio_check.py` (loops -19 dBFS RMS, peak at most -1, clean seams; stingers -18 dBFS RMS) and its PNG was read for section contrast (Hot Yard's breakdown and The NICE BOYS' hide section sit 2 to 3 dB under the rest). `--audio-test` gains `escape` (the border cuts to `escape` within a tick and the urgent fade and plays `escape_run`; `_rpc_go`, `_rpc_throwback` and `_rpc_clear` called locally log the pistol, the boing placed where you stood, and "Made It Back"; back in the store the music follows), alias checks in `moods` and `push_mood`, `picker_table`'s border cases expect `escape`, and `schedule_sim` now expects a hot yard to crossfade to Hot Yard.
- **Re-rendered at the W6 merge.** 4a and 4b were built side by side; 4a's `Song` now rounds the tempo so the intro, not only the whole loop, is whole 20 ms frames, and its `moods` check fails an intro between frames. `hot_yard`, `nice_boys_chase` and `escape_run` were re-rendered and re-encoded on the merged pipeline (tempos 115.942, 151.899 and 144.144; loop starts 8.30, 6.34 and 6.68 s; audio_check 0 violations; `encode_ogg --verify` 21.5 to 26.3 dB on the first pass, seam and second pass) and their `bpm_exact` registered. `nice_boys_incoming` (no intro) and the stingers were unaffected.
- **Considered:** renaming `crossing` outright (other features and saved logs may say it; an alias costs one dictionary entry); keeping "Lane Change" as a road-crossing cue (the crossing is going away); writing the songs into make_music.py (audio 4a composes there at the same time, and every song would conflict on `SONGS`).

### 2026-11-02: Parcels, streets and blocks are builders over data; a house is painted per vertex

- **Builders (art chunk 5, `levels/town/`):** `LotBuilder` (a house and yard at any transform, in the parcel frame: origin at the ground center, street edge at +z = depth/2 = +20, the job yard's own frame, the house facing +z, never rotated inside the parcel), `StreetBuilder` (`segment`, `intersection`), `BlockBuilder` (`build`, `build_for_job`), `SetPieces`, `SeasonDressing`, and `parcel.gd` (the root of anything that changes with the season: group `seasonal`, `apply_season` rebuilds its `SeasonDecor` or freezes its lake). Each is a pure function of its inputs (hood, seed, size, season): a `plan()` decides everything as data from RandomNumberGenerators seeded off the seed (`hash([seed, "parcel"])`, neighbors `hash([block_seed, "neighbor", slot])`), then `build()` places it. No global randomness, no colliders unless asked (`solid`), nothing networked, no StaticBody3D in the lot. Standalone for now: the job yard's block switches over in art chunk 6.
- **Critic C25:** `HouseBuilder.request_for(hood, house_seed, size, spec, neighbor)` maps the yard's own numbers (`LotGenerator.house_spec`, drawn from the yard's rng) onto the hood's look; archetype, door and accent come from `hash([house_seed, "art"])`, so the yard's rng draws never change. The yard's wall, roof and trim draws pick the hood's palette entries by a hash of the color. `LotBuilder` and (in chunk 6) the lot generator both call it, so the town's house matches its job yard. `HouseBuilder.spec_for(req)` gives the HouseSpec without nodes (cached).
- **Critic C24:** `LotBuilder.colliders(req)` (`[[center, size]]`: HouseSpec solids, roof solids, the main roof as a box, tree trunks) and `LotBuilder.skyline_boxes(req)` (`[[center, size, color]]`) for driving's streamer. `BlockBuilder.build_for_job(..., {town: true})` does only the yard-scope art (the ground tint; the yard's house, fences and beds are the lot generator's), and Mansion Hill's terrace is a block piece the town skips.
- **Signatures never depend on luck:** after planning, the block tops up each prop-based group a hood's style requires (junk piles, porch swings, pools, trampolines, violation flags, fountains, the rake topiary) by forcing the kind onto the nearest parcels until the minimum is met; set pieces guarantee the rest.
- **Chefty's lane and the van:** nothing but street surface (group `street_surface`) inside `ART_CHEFTY_LANE` from 0.15 to 3 m up (lamps stand at the sidewalk's back, poles back far enough for their crossarms, snowbanks on the sidewalk side of the curb, speed bumps and near-lane parking only past x 12). A far parcel whose house would sit within `ART_VAN_CORRIDOR_HALF` (13 m) of the NICE BOYS van's way out (the lot's PI * 0.75 corridor) is a vacant lot with a FOR SALE sign.
- **One mesh per pattern for houses:** HouseKit now paints per vertex by default (`paint_per_vertex`): the vertex color is the base color, CUSTOM0 the accent, the vertex alpha the snow amount (so a porch's no-snow faces no longer split a material), drawn by `Surfaces.painted(pattern, params)` (the surface shader's `painted` uniform, sRGB-to-linear with a clamp). Houses went from 12-20 draw calls to 5-9 (`ART_HOUSE_MAX_DRAW_CALLS` 10, checked by mesh_check). The meshes carry `keep_material` so `shared_look` leaves them alone. A block's parcels share one HouseKit for their lawns, drives and walks and one PropBatch for repeated props: a job block measures 390-530 GeometryInstance3Ds with its grass (Dandelion Acres, 22 parcels, is the most), under `ART_BLOCK_MAX_VISUALS` 650.
- **Headless checks:** `tools/block_check.gd` (run by `tools/mesh_check.gd`, the `meshcheck` CI step, no port): request_for archetypes, sign keys, every hood's job block (art hash twice, signatures, the visual count, Chefty's lane checked per MultiMesh instance, houses inside their parcels) and Maple Row through four seasons plus Lakeside's frozen lake. The dummy renderer forgets MultiMesh instance transforms, so `PropBatch` views keep a copy in meta `instance_xforms`. The CI art test with a client (A1-A13, port 7813) is art chunk 7.
- **The white glints (a bright spot with a dark middle on the gnome, ladder rails and wheelbarrow):** a NaN pixel smeared by glow. The shared cause was `to_linear()` taking `pow()` of a vertex color MSAA had pushed below zero, fixed in `props/stylized.gd` by art chunk 4; no glint shows in today's renders (small props, holds, every block). The same unclamped conversion was left in `tools/studio/icon_studio.gd` (now clamped, with `to_srgb`) and is clamped in the surface shader's new painted path. A second NaN source, `props/yard_models.gd` fence runs shorter than a post spacing (a division by zero in the post positions), is fixed, and mesh_check now checks every YardModels kind at three sizes for NaN-making vertices.
- **Grass** (`props/grass.gd`): `plant(..., options)` returns its root; options `tint`, `short`, `spacing_scale`, `area`. Existing callers get exactly the same lawn.
- **Networking:** none. No RPC, replicated property, save key, or yard or task generation changed (the job yard does not use any of this yet).
- **Review fixes (same day):**
  - **A parcel is a hireable house unless told otherwise.** `LotBuilder`'s `neighbor` now defaults to false, and `job: true` forces it false, so `{hood, seed}` alone (driving's streamer, every town house) is `HouseBuilder.request_for(..., false)`: the same archetype, door and colors as its job yard, never `a_frame` or `modern_glass`. Only the job block's anonymous neighbors pass `neighbor: true`. block_check plans 40 seeds per hood three ways and compares.
  - **Season decor is built exactly once.** `parcel.gd` has a `dressed` flag: the builder's last step is `dress()`, and a season turn before that (a block built over several frames) only moves `season`, which `dress()` then uses; the async block builds its remaining parcels for the moved season too. `_ready` catches up only for a root that was dressed off the tree. A turn drops every direct child named `SeasonDecor` or in group `season_decor`, so no stale holder survives. A `detail: "far"` parcel (the root keeps its `detail`) never gets season decor, at build or at a turn.
  - **Lawn clutter only goes on free lawn.** When the free piece of a lawn is narrower than 2 m, `_free_rect` returns an empty rect and that lawn gets no seasonal clutter (it used to return the whole lawn, drive and props included). No yard, no leaves. Tire planters and lion statues now mark their ground taken. The block's inflatable snowman stands in the middle of a parcel's free front lawn (decor `front`), never on a drive.
  - **`ART_VIOLATION_LABELS` is used:** after the top-up, `BlockBuilder._labels` gives that many plain violation flags their NOTICE OF VIOLATION words, nearest the job first, on top of the rebel flamingo's three. A label changes how a flag is drawn, not where it stands.

### 2026-11-01: Code never matches on player-facing text; the inline-text sweep fails

- **Decision:** game logic reads state, never the words shown for it. `ui/quip_player.gd` used to look for "on the way" and "is here" in the director's status line (the status said "are here", so the crew-arrival quip Q-009 never played) and compared the Border Return's status with "Thrown back". Now it reads `director.phase()` (`Phase.WARNING`, `Phase.CHASE`) and the new `border_return.gd is_thrown_back()` (true while the local player is being thrown back during a running crossing). Rewording a status line can no longer break behavior.
- **The sweep fails** (cast:3): `--cast-test` step 6 now fails on any two-word string literal on a text site (`.text =`, `text_sign(`, `_status = `, `_show_ended(`, `_text(`, `_set_phase(`) in `net`, `npcs`, `ui`, `levels`, `props`, `player` or `core`, outside `ui/strings.gd` and `net/dev/`. Every chunk after it puts its text in `ui/strings.gd` (critic C31). Only canon brand words painted on things may stay (`SWEEP_ALLOW`: HOMIE DEPOT, Chefty).
- **Data keeps ids and numbers; Strings keeps words.** `core/seasons.gd` keeps `IDS` and every number, and `name_of()` reads `Strings.SEASON_NAMES` (the summary uses `SEASON_ARRIVALS`); `net/town/neighborhoods.gd` HOODS and PERSONALITIES lost their `blurb` fields to `Strings.HOOD_BLURBS` and `PERSONALITY_BLURBS`, keyed by id. Names that are ids (hood and street names) stay with the data, as before.
- **HUD rows:** the cleanup and Chefty rows use the icons spec's amount keys (`hud_cleanup_amount` "%.1f%%", `hud_chefty_amount` "%d/%d", critic C7) inside `hud_cleanup_row` and `hud_chefty_row`, so the icon rows to come can drop the words and keep the amounts. Cast's planned `hud_cleanup` and `hud_chefty_bags` keys were never added.
- **Removed:** UI `homeowner_thanks`, `cue_radio`, `cue_arrived` and `cue_clear`, which nothing read after cast:2's pools.
- **Not changed:** the director's status still travels as a string in `_rpc_phase`, and Border Return's status is local; no RPC, replicated property, save key or generation changed.

### 2026-11-02: Key tokens everywhere, captions styled by Settings, scenes keep no words (settings chunk 4)

- **Every key name is a token.** All hard-coded keys in `ui/strings.gd` ("E", "G", "Tab", "left click", "A and D", "[E]") are `{key:<action>}` tokens now (critic C4 grammar), and every display site passes its text through `Strings.keyed()` at the moment it shows it: `interaction_prompt.gd` (new static `text_for(hint)`), `job_goal.gd` (the label, not `goal()`, so code comparing `goal()` with a `Strings.UI` value still works), `day_hud.gd`, `phone.gd` (`_text`, so host-written texts like Marty's welcome resolve on each peer with that peer's keys), `shop_window.gd` blurbs, `Strings.shop_item_blurb()` (returns keyed text, for shops:4's window), `voice_panel.gd` (refreshed on open), `ui_root.gd` (the HUD block and the rake hint, every frame, so a rebinding shows at once), `equipment/rake.gd`'s pile label, and `job_post.gd`'s sign. A plain hint (`"plain": true`) still gets no key in front, but its tokens resolve.
- **The prompt format** is `UI["prompt_format"] = "{key:%s}  %s"`, formatted with the hint's optional `"key"` action (default `interact`; critic C8's contract for chauffeur and shop prompts) and the text. `keyed()` compiles its pattern once (a static var, not a const, so the string audits don't read it as text).
- **Captions** follow `Settings` `interface`: `ui/quip_player.gd` has `show_caption(line)`, anchors its `Caption` label bottom-left (offset 80, 100 up, growing upward, at most 760 px wide and the screen's room, wrapping), sizes it `22 * caption_scale()`, adds a dark rounded StyleBoxFlat with `caption_backdrop`, and with `captions` off shows nothing but still plays the babble. `npcs/homeowner.gd` has `show_caption(line, seconds)` and, like the store clerk since cast:2, scales its Label3D's `pixel_size` by `caption_scale()` and hides it when captions are off (the timer still runs). The backdrop is screen captions only: a Label3D has no stylebox, and its outline already reads. The quip duration is `Constants.QUIP_CAPTION_SECONDS`; the look stays in the script (2026-11-01 "Tuning vs. presentation").
- **Run-event captions** (`ui/run_cues.gd`, the RADIO chatter and the NICE BOYS arrival and all-clear lines) follow the same settings (critic C19: all captions honor `interface.captions` and `caption_scale()`): the `CueCaption` label is `CAPTION_FONT_SIZE` (28) times `caption_scale()`, takes the backdrop through the shared static `quip_player.gd apply_backdrop(label)`, restyles on `Settings.changed`, and hides with captions off. The arrival line is not the only warning: the countdown (a HUD timer, not a caption), the vignette, the arrival beacon and the sounds always show, so turning captions off never hides that the crew is coming. The caption timer now runs even out of session, so a line never sticks after leaving. Crew barks (cast:6) should follow the same rule.
- **UI scale:** the lobby is anchored to the center (growing both ways) instead of placed at (460, 180), so it and the quip caption stay on screen at 125%.
- **Quip caption vs. the hotbar (W5 integration):** the caption keeps its 80/−100 corner (critic C7) but wraps before the hotbar's slot row (`hotbar_hud.slots_half_width()` plus a 16 px gap), never narrower than 340 px. At 1280 wide that clears the slots; only a very large UI scale or the 480 px hands card can still meet it, and the caption grows upward instead of sideways.
- **Scenes keep no player-facing words.** `ui/ui_root.tscn`'s menu, connecting, ended and Leave texts and `levels/world.tscn`'s gate and board signs are set by their scripts from `Strings.UI` (`menu_*`, `pause_leave`, `gate_sign_<zone>`, `sign_board_chefty`); the address field's `127.0.0.1` is data and stays. `net/net.gd`'s two "Player %d" fallbacks read `UI["player_unnamed"]`, and its and `net/steam_bridge.gd`'s session-ended reasons read `UI["end_*"]`. `--cast-test` step 6 now also sweeps `.tscn` files (in its script dirs plus `border` and `equipment`) for `text`, `placeholder_text` and `tooltip_text` sentences; "Homie Depot" joins the painted-brand allow list (the store's 3D sign).
- **Test:** `--settings-test` gains `strings` (spec check 16: every `{key:}` names a real action, `keyed()` leaves no token, no string hard-codes a key by five patterns, every rebindable action has a name, and with interact on R, phone on J and push to talk on K the prompt, a phone line, the voice panel and the goal line say so; the menu and signs read from Strings) and `captions` (the quip caption's size, backdrop and on/off with babble, its anchoring and the lobby's centering at 125% UI scale, and a homeowner's caption hidden when off and scaled when on), and its `bindings` check asserts "[R]  Hide" through `text_for`. `--open-settings=captions` (`net/dev/caption_demo.gd`) puts a talking homeowner in front of the player, a quip at the bottom left and the clerk's greeting on screen, for windowed screenshots at a UI scale written into the run's settings file beforehand.
- **Networking:** none. No RPC signature, replicated property, save format, or yard or task generation changed. The version-mismatch reason the host sends in `_rpc_rejected` is the same words, now read from Strings.

### 2026-11-02: The job yard and its street block are built in the house's neighborhood style (art chunk 6)

- **What:** `levels/lots/lot_generator.gd` gets `hood` (set by `job_lot.gd` from its replicated, spawn-only `hood`, like `mess`) and builds the yard in `HoodStyles.style(hood)`: the house is `HouseBuilder.build(HouseBuilder.request_for(hood, job_seed, size, house_spec))`, the same request `LotBuilder` makes for that address (critic C25), so the job house is the town's house. Then it starts `BlockBuilder.build_for_job(lot, hood, job_seed, yard, {town, yard_hash})`, which builds the neighbors, street, set pieces, back boundary, grass, the yard's mailbox and drive apron, and the street life over the next frames. `levels/world.gd` no longer builds the one static `Neighborhood.dress` block, `StreetLife.dress_lot`, or the LotZone grass at world start (that block was the same for every neighborhood); `Neighborhood.dress()` and its private helpers are gone (the layout constants, palettes, `house()`, `road()` and `lamp()` stay). `--hood=<id>` (`net/command_line.gd`) overrides the offer's hood in `world._load_job_lot`; clients get it through the lot's `hood` like any other.
- **The gameplay layout is frozen across hoods:** every `_rng` draw stays in its old order and count whatever the archetype (the house numbers, the door notes, the roof spots' jitter, `max(2, int(house width / 2.2))` columns from the yard's own house width even when the archetype's main roof is narrower or wider, for a spot it then moves or skips too; whether each bed is there, its length, flowers and weeds, decided against the house's middle as on the old box house; the tree's kind/height/crown/variant/turn; the spigot's side), and so is everything that goes into `_taken`: the beds get a fixed `ART_YARD_BED_STRIP` (1.5 m) strip in front of every house rectangle instead of their own rects, and the pool loungers use their own rng seeded `hash([job_seed, "lounge"])`. So the hood never moves a tree, a hedge, the pool, a hiding spot or a piece of litter, nor changes how many weeds there are to pull; `--layout-test` compares those across all six hoods for each seed and size. Look-only choices (the archetype via request_for's own `hash([seed, "art"])` rng; tree kind, crown and height; weathered pickets) use `_art`, seeded `hash([job_seed, "art"])`. Yard layouts still change from before art chunk 6 (BUILD_VERSION must be bumped by the integrator): the house's colliders, roof, gutters and windows follow the archetype, beds sit past the porch, the bed strip is taken, and the loungers take room before the litter is scattered.
- **Beds:** they stand either side of the walk to the archetype's door; when the door is too near a corner for a bed on its side, that bed goes past its sibling on the roomy side, and failing that the pair slides toward the middle (`_bed_xs`). A bed never leaves the house's width and is never dropped for where the door is, so a hood never takes pull_weeds off a job.
- **The house contract in the yard:** one `HouseBody` StaticBody3D with a box per HouseSpec `solids` entry (positioned at the house's origin); `Roof` holds the two climbable slopes from HouseSpec `roof` (`center`, `width`, `half_run`, `rise`, `top`) plus a box per `roof_blockers` (dormers, chimneys, cross-gables, door hoods, pediments: you bump into a chimney on the roof now) and `roof_solids` (estate wings). Clear-roof spots cover the roof's span less its 0.5 m gable overhangs, `max(2, int(span / 2.2))` columns by 2 rows by 2 sides, with any spot inside a blocker (grown 0.35 m) or within `ART_YARD_ROOF_SPOT_GAP` (0.9 m) of another sliding to free roof (rows 0.5, 0.3, 0.7, 0.15, 0.85, from the middle out): first on its own slope, then on the other one, up to the old grid's count. An estate's front slope is all dormers and pediment and its climbable roof is only the core gable, so this is what keeps Mansion Hill's roof job at 12 to 24 spots instead of 4; `--layout-test` fails a yard under `ART_YARD_ROOF_SPOTS_MIN` (10, 10, 16 by size), since clear_roof pays a flat rate. Gutters come from HouseSpec `gutters` pieces (split around blockers); a section every 2 m per piece, at least 2 on a side with one piece. Windows (with their sizes: `task_looks.add_window(..., size)` sizes the grime) and the spigot come from the spec. No lawn grows and no leaves fall on the house plus its porch (`_built_rect`, back of the house to the porch's front), since a porch deck would hide them.
- **The fence:** `hood_styles.gd` `job_fence` {kind, fresh, faded, surface}: picket, weathered_picket, vinyl, split_rail, privacy, iron. Each panel is one HouseKit mesh with `paint_per_vertex` off (one color, one material) so `task_looks.gd`'s paint branch, now per panel (`add_fence_panel(index, holder, fresh, faded, surface)`), repaints it by swapping the material. The paint spot is where it always was. About 400 picket MeshInstances per yard became 42.
- **Look-only extras:** the yard floor is painted in the style's ground color (the `grass` pattern with the style's flowers and dry patches; never lawn stripes, so mowing stripes stay readable); the drive, walk, beds, gutters and the pool's pad go into one HouseKit (`YardLook`); the shed is `style.shed` (a new optional key); yard trees take the style's kinds, crowns and heights but never shorter than `ART_YARD_TREE_MIN_HEIGHT` (6.5 m). A yard's dressing measures 129-149 GeometryInstance3Ds across the hoods (under `ART_YARD_MAX_VISUALS` 260; art chunk 7 tightens it).
- **Art hash:** the yard notes its look decisions (style, archetype, colors, fence, trees, ground, shed, loungers) into lot meta `yard_art_hash` and hands it to the block, whose digest includes it, so lot meta `art_hash` covers both (the town-mode holder's too). Lot metas `hood_style` and `archetype` are set at generate.
- **The block's job frontage:** `BlockBuilder.build_for_job` puts the yard's mailbox at (3.2, 0, 19.9) in the hood's kind (Dandelion Acres' kiosk becomes a post box: the yard keeps a mailbox for its sticky note), a drive apron across the sidewalk in the yard's drive surface, and calls `StreetLife.dress_block(root, lamps, back_boundary, chalk, seed, opts)`: posters on the near lamp posts (offset by the lamp kind's post), chalk on the near and far sidewalks where the style has a sidewalk and `chalk`, tags on a `wood_fence` (between its rails) or `cinderblock` back boundary only (the HOA wall's painted-over rectangles are set_pieces'), and the note on the mailbox's side. Grass also keeps off the yard's no-lawn rects (`yard.clear`).
- **The connected town:** `build_for_job(..., {town: true})` when `world.is_town_map()`: only the ground tint; the town draws the street. Town slots are still drawn and collided as `fallback_house.gd`'s classic box until the streamer builds them with `LotBuilder` (driving chunk E), so in `--town-map` a job house can differ from the stand-in you drove past. `townmap_test`'s parity step now loads the parity house's yard in its hood and compares its HouseBody with `HouseBuilder.spec_for(request_for(hood, seed, size))` solids.
- **Tests:** `--layout-test` now also sweeps every hood at every size on seeds 1000 and 8919 (36 more yards): its existing checks, plus the style and an archetype of the hood, HouseBody boxes inside the house's rectangle, roof, gutter and window spots outside every solid, gutters on both eaves, the spigot within 0.3 m of a wall, and the dressing's visual budget (`layout_test.result.yard_visuals`). The full art test (A1-A13, port 7813) is art chunk 7.
- **Networking:** no new RPC or replicated property (`hood` already rode the lot's spawn), no save change. Yard and task generation changed: the integrator bumps BUILD_VERSION.

### 2026-11-02: Playtest tools: fixed-step recording, scenario jumps, an observer (tools:playtest)

- **Why:** Taylan played 0.4.5 and found the game about 20% done in ways no single screenshot shows: leaves that vanish instead of moving, a rake with no stroke, a NICE BOYS catch with no gotcha, a camera that freezes in a hiding spot, a van ride you can't see. Agents had remote play (one command, one screenshot), so they never saw motion. `docs/PLAYTEST.md` is the guide; this is the architecture.
- **Even spacing is a fixed step, held to real time.** Godot's `--fixed-fps N` makes every frame exactly 1/N s of game time, but it can only be set at launch, scripts can't see it, and it turns real-time sync off (the game runs as fast as it can; `--max-fps` is ignored). So a playtest run is launched with `--fixed-fps N` and the game flag `--fixed-step=N`: `net/dev/pacer.gd` sleeps at the first physics tick of each frame until real time catches up with game time (a loaded machine just runs slower, never in bursts), and `net/clock_sync.gd` counts game time (physics ticks) instead of the wall clock (`game_ticks`), so rides, raids and the day clock (all on `Net.server_time()`) slow down with physics instead of running ahead of it. Measured: frames exactly 1/30 s apart with other agents' tests running, realtime ratio 0.99-1.00. Considered: Movie Maker (`--write-movie`) records the whole session from launch and can't start and stop from remote play; `Engine.time_scale` and `max_fps` tricks leave the step size to the machine's load; time-based sampling without a fixed step put frames 30-60 ms apart whenever PNG writing hitched.
- **Normal sessions are untouched.** `--fixed-step`, `--scenario`, `--observer` and `--playtest-tools-test` are dev flags (they turn on `net/dev/dev_tools.gd`); without them the clock, pacing and every gameplay path are as before. Scenarios also refuse to run unless `OS.is_debug_build()`.
- **The recorder** (`net/dev/recorder.gd`) captures after the frame is drawn, writes PNGs on worker threads (at most 10 pending), and builds the contact sheet and filmstrip itself with a 3x5 pixel font (the same glyphs as `tools/audio_check.py`), so it needs no Python; headless it records stand-in frames so CI can check the timing and files. Audio is an `AudioEffectRecord` added to the Master bus for the recording only. Each frame's wall-clock stamp is taken at its first physics tick (after the pacer's wait) so `tools/playtest_audio.py` can map the real-time audio onto game time; the wav's first sample is at the audio thread's next mix, `AudioServer.get_time_to_next_mix()` after the start (events.json `audio.wall_start`).
- **Event times are the frame's time (review fix).** The first recorder stamped an event with `max(_t, _phys_t)`, two clocks that started one frame apart depending on where in a frame `record_start` ran, and polled `audio/sfx.gd`'s log once a frame, so events sat up to a frame either side of the frame they were filed under and sync lags came out negative (-10 ms for the bag). Now one clock: `_t` is set in the recorder's `_process` (priority -1000, first in every process step), an event fired earlier in the frame (a physics tick, the network poll) is stamped when `_t` is known, a recording's frame 0 is the next frame whose `_process` the recorder runs, and the state polls run again after the frame is drawn so a change later in the frame lands on the frame that shows it. Sounds come through a dev hook, `Sfx.on_play` (a static Callable, empty outside a recording; it replaced the `plays`/`placed_total` counters), the moment `_accept()` takes them. The onset finder uses the median of the 60 ms before (a noisy bed's dips made every bump an onset) and looks only one hop early. `--playtest-tools-test` checks every event is filed under the first frame at or after it at a whole engine frame; the old recorder fails it. Measured: bag 7-20 ms, the rake's soft swell 50-90 ms, lags good to about ±10 ms.
- **Automatic flags are hints, not verdicts.** pop (a watched body moving more than 0.6 m in a frame faster than its speed limit), cam_snap, cam_frozen, view_frozen (the camera turned or moved but the picture didn't), visual_jump (a frame changing four times more than its neighbours). A sustained one (a van at 174 m/s) is one flag with `frames` and `until`. Following a fast vehicle over plain ground can raise view_frozen honestly; read the frames.
- **Scenarios use the game's own code** (`net/dev/scenarios.gd`): the director's `force_event`, `_begin_chase`, `force_capture` and its `_rpc_phase` message (sent to every peer, started earlier so the crew arrives in N seconds), the van's own arrival (its start time moved back so it arrives on the next tick), the hide spots, the leaf field's `request_rake`, Chefty's `request_enter`. Only the starting position is arranged (a host-ordered teleport). `reset()` undoes a raid, capture, ride, crossing, hiding spot or seat first. No new RPC: scenarios call existing ones on the host. The ride skip moves only the host's `_start_time`, so other peers drive on until `_rpc_arrived`; that handler now parks the body at the route's end (`_place(_parked_along)` and a physics-interpolation reset) instead of leaving it wherever the last tick put it, which also stops a lagging client's van or Chefty a little short of the end in normal play. An observer therefore sees the skip as one jump to the border. Remote play turns whole-number step arguments back into digits (`_word()`: cmd.json's numbers parse as floats, so `follow 1527381234` used to look for "1527381234.0").
- **Remote play sessions use the test save.** `Campaign._save_path()` now picks `campaign_test_<port>.json` when `--remote-play` or `--scenario` is on, since scenarios grant tools, start raids and get people caught; they never touch the real crew's save. (Superseded 2026-11-02 by "Dev sessions never touch the real save", which covers every dev flag.)
- **tools/play.ps1** gives each worktree its own port (9100-9499, from the worktree path) and folders (`build\play`, `build\play-<instance>`), records the PIDs it starts in `build\play\session.json`, and `-Stop` kills only those (it used to kill every windowed Godot on the machine, including other agents'). It waits for `ready.json` before the first command (a command written before remote play started was taken for a stale one), and launches Godot with `--log-file` instead of redirecting output (a redirected child kept the caller's pipe open, so `play.ps1 -Start | ...` hung until the game quit).
- **CI:** `--playtest-tools-test` on port 7820, headless, `Engine = '--fixed-fps 30'` (a new optional `$Tests` key in `tools/ci_local.ps1`, put before `--` on both host and client lines). The drive scenario needs `--town-map`; the same test checks it when run that way (by hand: see `docs/PLAYTEST.md`), and in the legacy world checks that drive refuses with a clear error.
- **Networking:** no new RPC, no replicated property, no save format change, no yard or task generation change. `clock_sync.gd` and `net.gd` changed only behind `--fixed-step`. `ride.gd _rpc_arrived` changed only what the receiver does locally (park at the route's end); its signature and what is sent are the same, so mixed builds still talk.

### 2026-11-02: The calm moods' music; loops and intros on whole frames; tempos registered exactly (audio chunk 4a)

- **What:** `tools/make_music.py` composes `leaf_me_alone_theme`, `curb_appeal`, `golden_hour`, `homie_depot_muzak` and `quiet_hours` (docs/GAME.md, "the calm moods' music"), encoded by `tools/encode_ogg.py`'s Godot route into `assets/audio/music/`. `audio/music_tracks.gd` registers them and the moods play them: title the theme, work_morning Rake It Easy and Curb Appeal in turn, work_evening Golden Hour, store Aisle Seven, night Quiet Hours. The Old Tune is gone from TRACKS, MOODS and `MUSIC_TITLES`; `assets/audio/sfx/music_loop.wav` stays in the sound bank until audio chunk 8's cleanup. Two instruments were added: `chime` (the store PA's soft tubular bell) and the `tock` drum (a lower, hollower woodblock for Quiet Hours' clock).
- **Loops and intros on whole frames:** a loop was a whole number of 20 ms frames, but its intro was only a fraction of that, so at 108 bpm Curb Appeal's intro was 391975.5 samples: the seam fold, the measured loop start and the game's seek could each round it a different way (audio_check saw a one-sample jump at the seam). `Song` now rounds the tempo on the largest stretch that both the whole song and its intro divide into (the gcd of their steps), so the intro is whole frames too and the loop point is a whole sample. Tempos move by about a tenth of a bpm (108 to 108.108, 88 to 88.073, 112 to 111.888); 96, 80 and Rake It Easy's 100 are exact, and Rake It Easy renders bit-identical.
- **Exact tempos in TRACKS:** `bpm` in `music_tracks.gd` is the sidecar's `bpm_exact`, not the round number, because `loop_offset()` is computed from it: the nominal 108 would put Curb Appeal's loop point 25 samples late. `--audio-test`'s `moods` check now fails a loop whose intro (loop_offset less the encode lead) is not on a whole frame, and a loop outside 60 to 150 s (15 s for a track only the raid warning plays), as the spec's check 2 has it.
- **Drum onsets:** `Song(drum_ramp=...)` fades every drum hit in over that many seconds (0.7 ms on the five new tracks; 0 by default, so Rake It Easy is unchanged). A kick whose first sample is its click, landing exactly on the loop start, otherwise reads as a seam jump.
- **Verify alignment:** `encode_ogg.py --verify` lines the playback up on the loop start (drums and bass, 8192 samples) rather than on the first sound. Aisle Seven opens on the PA chime, a pure F5 whose period is 63 samples, and both latencies locked one period off (129 instead of 66), so a good encode read as misaligned. All five new tracks, and Rake It Easy re-verified, measure 66 samples of latency and 21 to 26 dB SNR on the first pass, the seam and the second pass.
- **Networking:** none. Music is local presentation; no RPC, replicated property, save format or yard/task generation changed.

### 2026-11-02: One job site that moves; in the town the host infers zones from positions (driving chunk D)

- **One job site that moves.** In the connected town the job yard is still the one `lot_01.tscn` under `World/LotZone/LotSlot`, but `LotZone` is placed on the picked house's slot (`TownMap.yard_transform(house_id)`, never rotated) before the lot loads: the host calls `world.place_job_site(site, house_id)` in `_load_job_lot` before `add_child`, and every client calls it in `job_lot._ready` (before generating) from two new spawn-only `JobSync` properties, `site: Transform3D` and `house_id: int` (properties/5 and /6). Everything yard-relative (spawn points, the nav bake, the crew's patrol, tool and sign spawns, the leaf field, the homeowner) follows `LotZone`. The house is the offer's `house_id`, else the town house whose seed is the offer's (`Town.house_by_seed`), else `TownMap.test_site_house()` (`--job-seed`). `job_lot._exit_tree` calls the new `world.release_job_site(house_id)` on every peer, so the slot's dressing house and collider come back. `LotZone` stays where it was until the next placement.
- **The job loads on the pick.** `world.reload_job_lot(picked := false)`: in the town an empty site loads the board's current pick at once (the board's own refresh does this too, so the morning's first request is waiting without a click), a player's pick (board or phone `Take job`, `JobBoard._handle_choose`, which now also accepts re-picking the current offer when its yard isn't the one loaded) swaps the loaded yard, and a refresh by itself never swaps out a paid yard. `job_locked()` in the town reads the loaded yard's new replicated `stage` (`job_lot.gd`: `STAGE_WAITING` 0, `STAGE_ARRIVED` 1, `STAGE_PAID` 2; `JobSync` properties/7, spawn plus on change; host-set in `on_crew_arrived` and `_host_finish_in_town`), so host and clients agree (Campaign's clock flag is host-only): locked while Chefty is on an autopilot trip, while the yard is `ARRIVED`, or while someone is in `LOT` of a `WAITING` yard; never for a `PAID` yard, even with the crew standing in it or sitting in Chefty at its curb, so the next job is taken straight from the truck and swaps the paid yard (review fix). The goal line and beacon read `PAID` from the stage too, so a late joiner sees a paid yard as paid, and the homeowner offers that peer no prompt. `_load_job_lot` runs only after the world's `_ready` (`_built`), when the town's colliders and streamer exist. The phone shows `Take job` on the picked house too while its yard isn't the one loaded.
- **Paid, the yard stays.** `_host_finish` in the town settles (`Campaign.finish_job`), sends the hands back to the truck (`hires.despawn(true)`: each walks to Chefty in a new `crew_hand` `BOARD` state and frees itself there; `chefty.hands_aboard()` now counts a hand aboard as soon as its own body is gone, so each pops into a bed seat at the truck) and clears Chefty's bags (`on_job_paid`). Nobody is driven anywhere. `world._maybe_free_site()` (host, every `ZONE_TRACK_SECONDS * 5`) frees a paid yard once every player and Chefty are more than `SITE_FREE_DISTANCE` (90 m) from it and loads the next pick. A depart() trip home (tests) still frees it on arrival, as before.
- **The host infers zones from positions in the town.** `net/town/zone_tracker.gd` (`World/ZoneTracker`, host, town only) classifies every player every `ZONE_TRACK_SECONDS` (0.1 s): inside `Constants.STORE_AREA` is `STORE`; with a yard loaded, inside its `PAD_RECTS["LotZone"]` (yard plus near lane, in LotZone space) is `LOT`, grown by `ZONE_EXIT_MARGIN` (3 m) while already in it; else `TOWN`. A seated player is wherever Chefty's body is. `BORDER`, `TRANSIT` and caught players are left alone. Changes go through `Net.set_player_zone` (no teleport), so `Net` is still the one source and every consumer still reads `Net.player_zone()`. The first `LOT` entry for an open yard calls `world.on_crew_arrived()`. Zones may now be position-derived by the host only, and only in the town; clients still never infer one.
- **on_crew_arrived** starts the homeowner's clock once per yard (lot meta `crew_arrived`), and the hands hop off once Chefty is parked within `CHEFTY_ARRIVE_RADIUS` of the curb (lot meta `hands_off`): a crew that walked over gets its hands when the truck turns up. They're spawned at the tailgate's height just behind it (`CHEFTY_HAND_DROP_*`), so they drop off it.
- **The pocket rule (critic C23):** in the town, leaving the yard mid-job empties your pockets onto it only when you get more than `PROP_LEASH_METERS` (45 m) from the site after having been on it (once per visit), checked by the zone tracker; zone flips at the curb do nothing. **Props (`net_prop._come_back_in_town`):** the job's tools (group `job_tools`) and the yard's own props come back onto the yard when they settle more than `PROP_LEASH_METERS` from it or fall below y -5; anything else only gets the fall rescue, to `TownMap.respawn_point`. **Players (`player._stay_on_the_pad`):** in the town there are no pad rects for STORE, LOT or TOWN, only the fall rescue (to `TownMap.respawn_point`) and a clamp to `TownMap.TOWN_BOUNDS`; the border is unchanged. This ends the "teleported to the store spawn for walking off the pad" bug. `get_spawn_transform(TOWN)` is the respawn point nearest the player.
- **No doors in the town:** the store's Board (job_post depart), and the debug ToLot/ToStore gates are removed in town mode (`world._drop_legacy_doors`): Chefty is driven, or chauffeured.
- **Bedtime tow:** `day_clock._end_day` calls `chefty.host_tow_home()` first; if Chefty is outside `STORE_AREA` it is placed on the TruckPad (`dev_place`, which already sends `_rpc_placed`), anyone still seated gets out where it was, and the fee is split exactly (`floor(CHEFTY_TOW_FEE / players)` each, the remainder a dollar each to the lowest peer ids, host first) through `Campaign.charge` (capped at what they have), with Marty's text (`Strings.TOW_TEXTS`, kind "tow") through the town's text feed. The save gains the `vehicle` section `{dents, spent_bags, towed_day}` (critic's save keys; additive, read with defaults; written by `chefty_drive.gd` through `Campaign.add_save_hook`).
- **Coming home faces the road:** a real (not warped) autopilot trip to the TruckPad whose heading is more than `CHEFTY_HOME_SWING_MIN_ANGLE` off the parking heading within `CHEFTY_HOME_SWING_METERS` of the stop finishes with a kinematic handbrake swing (`_swing_step`: the chassis slides onto the stop over `CHEFTY_HOME_SWING_SECONDS` while the heading turns the short way, the tail out by `CHEFTY_HOME_SWING_BULGE`), like a warp, so it goes out in the snapshots and riders stay seated. Routes come into the store nose first off County Road 9, and the lot has no room for a loop or a three-point turn without crossing the spawns. The skid sound now also plays on every peer whenever the drawn truck turns faster than `CHEFTY_SKID_YAW_RATE` while moving (`engine_state().skidding`), so a swing or a hard drift squeals on clients, whose sim has no slip.
- **Where the job is:** `ui/job_goal.gd` in the town (STORE and TOWN zones) reads "Get in Chefty and drive to 14 Maple Row · 470 m ↗" (eight arrows, relative to this peer's camera), "Drive to ..." while seated, "Head over to ..." on foot within `GOAL_WALK_METERS`, and "Park in the chalk box ..." within `CHEFTY_ARRIVE_RADIUS`; "Paid! Take the next job ..." once the homeowner paid. `levels/lots/site_marker.gd`, built under each yard in the town on every peer (local, nothing headless), draws the chalk PARK HERE box at the curb point and the beacon: a translucent column (no fog) with the homeowner's first name on top. The camera only reaches 400 m, so a beacon farther than `SITE_BEACON_DRAW_METERS` is drawn pulled in along the line of sight and scaled down by the same share (it looks identical), widened with distance; the name is fixed-size, drawn over everything, and placed at most `SITE_BEACON_LABEL_METERS` from the camera on the same line so the fog never hides it. It fades out between `SITE_BEACON_FADE` meters as you walk up, and once the homeowner has paid it and the chalk box fade away over `SITE_PAID_FADE_SECONDS`. While a picked yard is on its way in (the host swapping a paid yard), the goal line already points at the picked house.
- **Smaller:** the run director doesn't roll a raid on a paid yard in the town; ambience treats `TOWN` like the yard on a neighborhood street (dogs, cars along that street); bots ask for the store from `TOWN` too, and a bot doesn't carry bags to a moving Chefty.
- **Test:** `--townmap-test` (port 7816) gains d1-d4: a request from house 2 picked on the board loads its yard at its slot (LotZone at `yard_transform(2)`, the slot's house hidden and its collider off, one HouseBody); walking in is `LOT` and starts the clock, and paid with the host inside the yard stays; 110 m away it goes and the house is back; house 3's request then loads there, its yard, the street and the store read `LOT`, `TOWN`, `STORE`, and leaves are cleared there. It leaves house 3's yard loaded, so CI compares both peers' `town_site` (46 house shapes) and `lot_layout`, checks the client's `lot_work.cleaned_fraction`, and checks the client heard the host's zones (new probe key `host_zones`, recorded here as an addition to the critic's list of extra probe keys; it can't fold into `town_site`, which must be equal on both peers). d2 also checks the yard is locked once arrived and unlocked once paid with the host still in it, and `town_site` gains `job_locked` and `job_stage`, which CI requires the client to report locked, equal to the host's, for house 3's arrived yard. The B part now clears the auto-loaded pick first.
- **Left for driving chunk F:** with the pad clamp gone in the town, a player about 3 m past the yard rect is `TOWN` and `director.can_capture` (zone `LOT` only) no longer catches them, and the raid's empty-lot timer winds down; F's "`can_capture` allows `TOWN` within the leash" closes this. Until F lands, NICE BOYS raids in `--town-map` are easy to walk away from.
- **Networking:** replicated properties added (`JobSync` `site`, `house_id`, `stage`) and the save gained the `vehicle` section: the integrator bumps BUILD_VERSION. No RPC was added or changed.

### 2026-11-02: The art test runs in the game with a client; budgets are measured; beds never cross the walk (art chunk 7)

- **What:** `--art-test` (`net/dev/art_test.gd`, port 7813, ci_local `art`, a netcode.yml step) is the in-game twin of `tools/block_check.gd`. The host frees and loads yards the way `--layout-test` does and waits for each block (lot meta `art_done`): every hood at every size on seed 1000, each hood's seed 8919 yard twice, Maple Row through the four seasons (`Seasons.apply_look(Seasons.first_day_of(s))`, restored to `DayClock.day` after) and Lakeside in winter, about 35 builds in 25 to 45 s headless. It reuses `layout_test.gd`'s `_check` and `_check_house` on an unparented instance (one copy of the yard rules) and `net/dev/guardrail.gd` for the words (critic C31), plus the art rule that no art text says "ice" in any case (the guardrail only bans the acronym). It ends on the configured yard (`--job-seed=777 --hood=lakeside --start-day=8`) and waits 4 s so the client has built it; CI requires `art_test.ok`, a nonzero host `lot_art` (probe key, 0 until `art_done`) and the client's `lot_art` equal to it, which proves a client builds the identical block from the replicated hood, seed and day. It pins `--legacy-map` until the town map moves the lot (critic C32).
- **Chefty's lane (A13) looks at a tree's vertices, not its box:** a tree's near or far mesh (`Look`/`LookFar` under a tree node) whose box crosses `ART_CHEFTY_LANE` only counts when one of its vertices is in the lane between 0.15 m and 3 m. A front-yard tree's box runs from its roots to its crown's top and reached over the street in every leafy season; its leaves are above 3 m there, which is fine for Chefty. Everything else (a rail, a fence run, a merged kit piece, MultiMesh instances) is still checked by box, since a low-poly run can cross the 4.4 m lane with every vertex outside it. A11 runs A13 on the yard too in every season, because CI's main sweep is winter, when the trees are bare.
- **Budgets are measured, not guessed:** `ART_BLOCK_MAX_VISUALS` 650 to 652 and `ART_YARD_MAX_VISUALS` 260 to 177, about 1.15x the most the test saw over every hood, size and season (a block 567: Dandelion Acres in winter; a yard 154: Oak Hollow's big yard in spring), counting each tree twice (its near `Look` and far `LookFar`) and the job house's own season decor. The test prints the maximum; ART.md says how to remeasure.
- **The job house gets season decor** like its neighbors, through the same `SeasonDressing.decorate` with `job: true` on a `parcel.gd` under the yard's Dressing (so it turns with the season), but only on the porch, beds, door and drive: never on the lawn, where litter and leaves are the work, and never string lights. Its autumn mums pot stands inside each bed at the back, clear of every weed (the lot generator picks the spot, since only it knows the weeds, and hands it over as a third bed entry), not on the lawn in front where it sat in the tire edging and grew grass through its base. Its winter snow piles stand along the drive (`Constants.ART_JOB_SNOW_PILES`), not at the curb where the homeowner waits; its pool float rides in the middle of the above-ground pool, clear of the leaves to skim. Look only: no collider, nothing a task reads.
- **Beds never cross the front walk.** `_bed_xs`'s last resort used to slide both beds round the house's middle and leave the walk at the door, so roughly 1 yard in 30 (4 of the integrator's 126 probe yards) had a bed across its walk. Now, when neither side of a corner door's walk has room for the pair, they are packed beside the walk (each on its own side if both sides hold at least `BED_MIN_LENGTH`, else both on the roomy side) and drawn shorter; their flowers and weeds keep their draws and squeeze together, so the weed count never changes. `--layout-test` checks every yard's beds against the walk and each other (Dressing metas `front_walk`, `bed_rects`).
- **No grass grows through the front walk:** the mow_lawn grid skips the walk's rectangle (it used to draw tall tufts over the paving). That changes which lawn cells a yard has.
- **The mesh check has to finish:** `street_life.gd` loads the store dressing when it dresses the store instead of preloading it; the preload dragged `props/shelf_stock.gd` (which names the `Campaign` autoload) into `tools/mesh_check.gd`'s --script compile and printed a compile error on every run. ci_local and netcode.yml now fail the mesh check unless it reaches `[BlockCheck] seasons`.
- **Networking and saves:** no RPC, replicated property or save change. Yard and task generation changed (bed positions and weed spots in yards where the fallback runs, and the mow grid along the walk): the integrator bumps BUILD_VERSION.
### 2026-11-02: Leaves stay a host grid; everything you see is a cosmetic layer over it (raking chunk 1)
