- **Why now:** Taylan's 0.4.5 playtest: the rake sound, the rake animation, leaves that "just teleport" ("we have physics, lets use it") and the leaf look were all terrible. Rake R1 rebuilds the stroke in five chunks; this one is the network layer the other four stand on. It revisits the 2026-10-12 "Leaves are a host-owned density grid" entry and keeps it.
- **What stays:** the host's 80x80 byte grid is the only truth, with 0.4.5's rules, bytes, scoring and tests. `net/dev/leaf_golden.gd` (in `--rake-net-test`) proves it: 40 scripted ops on a scratch field must give the per-op results, hash, `leaf_units` and `cleaned_fraction` recorded from the unmodified 0.4.5 field (commit 1 of the chunk). A deliberate rule change regenerates them.
- **Stroke events:** `stroke_event(ev)` once per stroke on every peer, in the frame its density changes, empty strokes included (a swing and a scrape on bare grass still happen), from one host helper `_emit_stroke` (driving:C3's plow uses it). The raking client emits its own prediction and takes the host's echo as an ack only; observers emit on receipt. Remote swings, flying leaves and sounds all hang off this one event instead of a replicated property, so a stroke has exactly one replication path. Mulch is coalesced to one event per mower per send tick (it was 60 reliable RPCs a second), emitted at the send tick right before the batch that carries it. A free-running 0.1 s timer let part of each window's mulch reach observers a batch ahead of its event, so raking:3 would have seen leaves vanish unhinted. The test now checks that every batch's mulch equals the events it acks.
- **Atomic batches and a prediction ledger:** one `_rpc_chunk_batch` per 0.1 s send tick replaces per-chunk RPCs, so a stroke's chunks never arrive split, and the batch tick lets the host's echo say which batch confirms which stroke (`ack_tick`). The raking client keeps the host's confirmed bytes plus its unconfirmed strokes and recomposes the whole field on each batch. 0.4.5 overwrote the prediction with each arriving chunk, so at any ping above the 0.3 s stroke interval the host's chunk for stroke N wiped stroke N+1: the snap-back. Now no cell ever falls back (0 reversals over 53 strokes at 150 ms / 2% loss, and 67 without the replay, which is how the test knows it can see one). The host and client share `_stroke_params`, so blower puffs (predicted as a 3-pass rake before) don't snap either.
- **Resets:** `field_reset(hard)` (new lot, join: snap) versus soft (out-of-reach or refused-bag resync: may animate). A soft resync carries the refused request's seq, and the client keeps the strokes it sent after that one (the host hasn't applied them yet) and replays them over the new bytes. Clearing the whole ledger would make a player who rakes on past a refused stroke see those strokes revert and then come back. There is also a deferred once-a-frame `leaves_changed(chunk)` for chunks whose bytes really changed, separate from `chunk_changed` (kept as it was for the 0.4.5 renderer and the stroke_visual burst until raking:2/3 merge).
- **Per-leaf Jolt bodies were rejected.** The bench for this spec: heaps of rigid leaves never sleep, cost about 3 ms per 1,000 bodies, and can't hand a leaf off exactly to a cell's slot, so the grid and the bodies would disagree; AGENTS.md rules out leaves as bodies anyway. Raking chunk 3 instead runs a custom leaf simulation (gravity, face-normal drag, flutter, tumble, bounce and slide on the pile) that carries each leaf that changes cell from where it lay to where it lands, as a cosmetic layer driven by these events; Jolt is used only for optional wall rays.
- **Kept on purpose:** push semantics (leaves go away from you; OQ-R1-1 push vs pull is Taylan's call, and a pull would be its own gameplay chunk). The in-place loop's cascade asymmetry (toward +X/+Z a stroke cascades leaves across the whole disc in one pass, toward -X/-Z one cell per pass; OQ-R1-2) is left for a follow-up R1-F (double-buffer `_apply_rake`, regenerate the golden constants, rerun gag_test's pile window and balance_sim), because R1 must not change field rules.
- **Protocol:** `_rpc_stroke(at)` and `_rpc_chunk(index, version, bytes)` are gone; new `_rpc_stroke_event(packed)` and `_rpc_chunk_batch(tick, indices, versions, data, compressed)`; `_rpc_request_rake` gains `seq, bite, tempo`, `_rpc_request_collect` gains `seq`, `_rpc_snapshot` gains `tick, hard, upto_seq`. RPCs changed, so the integrator bumps BUILD_VERSION in the R1 merge. No replicated property, save format or yard generation changed.
- **Ports:** 7821 rake_net, 7822 leaf_motion, 7823 rake_stroke (7820 is T1's playtest); next free 7824 (the CI ports table above).
- **Flags:** raking:1 registers all four R1 flags (`--rake-net-test`, `--leaf-motion-test`, `--leaf-motion-headless`, `--rake-stroke-test`) and their probe keys, so later chunks never edit `net/command_line.gd`'s long `wants_dev_tools()` line; `dev_tools` loads each test by path if its file exists.

### 2026-11-02: The leaf view: a litter skin, hero leaves and pressed grass over the host grid (raking chunk 2)

- **What:** `leaves/leaf_renderer.gd` is rewritten as the leaf view (contract C3 of `gap-specs/raking.json`), still the `LeafRenderer` child of each LeafField, so no scene changed. It draws a heightfield litter skin, hero leaves riding it and grass pressed under it (docs/ART.md "Leaves"); it never writes the grid. New: `leaves/leaf_palette.gd` (`LeafPalette`), `leaves/leaf_meshes.gd` (`LeafMeshes`), `leaves/leaf_shaders.gd`, `leaves/leaf_litter.gd` (`LeafLitter`), `leaves/grass_press.gd`, `tools/studio/leaves_subject.gd`, `tools/leaf_look_check.gd`.
- **Shader globals** (project.godot, their own commit): `leaf_height_map` (FORMAT_RF heights), `leaf_cover_map` (RGBA8: displayed density, look index, unused, grass press) and `leaf_rect` (the grid's world corner and inverse size; zero turns every leaf effect off). The last view to build owns them and zeroes the rect when it leaves the tree. The shaders read both maps with texelFetch and blend by hand, exactly as the CPU does, so no filter or format support decides where a leaf lands.
- **Built in parallel with raking:1 and raking:3:** the view listens to `leaves_changed` and `field_reset` when the field has them, else to `chunk_changed`, and then treats a refresh of every chunk at once (a lot's shaping, a snapshot) as a hard reset. It implements all of C3 including its own morph (every unclaimed unit eases in, every hero leaf it adds or takes fades), so it is complete without raking:3, and creates `res://leaves/leaf_motion.gd` as `LeafMotion` beside the field only if that file exists. Headless it builds nothing unless `--leaf-motion-headless` is on the command line or `keep_state_headless` is set (leafcheck).
- **Departures from the spec, on purpose:**
  - Hero count is `LEAF_HERO_MAX * (density / 255) ^ LEAF_HERO_CURVE` (6, 1.6), not one per 22 units with at most 8: the spec's count put about 20,000 hero leaves on a medium yard against its own 9,000 budget. A fresh lawn gets one per cell (the skin carries the carpet), a raked pile six. Seven per pile cell passed on a fresh carpet but not with half the yard raked into piles (seed 777 medium: 9,993 heroes, 465,000 triangles), the worst case mid-job, because hero leaves rise faster than density. At six that yard measures 8,760 and 414,000. Hero lifts are 4 mm apart, not 11, so the top leaves of a pile never float.
  - Hero MultiMeshes are per 2 x 2-chunk block and species (`LEAF_HERO_BLOCK_CHUNKS`), joined from per-chunk cached buffers: per chunk and species was up to 100 leaf draw calls against `LEAF_MAX_DRAWS` 60.
  - The mound spring's damping is 0.6 (about the spec's 10% overshoot; its 0.45 overshoots about 20%).
  - Leaf masks ride UV/UV2, not vertex colours, because the MultiMesh instance colour multiplies vertex colours. Litter leaves are drawn in their tree's shape (maple lobes, oak waves, ovals).
  - Colours blend leaf by leaf: each leaf takes the look of a cell up to `LEAF_LOOK_JITTER_CELLS` (1.6) cells away, hero leaves (`LeafPalette.look_cell`) and the skin's shader (uniform `look_jitter`) alike; per-cell looks alone drew a 0.5 m checkerboard.
  - Grass presses between densities 8 and 45 (`LEAF_GRASS_PRESS`), not 25 and 110, by up to 0.85 of its height (`LEAF_GRASS_PRESS_MAX`), not 0.8: a fresh carpet is about 60 units, so the spec's range left the grass under it standing at about a third of its press, and tips stood through the leaves.
  - `LEAF_SKIN_FULL` (70) is new: below it the skin draws its leaves sparser and the lawn shows through, which makes the ragged edge.
  - A unit that leaves or arrives with no hero slot (`take_launch` and `reserve_landing` returning slot -1) sits at `LeafPalette.skin_offset`, within `LEAF_SKIN_POINT_SPREAD` (0.45) of a cell's size from its centre, so always inside its own cell. Reusing `slot()` past `LEAF_HERO_MAX` put half of them up to a metre away, on lawn that may be raked bare.
  - Ending a fade (a launch, a landing on a fading slot, a kick) refreshes the fading MultiMeshes before the frame renders, and kicked slots never draw there; the three fading MultiMeshes have no visibility range (one box spans the field, so a range from its middle hid the far corners' fades). `stats()` adds `fade_drawn`.
  - `flake_log` writes one `snap` entry per hard reset (cell (-1, -1), slot -1), not one per cell.
  - Cover B (hard ground) is written as 0: the yard's no-lawn ground (drive, walk, under the house) starts with no leaves, and a pile raked onto a path should still look like a pile.
  - The leaf budgets are a medium yard's; a big yard may use 1.5 times them (`LEAF_BIG_YARD_BUDGET`; seed 777's big yard measures 10,515 heroes and 479,000 triangles fresh, 12,489 and 557,000 half raked).
  - The "turning" palette's centre is (0.80, 0.74, 0.26), not (0.66, 0.68, 0.26), which sat dE 2.7 from the olive the spec bans.
  - `levels/lots/task_looks.gd`'s mowing grass (the tall grass on most job yards) is pressed too, one line next to the spec's `props/grass.gd` line.
- **Lot generator:** `_shape_leaves` sets field meta `leaf_sources` from the yard trees it spawned (spot, crown, kind, variant), on every peer from the seed, before `shape_initial`. No change to the multiplier, the rng or the layout.
- **Neighbour yards:** `season_dressing._leaves` draws its autumn leaf patches as one MultiMesh of LeafMeshes maples in the flake ("mover") material and LeafPalette colours, so a neighbour's lawn matches the job yard's (still one node per parcel).
- **Test:** `tools/leaf_look_check.gd` (`leafcheck`, a headless `--script`, no port): mesh budgets, bent normals and masks; no lattice correlation over 1-8 cells (worst 0.066); no colour near the old olive and every season building; the mound curve and hero counts; `slot_world` on `surface_y` to 1e-4; skin points inside their cell; a raked lane eased in with a listener's claim delivered; a kick mid-fade leaving no ghost; budgets on seeds 777 and 1000 at every yard size, fresh and half raked into piles (a stand-in of `lot_generator._shape_leaves`, not the generator itself); the densest chunk's rebuild (0.25 ms). The studio's `--subject=leaves --kind=gpu` prints the CPU-vs-GPU height gap (0.12 mm).
- **Networking:** none. No RPC, replicated property, save format, or yard or task generation changed (the leaf_sources meta is local and derived from the seed).

### 2026-11-02: Leaf motion: the leaves you see travel between cells (raking R1, chunk 3)

- **What:** `leaves/leaf_motion.gd` (`LeafMotion`, group `leaf_motion`) is created by the leaf view (contract C3 in `gap-specs/raking.json`) as a sibling of the field and carries every leaf that changes cell from where it lay to where it lands. Each frame the view reports the field's changes (`targets_changed`); `leaves/leaf_flow.gd` (`LeafFlow.solve`, pure) explains them with the stroke events of the last `LEAF_HINT_SECONDS` (contract C1): rake, hand, blower, crew and plow strokes by monotone 1D transport along the stroke's own 8-way step (cells on one line parallel to the step, walked back to front, the k-th unit lost feeding the k-th unit gained, which is exactly `_apply_rake`'s cascade); bags and mowers as sinks to the bag mouth or the deck; burst bags as sources from the bag; whatever is left by greedy nearest matching within `LEAF_FLOW_MAX_DIST`; then gains nothing explains drop from `LEAF_DROP_HEIGHT` and losses nothing explains are left to the view's morph. `LeafMotion` claims the units it animates; `leaves/leaf_movers.gd` (`LeafMovers`, packed arrays, one slot per leaf) carries them.
- **Integer mass, no teleports:** a mover takes whole units off the view at launch (`take_launch`, the top flake hides that frame) and gives them back when it lands (`reserve_landing` when it starts homing, `land`), so displayed + in flight + unclaimed == target in units every tick, and a leaf that leaves a cell is the leaf you see fly (`LEAF_MOVER_UNITS` = 22, one hero flake's worth, instead of the spec's 40, so one flying leaf stands for one flake; `LEAF_MOVERS_PER_BATCH_MAX`, the distance share and the perf scale grow the units per mover of big items, but every transfer still gets at least one mover, so a rake cascade's many small transfers fly instead of morphing (a morphed flake pops in or out, the bug this layer exists to remove); the pool cap, rushing the oldest leaves home and the morph past it are what bound the cost). A later batch that drains a cell leaves are still flying into sends those leaves on (`retarget`, a new leg from where they are; a reserved landing is handed back by landing and taking off in the same tick), splitting one when only part of it must go on (`split`: a waiting one splits in two before anything is drawn; a flying one sheds a twin that grows out of it in mid-air and homes into a slot of its own, so nothing lands instantly). A bag, a mower deck or the yard's edge that empties a cell leaves are still flying into takes those leaves straight from the air (`to_sink`: a new scripted leg from where the leaf is, carrying its speed into the start of the slurp and long enough that no tick passes `LEAF_MAX_STEP`), so the cell never launches leaves it does not show yet and late leaves never land on a bagged spot. With no free mover to split into, the leaf is left alone and the rest goes the usual way (exact, since the view never clamps). A landed leaf parks on its exact slot until the view draws it (`LEAF_PARK_TICKS`). Kicks and dives take and return exact slots (`take_slot`/`return_slot`), so a kicked flake is never drawn twice: a launch that empties a slot whose flake is kicked up hands that slot back first and the airborne flake goes on as the launching leaf (its draw instance and motion carry over; more than one such flake each carries a share of the units), and a kick whose slot the view morphed away shrinks into the litter instead of landing on nothing. The bag's spiral is measured from where the leaf starts, so it leaves its slot without a jump.
- **The simulation:** semi-implicit Euler at the physics rate: gravity, drag across the leaf's face (`LEAF_DRAG_NORMAL`) and along it (`LEAF_DRAG_TANGENT`, the falling-leaf glide), a flutter pendulum about the long axis, end-over-end tumble from the launch spin (the pale underside flashes), the view's `surface_y` as the ground (the growing pile), bounce and ground friction. From `LEAF_HOME_AT` of the planned flight a critically damped spring (`LEAF_SPRING`) blends in over `LEAF_HOME_BLEND` toward the slot, tracking the slot's own motion (a mound rising under it), so leaves skid into place instead of snapping; at most `LEAF_MAX_STEP` a tick. `LEAF_WORLD_RAYS_PER_TICK` Jolt rays stop fast leaves against walls and fences (not while homing: the spring delivers a leaf even to a cell against a wall). Per-leaf Jolt bodies stay ruled out (AGENTS.md: leaves are a host grid, never bodies; heaps never sleep and cannot hand off to slots).
- **Launches (contract C4 rake heads):** a leaf under the head's span waits for the tine line to sweep past it, one beside the head for the head's forward plane (60% speed, half the hop: the bow wave), one already behind the line goes at once, one beyond the drive goes with the pressure wave from where the head stopped (`LEAF_SWEEP_SPEED`); with no head (blower, a hand without a posed rake, before raking:4) it goes on a schedule from the back of the stroke forward. Nothing waits longer than `LEAF_MOVER_WAIT_MAX`; events later than `LEAF_LATE_EVENT` skip waiting.
- **Caps:** `LEAF_DETAIL` by the video preset (pool cap and range; high = 900 movers, 32 m), fewer movers between `LEAF_MOTION_NEAR` and the range, none beyond (the view morphs), oldest airborne leaves rushed home when the pool is full and the rest left to morph, kicks only below `LEAF_KICK_POOL_SHARE`, and new batches get fewer, heavier movers when the simulation's average goes over `LEAF_MOTION_MS`. That budget is 2.0 ms, not the spec's 0.8: GDScript runs a mover in about 3.5 us here (one raker about 0.5 ms, four rakers and two hands about 1.5 ms average on the i9). A hard reset (`field_reset(true)`, a join, a new lot) drops every mover and claims nothing; a soft resync animates when small and morphs when big (`LEAF_RESYNC_ANIMATE_MAX`, `LEAF_FLOW_MAX_CELLS`).
- **The view must not clamp:** `take_launch`, `land` and the morph must keep `displayed` exact (never clamp at 0 or 255 there; draw `max(0, displayed)`), or the units stop adding up. raking:2's view implements C3; `net/dev/leaf_view_stub.gd` mirrors it for tests and captures until raking:2 lands (hashed slots, an eased mound, one MultiMesh a chunk, drawn `top_level` in world space since slots are world positions).
- **Chaff:** `leaves/leaf_chaff.gd`, six pooled CPUParticles3D (two leaf-bit sizes, two dust, grass, mower shreds), placed before `restart()` and then `reset_physics_interpolation()`. Each keeps its particle count (changing `amount` reallocates the instances) and emits all at once (`explosiveness` 1): with one_shot, particles still waiting to be emitted after `restart()` drew as black quads at the emitter in Godot 4.7.2.
- **Sound hooks (contract C5):** landings binned per `LEAF_SETTLE_AREA` over `LEAF_SETTLE_WINDOW` call `leaf_audio.on_settled(at, count, onto_pile)`, kicks and dives `on_kick(at, count)` (at most `LEAF_KICK_SOUNDS_PER_SEC` per player); both no-ops until raking:5 adds the group.
- **Test:** `--leaf-motion-test` (`net/dev/leaf_motion_test.gd`, CI port 7822, both peers with `--sim-lag=120 --leaf-motion-headless --leaf-motion-test`; the client reports its half to the host): per-cell conservation (target - displayed == the motion layer's ledger, every third frame, both peers), every flake change a launch/land/kick/morph, rake leaves launching as the (scripted) tines pass, +X and -Z strokes landing ahead (mean >= 0.5 m, < 2% behind), bag leaves in the bag within 0.55 s, burst-bag leaves out of the bag, no duplicate flakes while running through and diving into a pile, small resyncs animated and big ones morphed, hard resets snapping, running through a pile while raking it (no duplicate frames, kicked flakes handed to launches), bagging and mowing a ridge 0.15 s after a rake bite onto it (leaves redirected from the air, no launch taking more than its cell shows, books balanced and settled), burst-bag leaves fountaining (landing spread and peak height, not just their start), the `allowed()` gate for each display, no hinted fades on the host (and on the client once the field sends stroke events), four rakers and two hands for 20 s inside the caps and then 8 s on the low preset's 300-mover pool (conservation and quiescence through a full pool), no pops, no snaps, steps <= `LEAF_MAX_STEP`, and quiescence (the view equals the field, nothing in the air) on both peers. Before raking:1 the test sends the stroke events itself (`LeafMotion.add_hint`, mirrored to the client by RPC); the client-prediction check waits for raking:1. Windowed with `--leaf-motion-shots=<dir>` it films each moment in slow motion (frames 1/30 s apart from the side, first person at the spec's S4 times).
- **Networking:** none of its own. Cosmetic and local on every peer; it never writes the field. No RPC, replicated property, save or yard change (the test's `_rpc_hint`/`_rpc_final`/`_rpc_client_report` are test-node RPCs).

### 2026-11-02: R1 integration: leaf motion on the real view and real events

- **Why:** raking:3 was built and tested on a stub view with synthetic events. Merged onto raking:1's field and raking:2's view, `--leaf-motion-test` failed. Three causes were real presentation bugs and two were test assumptions.
- **Host mulch is explained before its event.** raking:1 tells a mower's mulch once per send tick, after its leaves already left the host's field (the one documented exception to "event in the frame of the change"). The host's motion layer saw an unexplained loss and faded it (869 units in the test). `LeafField.pending_mulch()` (host only, local, read-only) returns the mulch not told yet as C1-shaped events. `LeafMotion` adds them as hints for that one solve only, so the leaves fly into the deck in the frame they go. Clients get the event ahead of the batch and never need it. No RPC or event changed.
- **A morph never pops.** The real view dropped a fade once `LEAF_FADE_MAX` fades were running and showed the leaf at once (`instant`, 638 times in the big-resync and full-pool steps). `_step_morph` now holds a cell whose step would change its hero count while the fade slots are full. The cell keeps its accumulated step and catches up as fades finish (each lasts 0.15 s).
- **Headless hero count leaves kicked flakes out,** as the GPU path does (`_cell_slices` skips hidden slots). Without this, every airborne kick counted as a duplicate.
- **Test fixes:** the real view logs one `snap` per hard reset, so `snap` is now an allowed log entry. The conservation check skips frames while the view is morphing, as the stub version skipped its fades. The client hard reset needs `_send_snapshot(peer, true)`, since raking:1 made snapshots soft by default. The host test holds off NICE BOYS rolls: a raid caught the host mid-test and carried its camera off the yard, so every load-step batch was LOD-skipped and passed with nothing flying. The load steps now also need `peak > 0`.
- **`stroke_visual` is gone.** It was kept as a stand-in until the new view and motion layer merged. With raking:1-4 in, nothing needs it. `rake.gd` and `crew_hand.gd` lose their stand-in matching, and the playtest recorder logs a `stroke` event (kind, actor, bite, moved, local, at) from `stroke_event`. The signal was local, so the protocol is unchanged.

### 2026-11-02: The rake swing is a local timeline driven by stroke events (raking chunk 4)

- **What:** `equipment/rake_stroke.gd` (`RakeStroke`, pure static plus a per-actor registry) turns a swing phase into a pose sample; `props/rake_motion.gd` (`RakeMotion`) puts the rake there and publishes the head on its Pose node (group `rake_heads`, contract C4 for the leaf movers). The raker's own peer runs the swing from the button (`local_step`, in `rake.gd`'s authority branch, so bots swing too); every other peer re-phases it at each stroke event's receipt (`on_event`/`remote_step`, outside the authority guard). There is no replicated swing property: the field's stroke events are the one path (raking:1). Before raking:1 merges, clients match the old `stroke_visual` to the raker instead (dead code once `stroke_event` exists).
- **Geometry, not the spec's numbers:** the head runs 1.25-1.95 m out (raking.json asked 0.85-2.25). With a 1.6 m handle, a cartoon's 0.5 m arms and hands near the top of the handle, closer than 1.25 m puts the hands at the face and further than about 2 m leaves them off the handle even with the lunge; the 0.7 m drive is what fits. `--rake-stroke-test` asks for at least 0.6 m of travel a swing (it catches a frozen rake) instead of the spec's 0.9.
- **Two poses of your own rake:** with your first-person arms and camera, your rake is posed for your eyes (both hands at the top, aimed past the right shoulder, no lunge, `RAKE_FP_*`), and the arms may stretch to hold it; everyone else, and you in the over-the-shoulder camera, see the body pose. The head path (and so the stroke and the C4 head) is the same in both. Headless peers never take the first-person pose.
- **The hand's grips use the body's real shoulders** (last tick's) when it has a CartoonBody, so the lower hand shortens its span when the arm can't reach; `CartoonBody.set_work_pose` follows its target in 15 ms because the swing is already smooth (a slower follow made the shoulders lag the hands by 10 cm).
- **NetSmoother records turning on the spot,** not only moving: a hired hand that stopped and turned to its pile (and a player turning while raking in place) kept facing the old way on clients until it next moved.
- **Networking:** no RPC, replicated property, save or generation change in this chunk (the test node's two RPCs are dev-only).
- **Only a rake in hand publishes a head (review fix):** a dropped, pocketed, or bag-hauling hand's rake leaves `rake_heads` and is marked `phase=-1, down=false` (`RakeMotion.unpublish`), so raking:3's leaf movers never keep pushing with a stale lowered head. `--rake-stroke-test` drops the rake mid-drive and checks it.
- **A joining client gets everyone's wallet (review fix):** the host's `Campaign._on_player_joined` now also sends the newcomer the wallets of the peers already bound (`_rpc_wallet.rpc_id`, the existing RPC), so a client draws a crewmate's Tune-Up or Leafinator rake head from the first job, not only after the next payout. The rake's tier still comes from unlocks, not a replicated prop property. `--rake-stroke-test`'s client checks it has the host's wallet on joining and draws the host's Tune-Up head.
- **Tuning:** the swing's shares and timings (`RAKE_READY_LEAN` ... `RAKE_FLICK_FLEX`), the work pose's shares (`WORK_*`), `RAKE_HAND_REACH`, `RAKE_FP_GRIP_OFFSET`, `HIRE_TURN_SPEED` and `RAKE_LOAD_GUESS` live in `constants.gd`; the shoulder estimate and the grip search steps in `rake_motion.gd` stay local (geometry, not feel).

### 2026-11-02: Raking feel, the look: knees, a longer drag, a first-person framing, a windrow of riding leaves (R1b rakefeel:look)

- **The head path, revisited.** The 1.25-1.95 m drive above was set by hands near the handle's top. Now the upper hand's spot comes from the body's real shoulders (`RAKE_GRIP_NEAR/FAR`, chest close in, belly at full stretch) and may sit up to 0.5 m down the handle (`RAKE_HAND_SLIDE_MAX`), so the head plants at 0.85 m (the handle's top passing beside the left of the head, not through the chin) and the lunge carries it to 2.1 m; the right hand slides up to 0.24 m from the left at full stretch (`RAKE_HAND_SPAN_MIN`). The drive got a little longer in the swing (`drive_end` 0.74, `flick_end` 0.87; the bites stay at 0.2 and 0.5, so throughput and the field's strokes are unchanged) to keep the head under 0.15 m a tick.
- **First person draws its own head path:** `RakeMotion.fp_reach` remaps the same sample to 1.45-2.35 m for your own eyes, so the tines clear the hotbar. The C4 head published from it is that remapped one, so on the raker's own machine the leaves follow the rake it sees; every other peer poses your body's rake and its own movers follow that. Leaves land where the field says either way; only the in-flight look differs between seats.
- **Knees in `CartoonBody`.** Each leg is a thigh pivot with a "Knee" pivot holding the shin and shoe (`_shins`, `_shoes`; the hurt-foot wrap, stump and bandage hang off the shin). `_leg_reach` is two-bone IK in the leg's plane (`BODY_THIGH`, `BODY_SHIN` to the shoe's middle). `set_work_pose(lean, twist, hip, crouch := 0.0)` (C4 stays compatible) plants the feet in `RAKE_STANCE_*` while working and standing (`stance()` 0..1, blended over `RAKE_STANCE_SECONDS`, off above `RAKE_STANCE_SPEED`), sinks the hips by crouch and solves the knees; the head rides the lean round the hips at its real height (`WORK_NECK` 0.72; it floated off the collar at 0.55) and sinks with the crouch. The NICE BOYS' leg mesh split at the knee too (shorts on the thigh, the knee, shin, sock and stripe on the shin); their outfit's extra triangles fall slightly.
- **The windrow is a mover state.** `LeafMovers.RIDE`: a rake or crew leaf launched while its actor's head is down (and its landing spot still ahead) locks to a spot on a berm ahead of the tines (across the head, 4-30 cm ahead, stacked by how many ride), eases into it from where it was picked up at most `RAKE_RIDE_SLIDE` m/s, rolls, and is let go (FLY, aimed at its slot, with part of the head's speed) when the head lifts, at `RAKE_RIDE_MAX`, early for a spilling share, or once its own spot is behind the berm. `LeafMotion._read_heads` adds the head's velocity. The books are untouched: units are taken at launch and given back at landing as before, the ledger and the inbound redirects count RIDE as in flight. Leaves already behind the tines when their stroke lands now launch over `RAKE_SWEEP_STAGGER` and ride (they slide up into the bunch) instead of hopping off at once.
- **Ragged edges in the litter shader.** Each litter leaf decided by the nearest cell's density, so a raked edge was a 0.5 m staircase. Now a pixel first reads the 5x5 cells around it; where they agree (most of the lawn) every leaf uses that, otherwise each leaf reads the bilinear density off a slow wobble of its own middle (`LEAF_EDGE_WARP`), and a `LEAF_STRAY_SHARE_EDGE` share read a neighbour up to `LEAF_STRAY_REACH` away at `LEAF_STRAY_WEIGHT` (stragglers). Every input is a function of the leaf's middle, so no leaf is cut in two; the 5x5 bound covers every leaf that can reach the pixel (made exact by the review fix below). `LEAF_GRASS_SPRING` 0.4 s became 2.5 s.
- **Tests:** `--rake-stroke-test` (its existing port) now wants at least 1.1 m of travel a swing, the stance planted, the knees bent over 20 degrees in the drive and the back leaning; `--leaf-motion-test` checks leaves ride the windrow (`windrow`).
- **Networking:** no RPC, replicated property, save format or yard/task generation change.
- **The flick's sound moved with it:** `LEAF_SFX_FLICK_PHASE` is 0.24 (drive_end 0.74 minus bite B 0.5), so the fwip lands on the wrist flick (it played about 24 ms early at 0.2). (R1b integration: superseded; rakefeel:sound removed the constant and times the flick from `RAKE_SWING_PHASES` itself, so it follows these phases.)
- **Review fixes (same chunk):**
  - *Feet are world-anchored in the stance.* The stance used to pin the feet in body space whenever the raker was slower than 0.9 m/s, so a raker creeping behind the rake skated. Now `CartoonBody._stance_feet` keeps each stance foot on its world spot and steps it (one at a time, `RAKE_STEP_*`) once the body has carried its home in the stance more than `RAKE_STEP_DRIFT` away (only with the hips near upright, `RAKE_STEP_LUNGE_MAX`), or at once when the leg comes within `-RAKE_STEP_STRETCH` of not reaching it (the lunge carrying the hips past a trailing foot). Above `RAKE_STANCE_SPEED` (0.75 m/s) the walk cycle owns the legs, blended over `RAKE_STANCE_SECONDS` (0.25 s). `RAKE_STANCE_BACK` moved in to 0.09 m so the back leg reaches the full lunge standing still. `foot_position(i)` / `foot_planted(i)` are for tests; `--rake-stroke-test` checks that a planted foot moves at most 3 mm a tick while the host creeps at 8 mm a tick, and that each foot steps.
  - *The head's speed is the pose's own* (C4 `head_vel`, measured over real time per drawn frame), not a per-tick difference of per-frame poses; it counts as zero once the pose is older than `RAKE_HEAD_VEL_STALE` or faster than `RAKE_HEAD_VEL_JUMP`. `_read_heads()` is back to its old signature.
  - *Riders get rushed.* `LeafMotion._rush_oldest` takes RIDE movers too, riders first, so a full pool lets the windrow go before it hurries flights.
  - *The litter's edge test is exact.* `even` now means the 5x5 cells are all the same value (not within 4), and the warp and straggler reaches are clamped in the shader to a cell less two tiles (`LEAF_STRAY_REACH` 0.27), so every read a leaf can make from any of its pixels lies inside those 25 cells: where they are equal each read would return `lo`, and a leaf decides the same at every pixel, whichever branch a pixel takes. The 5x5 scan stops at the first two cells that differ. Cost, honestly: a pixel with no edge near it (bare lawn included) now reads 25 texels before it can discard, against 4 before; edge pixels read up to 4 per candidate leaf. Not yet measured in frame time.
  - *Feel numbers moved to constants:* the windrow's spill, hop, carry, roll, wobble and drop-off numbers (`RAKE_RIDE_*`; `RAKE_RIDE_CARRY` is now the true share, 0.275, the same motion as before), the head lag's clamp and smoothing, the hop/jump/fallen knee angles (`BODY_*`) and the crouch's rise (`RAKE_CROUCH_RISE`).

### 2026-11-02: Rake and leaf sounds play from the leaf field's stroke events, keyed per source (raking chunk 5)

- **What:** `audio/leaf_audio.gd` (class `LeafAudio`, a child Sfx makes, group `leaf_audio`) hooks the current leaf field's `stroke_event` (raking:1, contract C1; `has_signal` guard, re-hooked per lot) and plays every rake, hand, crew, bag, scatter and plow sound from it, positionally, on every peer; the leaf motion layer reports landings and kicks through `on_settled` / `on_kick` (contract C5). Nothing in `equipment/` calls it. `Sfx.play_at(id, at, db, wobble, source := 0, pitch := 1.0)`: the gap throttle is keyed `id:source` (0 keeps the old id-only key), `pitch` multiplies the wobble. `audio/sound_bank.gd` entries may carry `wobble`, `db_jitter`, `unit_size`, `max_distance` and `shuffle` (a shuffle bag of variants, `Sfx.pick_variant`); every existing id keeps its old behaviour. Recipes live in `tools/sfx_leaves.py` / `tools/sfx_grains.py`, out of `make_sfx.py`'s much-edited `SOUNDS` dict; `tools/rake_audio_check.py` measures them.
- **Why:** the stroke event already reaches every peer exactly once at the bite (predicted on the raker, applied on the host, received elsewhere), so sound, leaves and the swing line up with no RPC of audio's own, and observers finally hear other players rake. Keying the throttle per source stops two rakers' strokes from cancelling each other.
- **Considered:** playing from `equipment/rake.gd` (only the raker would hear it, and it would duplicate raking:4's timeline); a synced "is raking" property (a second replication path for strokes).
- **The bag tie is LeafAudio's on every peer.** On the host the field's `_apply_collect` emits `bag_requested` (so `world._spawn_bag` runs) before it tells the `bag` stroke, so a tie from `_spawn_bag` came first: the host (every solo player) heard tie, open, stuff. `world._spawn_bag` now skips its `bag` play when `LeafAudio.owns_bag_tie(field)` (the field has `stroke_event` and a LeafAudio is listening), and LeafAudio schedules the tie at `BAG_TIE_DELAY` for `bag` and `crew_bag` alike; before raking:1 merges, world still ties its own bags. `LeafAudio.handle(ev)` lost its `host` argument. `--audio-test`'s `bag_host` runs the host's real order (`world._spawn_bag_for` with a stand-in field that tells strokes, then the stroke) and checks one tie, last.
- **Networking:** no RPC, replicated property, save or generation change.
- **Revisit:** when Taylan has listened (the acceptance gate), and when audio:8 sets the final mix.

### 2026-11-02: Rake sounds are timed by the swing's phases in physics ticks; positional sounds start when asked (R1b rakefeel:sound)

- **What:** `LeafAudio` places every sound after a bite from the swing's phases: `after_bite(bite, crew, tempo, point, actor)` = (point - the bite's phase) x tempo, where the bite's phase is `RakeStroke.bites(crew)`, points come from `Constants.RAKE_SWING_PHASES` (`swing_phase`), and on a peer that is drawing that actor's swing within `LEAF_SFX_LIVE_PHASE_SLACK` of the bite, the live `RakeStroke.phase_of(actor)` (the raker's own swing bites on the tick nearest its phase, up to half a tick early). Waiting plays count down in `_physics_process` by the tick's delta and fire on the tick nearest their moment; one asked for on a tick starts counting on the next, like the swing (stamped with `Engine.get_physics_frames()`). The plant and the scrape start at "plant" (now: the first bite is the plant), the flick at "drive_end" after the swing's last bite; `scrape_pitch` stretches the scrape over plant..drive_end. `RAKE_SCRAPE_DELAY` and `LEAF_SFX_FLICK_PHASE` are gone. `Constants.RAKE_CREW_BITES` is `[0.2]` (was 0.25, after the plant).
- **Why:** the old scheduling was wall-clock msec flushed in `_process`, so a "15 ms later" scrape was asked a frame later and, being a 3D player, started another frame after that. Phases keep the sound on the swing whatever rakefeel:look does to its shape or tempo; physics ticks are the swing's own clock (wall time drifts from it whenever a frame runs long).
- **Positional sounds start when asked.** Godot 4.7's `AudioStreamPlayer3D.play()` only arms the sound; it reaches the mix on the player's next internal physics tick. Measured in a bare project at 30 fps (R1b): 2D 1-11 ms, 3D asked from `_physics_process` 0-11 ms, 3D asked from `_process` 31-41 ms. Everything an observer hears is asked from the network poll (between ticks), and LeafAudio's old delayed plays from `_process`. `Sfx._start_now` runs `NOTIFICATION_INTERNAL_PHYSICS_PROCESS` on the new player right after `play()` (from `play_at` and `play_on`), which starts it in the next mix (measured -4 to 10 ms); the real tick then only updates its panning. This applies to every positional one-shot, not just the leaves.
- **Godot's 3D gain clamp and muffle are per-id now.** `AudioStreamPlayer3D` clamps distance gain + `volume_db` to `max_db` (3 dB): with `RAKE_SFX_UNIT_SIZE` 3.5 m everything the raker hears was pinned at +3 dB, so trims and the leaf layer's scaling by leaves moved did nothing up close. It also low-passes by how far under full volume a sound is, its own trim included (-24 dB at 5 kHz by default), which took a quiet crackle's clicks away. Bank keys `near_db` (`max_db` = volume + near_db) and `muffle_db` (`attenuation_filter_db`) are set for the leaf ids only (`RAKE_SFX_NEAR_DB` 6, `RAKE_SFX_MUFFLE_DB` -6); every other id keeps Godot's defaults, so the rest of the mix is unchanged. Per-id trims are `LEAF_SFX_MIX_DB`.
- **The tie is `bag_tie`** (`tools/sfx_leaves.py`, 3 variants, -27 dBFS RMS, no membrane thump), at `BAG_TIE_DELAY` 0.7 s; `bag` keeps its other users (`world._spawn_bag` before raking:1, a bag prop bursting, the settings preview). `tools/rake_audio_check.py` checks the bag's beats (50 ms short-term peaks plus the game's trims) within 3 dB of each other and draws the previews at the game's trims and timings.
- **Tests:** `--audio-test` (port 7811, no new port) `leaf_audio` gains `sync_bites` (every swing's first bite at or before its plant, the last before drive_end), `sync_phase` (a real `RakeStroke.local_step` swing at 60 Hz crosses drive_end on exactly the tick the flick is planned for, at 0.6 s and 0.45 s swings), `sync_ticks` (a waiting play fires within a tick of its moment, counted in physics frames), `crew_tempo` (a hand's one-bite swing at its own slow tempo plans the flick for that swing's drive_end and pitches the scrape to it) and `near_muffle`; the bag checks expect `bag_tie` and no `bag`. `--rake-stroke-test`'s hand check requires every `crew_rake` event to carry the hand's cycle.
- **Networking:** no RPC, replicated property, save or yard/task generation change. `RAKE_CREW_BITES` changes when a hired hand's stroke lands inside its own swing (local presentation and the host's crew timing; nothing replicated). A `crew_rake` stroke event now fills its existing `tempo` field with the hand's swing (`host_hand_rake(..., tempo)`, from `crew_hand._cycle()`) instead of 0: with one bite, a hand's swing is its own last bite, so its flick and scrape need that swing's length (the default 0.6 s put the flick 25-200 ms early); clients' `RakeStroke.on_event` uses it in place of the stroke-gap guess. Same 36-byte packet, so no format change.
- **Revisit:** when Taylan has listened, and when audio:8 sets the whole mix (the music under work is the obvious next lever: a gentle duck while raking was out of this chunk's scope).

### 2026-11-02: Dev sessions never touch the real save (save:guard)

- **Why:** in W6 agents' windowed runs (`--start-zone`, `--job-seed`, `--rank`, `--open-phone`, `--screenshot`, `--tour` ...) wrote Taylan's real `campaign.json`: dev hires, a player named Claude with credits, extra lots and a vehicle section. Only `--run-seconds`, the smoke test, `--remote-play` and `--scenario` picked the test save; every other dev flag fell through to the real one.
- **The rule is an allowlist, not a list of dev flags.** `net/command_line.gd` records every user argument that isn't a player flag in `dev_flags` (`PLAYER_FLAGS` `--host`, `--no-steam`, `--town-map`; `PLAYER_FLAG_PREFIXES` `--join=`, `--name=`), unknown ones included, so a new flag is a dev flag until someone adds it to the allowlist on purpose. `is_dev_session()` is true with any dev flag, a headless display or a Movie Maker run; `dev_session_reasons()` names them. `--town-map` is a player flag only alone: next to any dev flag the session is a dev one anyway. `--port=` is a dev flag: no menu offers a port (it is a `docs/NETCODE.md` dev flag), and agents pick custom ports precisely to keep their runs apart, so a hand-launched `--host --no-steam --port=9150` is a dev session. Engine arguments Godot consumes before `--` (`--fixed-fps`, `--quit-after`, `--script`, `--quit`) never reach scripts (`OS.get_cmdline_args()` leaves them out; checked on 4.7.2), so they cannot mark a session and are not checked; an automated windowed run has to carry a dev user flag too (`ci_local.ps1`, `play.ps1` and the soaks all pass `--port=`, and `--fixed-step=` goes with `--fixed-fps`). The editor's own engine arguments are not in the list, so an editor or debug run with no game flags keeps the real save.
- **One resolver.** `Campaign.save_path()` (static `resolve_save_path(dev_session, port_given, port)`): a player's session gets `user://saves/campaign.json`; a dev session gets `campaign_test_<port>.json` when `--port=` was given (every test, `tools/play.ps1`, the soaks) and `campaign_dev.json` when it wasn't (hand-launched windowed runs). `Settings.path()` uses the same `is_dev_session()`, so the settings file follows the same rule (it used to check only `wants_dev_tools()`). This replaces the 2026-10-20 "Test save" rule and the playtest tools' remote-play/scenario check.
- **The guard.** `Campaign._save()` calls `_refuse_write(path)` before touching anything: when a dev session resolves the real save, its `.tmp` or its `.bak` (static `write_forbidden`, compared on the globalized, lower-cased path), it `push_error`s with the path and the reasons and writes nothing.
- **Tests:** `--settings-test` (port 7808, no new port) gains `save_path` (the run's save is `campaign_test_<port>.json`; the resolver's three cases; four player flag sets classify as player; 43 dev flags, an unknown one included, each count as dev beside each player set; the guard forbids the real save, `.tmp`, `.bak` and its absolute path and allows the test saves) and `real_save` (after a `save_now()` that lands in the test save, the real `campaign.json`, its `.bak` and `settings.cfg` keep their modified time and MD5). `tools/ci_local.ps1` also stamps those three files before the run and fails a `real-save` result if any changed by the end, covering every test and soak in the run.
- **Networking and saves:** no RPC, replicated property, save format or generation change. Existing test saves are untouched; a hand-run dev session now starts from `campaign_dev.json` instead of the real save. Taylan's real save is left as it is (backed up; he decides).

### 2026-11-02: tossing leaf bags into Chefty (bags:throw)

- **Why:** Taylan asked to throw bags into Chefty instead of walking over and pressing E. Before this, a toss could not go in: a survey of 42 throws from 2, 4 and 6 m at every wind-up (the old `--toss-survey`) caught none. Bags left the player 0.4 m off the ground with little lift, so they hit the bed's side wall; from 2 m they spawned inside it; hard throws from 6 m sailed over the whole truck. A bag that did land in the bed vanished the same frame.
- **One flight model, shared.** `net/components/bed_toss.gd` is pure math every peer runs: the leaf bag's lob (`launch_velocity`), a flight stepped the way Jolt steps a free body (gravity, the project's linear damping), the catch volume (`catches()`), the truck's hull for a bag-sized box (`hits_hull()`), the close-by hop (`close_toss()`) and the aim help (`assist()`, the smallest nudge within the caps that a flight says goes in). The host launches with it, the arc preview draws it, and a throwing client predicts its own bag with it, so all three agree. Flights run in the bed's space with one transform per flight, because the aim help and the preview run dozens a frame.
- **Host-authoritative catch, counted once.** The host checks every loose leaf bag each physics tick (`net_prop.gd _try_load_bag`). On a catch it sends `_rpc_bed_catch(slot, seconds, amount, speaker, line)` first and calls `accept_bags` second, so every peer holds the load spot before the count arrives; the bag is counted at the catch and nowhere else. A full Chefty (`bag_count() >= TRUCK_BAG_CAPACITY`) never catches: a bag that meets a drawn bag of the heap (`truck_bed_load.gd in_pile`, each spot's side reach and top, not the catch margin) is pushed back out the way it came (`_rpc_bed_refuse(at)`, cosmetic). E by the truck uses the same catch (a hop from the hands), so no path frees a bag in mid-air any more.
- **No teleports: each peer draws the settle itself.** After a catch every peer glides its own drawn bag (host: the body; client: the `Visual`, `top_level` while it does, since the body jumps with each snapshot) from where it drew it, moving as it moved, into its spot in bed space (`bed_toss.gd settle_plan()`: cubic Hermite stretches, so position and speed are continuous, and it rides along if Chefty drives). When the straight glide would cut through a side wall, the tailgate or the cab (`cut_depth()`, the drawn bag's turned box against the truck's solid boxes, `WALLS`), it goes through points over the wall instead: up first if it starts beside the truck, over at `BED_SETTLE_CLEARANCE` above the wall for the bag's reach, down into the spot, with each point's speed held back so no stretch swings past its next point, and done turning by the time it's over the wall. Its length sets the glide's seconds (`settle_seconds()`), which the host sends with the catch. Review fix: the first version glided the close-range hop and the E heave straight through the side wall. It lands, sits `BED_SETTLE_HOLD_SECONDS`, and the host frees it; the load's own bag (`props/truck_bed_load.gd`, cosmetic meshes from the replicated count) shows the same frame. The test measures the gap at the swap (`max_pop_m`, 0.0).
- **A throwing client draws its own toss at once.** At 150 ms the host's bag starts a round trip after the key comes up. So the thrower's peer sends where the bag left its hands (`request_throw`'s `from`, trusted within `THROW_FROM_TOLERANCE`) and flies it locally from that point with the same launch and aim help (`_predict_toss`); if its flight goes in, it starts the settle on its own say-so (provisional) and the host's catch continues it; if the host never confirms within `BAG_TOSS_PROVISIONAL_SECONDS`, it eases back onto the host's bag. A flight that misses waits where it came down until the host's bag gets there, then eases onto it. The client also draws a bag in its own hands where the host hangs it (it used to be drawn 0.35 m low and then overwritten by the smoother's lagged copy). Measured at `--sim-lag=150`: largest frame-to-frame move of the drawn bag 0.15 m (its flight speed), no swap gap.
- **Networking:** `_rpc_request_throw` gained a `from: Vector3` argument (a signature change); new RPCs `_rpc_bed_catch` and `_rpc_bed_refuse` on the prop. No replicated property, save format, or yard/task generation change. The integrator should bump `BUILD_VERSION`.
- **Test:** `--toss-test` (`net/dev/toss_test.gd`, port 7824, `Extra = '--toss-test --sim-lag=150'`, host plus a Target client with its own half): host tosses from 3 and 6 m and a short one the aim help saves, each counted once with its dollars, no jump, no swap gap, the stack drawn to the count; a toss along the truck stays on the ground; the arc preview agreed; the lagged client's tosses from 3 and 6 m count once; a thrown wheelbarrow still unloads; a full Chefty bats a bag out uncounted; a 2 m toss and E from 0.9 m and 3 m off the side go in with the drawn bag never cutting into the truck deeper than at its ends (measured every frame); and a sweep plans 2475 settles from beside the truck into spots across the load and checks each, five times as finely as the plan does. The test waits for the wandering homeowner to leave the throwing lane (a bag bonks them otherwise, fairly). `--toss-survey` prints a table of real and previewed catches from 2, 3, 4 and 6 m at 11 wind-ups. The `toss`, `toss_full` and `toss_close` scenarios (`net/dev/scenarios.gd`) put a bag in your hands 4 m from the bed (empty or full Chefty) or 2 m, right by the side wall, for recordings.
- **Load spots know their bag.** `truck_bed_load.gd hold_slot` keeps `{at, bag}`, and `unhold_slot` / the release act only for the bag that holds the spot, so a client's wrong guess can't un-hide a spot another bag is gliding into.

### 2026-11-02: Satellite Annex B is a set of cosmetic props driven by state calls; its statics bake into one mesh (R2 chunk 5)

- **What:** `levels/annex/annex_b.gd` (`AnnexB.build(parent, seed)`) builds World/BorderZone/AnnexSet on every peer, the same from the seed (`Constants.ANNEX_SET_SEED`, 27); `apply_run(set, looks)` dresses windows A-C for a run. The node contract is gap-specs nice_boys C9-C11: markers, Path3D routes, `Windows/A..D` (`props/office_props.gd` ServiceWindow), `FloorLines/A..C`, `HeadDesk/BigStamp`, the appliances, `CoSignSheet`, `OutgoingTray`, `Turnstile`, `LightRows/Row0..3`, `WallClock`, `NowServing`, `Dispenser`, `Stickies`, `Staff/{ClerkA..D, HeadNiceBoy, Greeter, Janitor}` (`npcs/clerk.gd`). `world.gd` calls it where `border_dressing.gd` was (deleted, with the sand surroundings; the zone now has a neighborhood's lawns, trees and houses round it, the street's corridor kept clear).
- **Every prop is local and cosmetic.** `set_state(state, at, args)` shows a state from server time `at` on, fast-forwarding a late call, and plays the prop's own sound on the frame its motion lands (the KA-CHUNK at the end of the slam, `BigStamp.slam_lands_after()` after the slam's `at`; a sound later than `Constants.BEAT_SKIP` is skipped, the beat clock's rule). A service window's parts (blinds, clock sign, stamp, lamp) each keep their own clock, so a lamp_off right after a blind_down never freezes the blinds halfway. Nothing in the set is replicated or decides anything: R2 chunk 6's Annex calls the same states on every peer from its host-ordered events. The clerks' `play(state, at, args)` is a pure function of the state and the time since `at` (poses written after the CartoonBody's own tick, `process_physics_priority` 10, so no `register_gesture` is needed); the host's "is he looking" never reads them. Clerks are dressed here, not through `npcs/crew_member.gd`, so the office never loads the crew's networked script.
- **Budgets:** `Constants.ANNEX_MAX_TRIS` 60,000 and `ANNEX_MAX_DRAWS` 60 for the whole set, staff aside (they are characters). Static looks carry meta `bake` (glass panes `bake_glass`) and `AnnexB._bake` merges them into the Furniture and Glass meshes after the build; the room measures about 10,300 triangles and 56 draw calls at rest, 57 in the busiest pose chunk 6 drives (every window closed and on a break, the tray receiving). To hold that: a window's dead lamp is baked and the lit bulb (its own mesh) is drawn only while it serves, and the clock sign is one quad whose card and words are one SubViewport-printed texture (`OfficeProps.card_texture`), not a mesh plus a Label3D. `tools/annex_check.gd` (run by `tools/mesh_check.gd`, the meshcheck CI step) checks the contract, the budgets at rest and busy, that the closed turnstile seals its passage from the floor to the lintel (its collider runs that high: a jump with a mid-air crouch cleared the waist-high arms) and opens clear, a capsule flood fill from the curb to every place someone stands (and that the clerks' side is closed), camera anchors outside colliders, every sign against the guardrail, determinism, and that Form 27-B held by someone else sits in their left hand. A peer with no camera (a headless host, a bot) never runs the staff (they'd pose every frame for nobody); past `ANNEX_CULL_DISTANCE` the set stops drawing too.
- **Interim:** until chunk 6 removes Border Return, its lanes still run on this pad, so `world.gd` turns the set's colliders off (`set_solid(false)`) while a `BorderReturn` node exists; with chunk 6 merged the set is solid.
- **Form 27-B and the receipt** are 3D props with SubViewport paper (`props/form_27b.gd`, `props/receipt_slip.gd`); in first person they ride the FirstPerson camera and draw over the world (no depth test, unshaded) so a counter or a wall never cuts them. Held by anyone else, or by you in over-the-shoulder view, they hang in the hand instead (the form in the left, from the left shoulder pivot an arm's length down, `FORM_HAND_DROP`; the slip in the right): `ReceiptSlip.show` and `Form.attach_auto` pick by `OfficeProps.sees_first_person(player)` (the owner's `CameraRig.first_person`), once, when shown. The receipt prints its total line big by index, not by its words. The icons are imported for 3D; the paper draws decompressed copies (the compressed ones drew as blank squares on a 2D canvas).
- **Music:** `annex_hold` ("Please Hold") is `escape_run` re-orchestrated with the same tempo, form and bars; tracks that share a `sync` name in `audio/music_tracks.gd` switch on the next bar line at the same bar. `pick_mood` maps BORDER to `annex_hold`, or `escape` while the group `annex` node's `urgent()` is true. New director hooks (C15): `duck`, `request_section` (`MusicTracks.SECTIONS`: the chase's "hide!" bars 28-36), `set_ambience` (`van_cab` muffles the music at 1.2 kHz and asks the world's Ambience for the cab's radio), `slowdown`; all logged in `MusicDirector.hook_log`. The bar-line cut's arithmetic (`MusicTracks.bar_switch`: wait and seek, file time with the encode lead, the last bar wrapping to the loop) and the section hold's (`MusicTracks.section_seek`) are pure static functions, so `--audio-test`'s `annex_music` checks them at set playback positions (a dry run never plays or seeks). `audio/cue_hooks.gd` plays Busted `CATCH_BARK_AT` after the local GOTCHA (or `peer_caught` without an Encounter) and Made It Back on `rider_through_turnstile`; the old TRANSIT trigger stays only while the run director has no `peer_caught` signal (until chunk 2), so Busted never goes silent in between. Still interim until chunk 6: the legacy Border Return crossing (Zone.BORDER) hears the lobby muzak and the office bed, not the escape cue; merge chunks 2 and 6 with this one. `audio/ambience.gd` (now in group `ambience`) plays the office bed in the BORDER zone.
- **Sounds:** `tools/sfx_r2_annex.py` (imports `make_sfx` as its base) makes the chunk's C17 ids plus `office_phone`; registered under `# ---- R2:5` in `audio/sound_bank.gd`.
- **For chunk 6:** call a clerk's `play()` directly (`clerk.play(&"stamp", at)`), not `call("play", &"...")`: `--audio-test`'s source scan reads `call("play", "<literal>")` as a sound id.
- **Networking:** none. No RPC, replicated property, save format, or yard or task generation changed.

### 2026-11-02: The stage: one extra camera, staged bodies, beats, one caption stack, an iris (R2 chunk 1)

- **Why:** Taylan's 0.4.5 playtest: the catch had no animation, hiding froze the camera (the first-person camera lives under `Visual`, which hiding hid), a cut to the border came without cover, and raid text piled up over the goal line. R2 (gap-specs/nice_boys.json) rebuilds the encounter in six parallel chunks; this one is the seams they all share. The contracts (C1-C5) are on the local branch `r2-contracts`; chunks 2-6 `git merge --no-edit r2-contracts` first.
- **StageCam** (`player/stage_cam.gd`, `CameraRig/StageCam`, owner only) is the only extra camera. A stack of shots keyed by id, the highest priority (newest on ties) on top; a shot is `{anchor, offset, blend_in, blend_out, yaw_clamp, pitch_clamp, orbit, spring, fov, lag, priority}` (plus an optional `hide_self` for an eye inside your own head). It is `top_level`, interpolation off, and updated every rendered frame from the anchor's `get_global_transform_interpolated()`, so it never sits under a node whose visibility toggles and a cut never smears. It `make_current()`s in the same call that takes current from the old camera, and popping the last shot blends back to `CameraRig.current_base_camera()` and hands current back with one `make_current()`: exactly one current camera every frame. Blends are a cubic ease-out from wherever the view was really drawn. While it's live, `CameraRig.toggle()` only changes which camera it hands back to. Mouse look goes to the top shot (`player._input`), clamped by its `yaw_clamp`/`pitch_clamp` or orbiting with `orbit`. Ids and priorities in use: hide_peek/hide_orbit 20, van_seat 30, annex_walk 35, escort 40, gotcha 50, freshener 60. `camera_rig.set_active` stays until chunk 6 deletes its last caller.
- **Staged bodies** (`player/stage_body.gd`, `Player/StageBody`, every peer): `set_staged(anchor, offset, blend, pose)` is called on every peer from the caller's reliable RPC, and every peer places the body from the same anchor: the owner moves the CharacterBody3D on physics ticks (priority 110, after the player and the anchors); other peers tell the network smoother to keep off (`is_riding_visual()` is true while staged) and place `Visual` every frame from the anchor's interpolated pose, interpolation off, so a synchronizer packet landing mid-frame can't shake it. The first placement blends from where the body was drawn; `clear_staged()` leaves the body where it is (the owner already holds that pose) and the smoother resumes from the next sample. The body stays upright whatever the anchor's tilt. Poses `stand`, `escorted` (worried face), `sit_buckled` (`cartoon_body.sit(1)`), `walk_behind`; the walk cycle comes from the body's real speed on every peer. The probe logs the largest per-tick step and steps over `STAGE_MAX_STEP_METERS` outside a closed iris.
- **Control locks:** `player.set_control_lock(reason, on)` stacks reasons; while any is on, the movement block returns early (one line at the top of the authority block) and `player._input` swallows (and `Input.action_release`s) every held-back action, so neither event handlers nor `Input` polling see them. What each reason lets through is data, `Constants.STAGE_LOCK_ALLOW`: `nice_cite` jump and look within 40 degrees, `escort` and `stage_walk` look, `van_seat` primary and look, `annex_closing` nothing. With several locks on, an action passes only if every lock allows it.
- **Beats** (`net/run_events/beat.gd`, static, preload it): every staged moment is `{kind, at, args}` sent reliably by the host with a server time; `lead_at()` (now + `BEAT_LEAD` 0.15 s) for beats without anticipation of their own, `late`, `progress`, `plays_sound` and `should_snap` (later than `BEAT_SKIP` 0.5 s: jump to the end pose, silently). Game logic runs on host timers from constants, never on animation-finished signals.
- **Gestures** (`player/cartoon_body.gd`, the only R2 edit there): `register_gesture(name, seconds, pose_fn(body, t01, delta))` is a class-wide registry, so each chunk registers its gestures from its own file; `has_gesture`; `pose_part(name)` returns the pivots gestures pose (hips, torso, head, face, mouth, arm_l/r, leg_l/r, eye_l/r, brow_l/r, clipboard, megaphone, prop_hand); `set_anim_speed(scale)` is per character (0 holds the walk, gesture and face for a hit-stop; never `Engine.time_scale`). `gesture()` on an unregistered name is now a no-op with one warning (it used to play 1.5 s of nothing).
- **Iris** (`ui/iris.gd`, group `iris`, owner-local): `close(at, seconds, shape)` with `&"seal"` (a Daisy Seal rosette hole closing to a little daisy, drawn by a shader from `props/nice_seal.gd`'s colors and scallop), `&"door"` (a door swinging shut) or `&"fade"`; `open(at, seconds)` never sooner than `IRIS_HOLD_MIN` after it shut; `is_closed()`, `closed_since()`. Its own CanvasLayer over the HUD, dropped under the pause buttons and Settings while they're up. Plays `iris_swish` (`tools/sfx_r2_stage.py`, which imports `make_sfx` as a library; `tools/make_sfx.py` itself is untouched by chunks 1-5).
- **Caption stack** (`ui/caption_stack.gd`, group `caption_stack`): `push(kind, text, seconds, priority) -> id`, `drop(id)`. One centered column stacked upward from just above the job goal line, as wide as fits between the left-hand HUD (job panel, heat meter), and moved up over anything it would still cover; at most `CAPTION_STACK_MAX` (3) lines, the rest wait (highest priority first) and their time runs only while they show. every kind (`teammate`, `radio`, `quip`, `subtitle`) follows Settings captions on/off, as critic.md C19 requires (review fix: the first build let `teammate` lines ignore it), like every caption since settings:4. All are sized by `caption_scale()` with the caption backdrop. The run cues' radio and all-clear lines and the raid quips (Q-005, Q-009, Q-006) go through it; other quips keep their bottom-left caption.
- **Guardrail:** a `custody` family (detain*, detention, custody, holding cells, booking, mugshots, fingerprints, inmates, jail*, prison*, cell blocks, perp walks, processing centers or facilities) and courtroom bail (posted bail, bail money, on bail, ...). Bail is not banned as a bare word, because Chefty's "Bail out!" prompt is the jump-out-of-a-moving-truck kind.
- **After the first recordings (same chunk):** a staged body's blend eases in and out (a cubic ease-out started at full speed and read as a pop); released, other peers ease the Visual back onto the network pose over `STAGE_RELEASE_BLEND` (0.3 s) instead of jumping back along the path by the link's delay; StageCam skips your own head and body while its eye is within 0.6 m of your head (a blend out of first person starts inside it); caption lines live in clipping slots that open and close (`GROW_SECONDS`), so lines never overlap mid-move, and the stack's width only keeps clear of the side columns beside its band (the job panel and heat meter), its whole column gliding up over the bottom-left quip caption when one appears. The stage test's anchor is a pure function of server time, so a client hitch moves it a long way in one tick; the test counts those as anchor hitches and fails only on body steps its anchor didn't make (one run saw a 3.4 m anchor jump on a loaded machine about 6 s after join; worth watching for chunk 4's time-driven van legs).
- **Test:** `--stage-test` (`net/dev/stage_test.gd`, port 7826, host plus a Target client, `Extra = '--sim-lag=150 --stage-test'` so both run it): the host stages the client's body on a moving anchor with one reliable RPC (within 0.05 m of the anchor on both peers after the blend, no unexplained step over 0.5 m, released with no slide on the owner and no frame step over 0.12 m on the host); StageCam pushes, priorities, an orbit with clamped look, a toggle while live, pops, cuts and a freed anchor with exactly one of the player's cameras current (and never none) on every frame; control locks and their allowances; registered gestures, an unknown name ignored, `set_anim_speed(0)` holding the body; the iris's times and the hold; the caption stack at caption sizes 1.0 and 1.3 (the largest Settings offers; the spec's 1.5 doesn't exist) against the goal line, job panel and heat meter, and captions off; the goal line in phases 1, 2 and 3. The client reports its half by RPC; CI needs both `stage_test.ok`. The probe gains `stage_cam`, `stage_body` and `stage_test`. `settings_test` reads the radio caption from the stack now, and `cast_test`'s CUE_KINDS drops `arrived`.
- **balance:E0** (pulled forward from balance:E): `Economy.run_roll_chance` spreads BAL-14's per-lot chance (now `run_lot_chance`) over a lot's rolls, `1 - (1 - p_lot)^(HEAT_ROLL_SECONDS / (RAID_LOT_MINUTES * 60))`; mirrored in `tools/balance_sim.py` with `RAID_LOT_MINUTES` in its constants list, `balance_test` grid cases for heat 0/50/94/100 x lots 0/3/10 and an invariant that a lot's rolls compound to the per-lot chance. The raid model's `border_return_minutes` is now `transport_minutes` + `annex_minutes`. Numbers in docs/BALANCE.md.
- **Review fixes (same chunk):**
  - A control lock holds its actions back in `player._input` only while the mouse is captured and no window is open (`UIRoot.any_window_open`); otherwise the GUI gets every click and key first (left click is `primary`, so the pause menu, phone and shop were dead during `nice_cite`), and `_unhandled_input` still drops what the lock holds.
  - The director's cohort is right on clients too: `release_peer` runs only on the host, so a client lets a cohort member go when the replicated zone brings them back from TRANSIT or BORDER (no new RPC). "All clear" compares `director.cohort_id()` from the NICE BOYS' arrival with its value when they leave, not the cohort's size (a teammate still at the crossing from an earlier raid is in it).
  - StageBody keeps an `_active` flag apart from `_anchor`, so an anchor freed under a staged body (a van left) still gets a full release: standing pose, the collision exception dropped (by RID when the carrier is gone), and on other peers the release ease and interpolation mode back.
  - StageCam honors `CameraRig.is_active()`: while the rig is off (the Border Return camera, `set_crossing_view`) a shot never `make_current()`s, pushed or popped, and the rig takes the view back when switched on again.
  - The stage test compares the peers for real: the client's own staged body against the host's anchor at the same wall-clock moment (both run on one machine), within 0.05 m; every step over `STAGE_MAX_STEP_METERS` fails, anchor jumps included; it starts only after the client's clock has settled (ping under 0.6 s, offset still for 1.5 s). New checks: a freed anchor, a click through a lock, a shot under an inactive rig.
  - Q-006 (the real-name "They took ...") honors its row's `max_session` again.
  - **Open, for the net lane and R2 chunk 4:** a client's server time can snap by a second or more a few seconds after joining. Pings sent during the join's loading stall come back with a 2.4 s round trip, the estimate is built from them, and the first good ping snaps it (`clock_sync.gd`, `SNAP_SECONDS`). Anything driven purely by server time (a van pose) jumps with it. The stage test now waits it out instead of excusing it.
- **Networking:** none in this chunk. StageCam, the iris and the caption stack are local; StageBody runs from other chunks' RPCs. No RPC, replicated property, save format or yard/task generation changed here.

### 2026-11-02: The catch is the Encounter's; barks are one host RPC; Phase.DEPARTING (R2 chunk 2)
- **Why:** Taylan's 0.4.5 playtest: "They just interact with you, theres no animation, no gotcha, suddenly you're just in a van." The catch was a distance check and a 3 s batch.
- **The Encounter** (`net/run_events/encounter.gd`, `World/RunDirector/Encounter`, group `nice_encounter`, made by the director on every peer so catch RPCs never ride on spawned crew) owns each caught player's stage: NONE, WINDUP, GOTCHA, CITE, ESCORT, BOARDING, SEATED, RIDING, ANNEX, RETURNING, FREE. Other chunks move a player on with `host_set_stage`; frees go through `host_free(peer, by_peer, cause)`. Every staged moment is `{stage, at, args}`, played from server time `at` on every peer; the host decides with timers from constants, never animation signals.
- **The lunge is host-judged with lead.** `_rpc_windup(grab_id, crew, peer, from, dir, contact_at, from_spot, travel)` goes out with `contact_at = now + CREW_LUNGE_WINDUP + min(rtt/2, CREW_LUNGE_WINDUP_LAG_MAX)`, so at 150 ms every peer sees the whole wind-up; the verdict at `contact_at` is the host's: your chest against the path every peer draws the hand along (from his start to the body's `travel` plus `CREW_LUNGE_ARM`), within `CREW_GRAB_REACH` plus `CREW_GRAB_SLACK`. So a target who ran into the lunge is caught, and one who stepped back past the drawn hand is not. No client reports hits. A whiff's RPC carries the peer and the crew index (`_rpc_whiff(grab_id, peer, crew, at)`): the host has already dropped the grab when it plays locally, and a late joiner may never have had it. The hit-stop runs `CATCH_HITSTOP` from when each peer hears of the catch (capped at `at + CATCH_HITSTOP + BEAT_SKIP`), so a lagging peer still sees the freeze in full, together with the slap.
- **NICE BOYS are bodies, the brain decides.** `npcs/crew_member.gd` steers (accel, turn rate), senses (cone, near sense, hearing via `heat.gd`'s per-player noise) and shows its replicated `mode`/`mode_at`/`mode_peer`/`mode_arg` (Synchronizer, ON_CHANGE, in the spawn state). `npcs/crew_brain.gd` (host RefCounted) picks modes. Poses are `npcs/crew_gestures.gd`, an overlay node that runs after the CartoonBody each tick (also registered through chunk 1's `register_gesture`).
- **Placed, not smoothed.** Seated in the van, hopping in or out, winding up, lunging, in the gotcha and the cite, every peer places the NICE BOY from the same data (the van's pose, the RPC's args); the host moves the real body on physics ticks and a client draws its `Visual` (top level, interpolation off) every frame, so the synchronizer moving the body underneath can't drag it. A client holds the end of a hop until the network catches up, so the hand-back doesn't pop.
- **Barks are one host RPC** (`crew_member._rpc_bark(kind, index)`, cast:6 absorbed): the host picks kind and line (with a per-crew gap and first delay), every peer shows the same line. The gotcha's line is played from the catch's own timeline (no RPC), so it lands on the same frame everywhere.
- **Director:** `enum Phase { QUIET, WARNING, CHASE, DEPARTING }` (DEPARTING appended). The crew is made at WARNING, seated (`shuttle.host_spawn_crew` when chunk 4's Shuttle exists). The 3 s capture batch, `_rpc_cohort` and `CAPTURE_BATCH_SECONDS` are gone; clients learn a catch from the Encounter's `_rpc_catch` at once (`director.mark_caught`). The van hold (`VAN_HOLD_SECONDS`/`VAN_HOLD_MAX`), the recall (`CREW_RECALL_MAX`), `HEAT_AFTER_EVADED`, the escape XP, `last_raid_caught()`, `loudest_peer()`, late-join `_rpc_request_state`/`_rpc_state` with the Encounter's (and the Shuttle's and Annex's) snapshots, `dev_reset()` for scenarios.
- **Without the Shuttle** (until chunk 4 merges) the cast:5 van that pulls up (`van_arrival.gd`, parked through CHASE and DEPARTING, leaving only at QUIET) carries the seated crew (`Constants.CREW_VAN_SEATS`), escorts end beside its open side, and the old ride (`_rpc_legacy_ride`, `Rides/Van`) takes the riders to the border. The integrator drops that path with chunk 4.
- **Itemized row:** `hotbar.host_empty_pockets(peer, "itemized", origin, dir)` pops the thing in hand, then each pocket, every `CATCH_ITEM_GAP`, in an arc onto a row `CATCH_ROW_SPACING` apart; `_rpc_itemized(at, index)` plays the rising tick on every peer. No toast.
- Rescues and the escape XP use Campaign's `add_rescue` / `award_escape` when chunk 6 lands; until then `add_xp` plus the day tally `rescues`. `encounter_test` checks the tally and the XP strictly today and turns those checks into notes once chunk 6's methods exist (chunk 6 makes them strict against its own tally).
- **Backstops:** an escort still walking `CREW_ESCORT_GIVE_UP` s past `CREW_ESCORT_MAX` (pinned on geometry, the door off the navmesh) hands the player to the door from where they are, so the raid can't hang in DEPARTING. The brain's investigation timer belongs to the investigation's `mode_at`, so one cut short by a sighting or re-targeted by another can doesn't leave a stale timer.
- **Smoothed turns:** `NetSmoother` records a snapshot when the body moved *or turned*. It used to skip snapshots with an unchanged position, so a NICE BOY turning on the spot (spotting you, standing to inspect) kept his old facing on clients until he walked off, then swung up to 1.5 rad in one frame. `encounter_test` now measures every NICE BOY's per-tick yaw from outside the steering, on the host (at most `CREW_TURN_RATE`) and on the client (at most three ticks' worth: frames are uneven and loss bunches a little).

### 2026-11-02: One van per trip, its pose a pure function of planned legs; never faded (R2 chunk 4)

- **Why:** Taylan called the old van unfinished: it popped from the arrival to a separate ride (`Rides/Van`, `setup_as_van`), flew to the border along a lifted curve, and nobody saw the crew get in or out. R2's Courtesy Shuttle (gap-specs/nice_boys.json chunk 4) replaces both with one van per trip.
- **`net/run_events/shuttle.gd`** (`World/RunDirector/Shuttle`, group `nice_shuttle`; contract C12) holds trips. A trip is a list of legs `{k, t, ...}`: a moving leg carries its route (ground points the host checked, swerved round trees, cars and props) and a speed profile (v0 plus constant-acceleration segments); a standing leg carries its pose. **The van's pose is `leg_pose(legs, Net.server_time())` on every peer**, so nothing streams positions; the host sends the whole list (`_rpc_plan`) whenever it grows, each new leg starting `VAN_PLAN_LEAD` ahead, so a 150 ms client never sees a leg start late or rewind. `--shuttle-test` checks host and client poses at the same server times on every leg.
- **Seats are staged bodies** (nice_boys:1's `player.set_staged`): every peer places riders from per-rider anchors under the van that the shuttle animates (the walk or step up, the seat, the knock lean, the step down), the owner gets `stage_push(&"van_seat")` (with `hide_self`) and `set_control_lock(&"van_seat")`. Without staging (before r2-contracts) the shuttle joins `rides` and seats them through `seat_of`/`seat_transform`. The crew ride the same way through `crew.set_staged` (nice_boys:2); until then a fallback here turns the host crew's brain off and draws client crew from the interpolated anchors.
- **The driver is the van's own** local, cosmetic NICE BOY (never replicated, never leaves the wheel), not a crew member: the spec's "lowest-index crew not escorting" would have needed the crew brain to walk someone back to the cab, and a driver must be in the seat in every frame. The head NICE BOY rides shotgun instead. The mirror the driver checks is the door-side one (`Shell/MirrorR`, the spec's MirrorL), so a rescuer at the door can see it glint.
- **Never faded:** each peer hides its copy only when its own camera can't see it (frustum, a ray, or past `VAN_HIDE_METERS`) and the leg allows it; a leg that jumps (lot to Annex) is drawn there only when nobody here sees the van, else the van lingers where it was until they look away. So the host moves riders' zones once their iris has closed and the van has reached its route end out of the yard's sight (or `VAN_HIDDEN_CAP` later), and observers still never see it vanish.
- **Zones:** riders stay `LOT` until the zone move behind the iris (`Net.move_player_to_zone(peer, BORDER, &"", false)`: a relabel, the body is staged); `TRANSIT` is no longer used by the raid. At Annex B's curb the shuttle calls the `annex` group's `begin(trip_id, riders, at)` (nice_boys:6), or, without it, releases the riders and calls `world.on_ride_arrived(BORDER)` so Border Return still runs.
- **The legacy yard's pad walls** keep people within x +-20 of the yard: the van parks at least `VAN_PAD_INSET` inside, so its door and the curb riders step onto are walkable. It always parks facing down the street (-x), open side to the yard; it never comes in from the other end (that would need a U-turn).
- **Cross-chunk:** the pre-R2 director keeps working: `van_arrival.gd` is a one-line shim (`extends shuttle.gd`, renamed `Shuttle` in `_enter_tree`), `_build_van` is gone, `van_park_point()` and `van_occupied()` ask the shuttle, and without nice_boys:2's director (`host_begin_departure`) or Encounter the shuttle spawns the crew seated at the warning, seats whoever the cohort lists, and departs on its own. With them, the director calls `host_spawn_crew`, `host_crew_board`, `host_board` (from the Encounter) and `host_depart`; `is_gone()` tells it when to free the crew. `ride.gd` lost `setup_as_van` and its van branches, and `_rpc_arrived` now clears only players riding that ride.
- **Test:** `--shuttle-test` (`net/dev/shuttle_test.gd`, port 7829, `Extra = '--sim-lag=150 --shuttle-test'`: both peers run the node). Smoke's van check is now seats == boarded on both peers. Scenarios: `van_seated`, `van_unbuckle` (needs -Observer), `van_ride` (seated, leaving in 2 s), `van_straggler`, `dropoff`.
- **Review fixes (same chunk):** the legacy Annex hand-off unstages riders on every peer before Border Return teleports them (`_rpc_release_riders(trip, at, handed_on)`: `handed_on` only when the Annex re-stages them from the seat anchors), or the anchor held them at the curb and the clamp dropped them on the lane's exit; `StageBody` now really lets go when its anchor is freed (it used to return early and leave a remote visual uninterpolated). The host forgets an ended trip only once its own copy of the van is gone, and a late joiner's snapshot updates trips a plan already built instead of orphaning their vans. A pull from the curb (or a drive-on) plays `van_pull`, the putt-putt alone, not the old `van` one-shot with its door bonk. The drop approach is at least `RETURN_PULL_UP` long (a cut-short route brakes all the way), and a knock is heard within `VAN_KNOCK_HEAR`. `--shuttle-test` adds `knock` and `rescue` (both from the client's own Shuttle across the lag), `border` (the client in the cohort reaches its lane start a second after Border Return begins) and `driveon`.

### 2026-11-02: Hiding hides the drawn body, not Visual; searching is suspicion with a telegraphed inspection (R2 chunk 3)

- **Why:** the 0.4.5 recordings: hiding froze the first-person view (`set_hidden_in` hid `$Visual`, and `FirstPerson` lives at `Visual/Head/FirstPerson` with physics interpolation on), the body moved 1.4 m in one frame in and out, and a NICE BOY who saw you go in always took you while one who didn't never looked.
- **The freeze, at its cause:** nothing hides `$Visual` any more. `player.set_body_drawn(on)` / `is_body_drawn()` show or hide `Visual/CartoonBody`, `Visual/Head/CartoonHead`, the first-person arms and the nameplate; the camera's branch stays in the tree. `character_life._shown` reads `is_body_drawn()`; a grep found no other reader of Visual's visibility. hotbar:D's stowed looks must use `is_hidden()` / `is_body_drawn()` too.
