- **The view while hidden** is a StageCam shot (R2 chunk 1, priority 20): `&"hide_peek"` anchored at the spot's `peek_anchor(side, slot)` just outside the face you came in by (never inside geometry), `yaw_clamp` +-`HIDE_YAW_CLAMP`, `hide_self`; or `&"hide_orbit"` (orbit, spring) when the camera toggle says third person. The owner's `body_yaw`/`head_pitch` follow StageCam's `look_angles()`, so the replicated look drives the tells. The framing (leaves, lid and rim, boards, pile leaves) is a 2D `player/hide_frame.gd` overlay on a CanvasLayer under the HUD, faded with the shot.
- **Owner-predicted moves** (`player/hide_view.gd`, child "HideView", made on first use): dive, climb-out, bonk, lift and pile pop run on the owning peer's physics ticks along a ballistic arc; the player's movement branch and fall tracking stand aside while one runs. A host answer that changes the side or slot mid-move re-aims from where the body is (no jump). Other peers see the replicated body, play the same gesture by name, and stop drawing it once it reaches inside.
- **Hide spot protocol** (`net/components/hide_spot.gd`, host-authoritative): `_rpc_set_occupants(peers, entry_sides, slots, at)` replaces `_rpc_set_occupant` (each occupant's place is replicated and kept while others come and go; a newcomer gets `free_slot()`, the lowest free place, so two hiders never share a shed's knot hole); a removed occupant (a disconnect) also lets go of the look's peek and glint; `_rpc_exit_side(peer, side, at)` (the owner proposes the side it faces with `_rpc_request_exit_toward(side)`, the host sweeps a capsule there and picks the nearest clear side); `_rpc_refuse(reason)` to the asker; `_rpc_pull(peer, to, at)`; `_rpc_inspect(crew_index, at, phase)`; `_rpc_notice(count)`. `occupant` (the first occupant) stays for older callers. Capacity per kind (`HIDE_SHED_CAPACITY` 2). Reach is to the box's nearest edge. A shed is offered and accepted only from before its door (`can_enter_from(at)`; the host, a moment behind, refuses only the back half), and a step from off to one side goes by the doorstep, so nobody walks through a wall.
- **Suspicion and inspection** (contract C14): `suspicion()`, `inspect_score(from, last_seen)` (< 0 = skip), `inspect_point()`, `host_inspect_begin(crew)`, `host_inspect_resolve(crew) -> {outcome, peer}`, `host_inspect_cancel(crew)` (a poke cut short: no outcome, no sticker), `occupants()`, `peek_anchor()` on hide spots and, for the pile, on `player.gd` (host only; the pile's anchor is made on every peer). A poke never resolved or cancelled stops holding the spot `HIDE_INSPECT_STALE_SLACK` after it should have ended, or as soon as the NICE BOY is freed. Found requires `director.can_capture`. Without the R2 Encounter (no `nice_encounter` group node) a found hider goes to `director.note_capture` after the lift; with it, the crew brain hands them to `Encounter.host_windup(crew, peer, true)`. The old crew's `was_seen_entering()` now means "suspicion at the found mark", and `pull_out()` respects immunity.
- **Player:** replicated `peeking` (owner -> all, player.tscn property 15); `_rpc_pile_popped`, `_rpc_pile_inspect`, `_rpc_pile_pulled`. The pile mound is a local mesh under the drawn Visual (smoothed), shown once the dive has had time to land. A pile that arrives with the body's spawn (a late joiner) shows the mound at once and replays no cannonball. In the third-person orbit the hider's `body_yaw` follows the orbit's yaw within `HIDE_YAW_CLAMP`, so a hard turn shakes a hedge there too.
- **Sound:** `tools/sfx_r2_hide.py` (hedge_rustle x3, peek_rustle x2, bin_lid, bin_clang, shed_door, pile_dive, pile_pop, hide_bonk, notice_slap). While hidden the owner's Sfx bus gets a `HIDE_MUFFLE_HZ` low-pass and Music is muffled (re-asserted each frame so the phone or pause closing doesn't undo it); a hider's voice goes through a runtime `VoiceMuffled` bus (low-pass, then Voice) on every peer. `HideView.clear_bus_for(tree, at)` gives the Chatter bus for a sound within `HIDE_CLEAR_CREW_METERS` of a hidden local player, for the crew's footsteps (R2 chunk 2 can route `crew_step` through it).
- **Fix outside the chunk:** `net/components/net_smoother.gd` kept extrapolating a remote body past where it stopped (it recorded no sample while the body stood still, so the last motion's velocity carried the visual up to 0.1 s on: a dive left the body 0.54 m underground and the climb-out popped). It now records one copy where it stopped once the origin has been unchanged for `STOP_AFTER_SECONDS` (0.07 s: past the 0.033 s sync interval with room for jitter, inside the 0.1 s render delay so the visual never extrapolates past a stop). Not on the first unchanged tick: physics runs at 60 Hz and updates arrive at 30 Hz, so that copied every update and rendered every remote body at alternating still and double speed. Hitch stats are measured between updates, never to a stop copy. `equipment/rake.gd` does nothing while hidden (right click inside a hedge peeked and bagged the pile in front of it).
- **Test:** `--hide-test` (`net/dev/hide_test.gd`, port 7828, host + Target, both run it; also passes at `--sim-lag=150`).
- **Networking:** RPCs and a replicated property changed (above); the integrator bumps BUILD_VERSION. No save format or yard/task generation change.

### 2026-11-02: Satellite Annex B replaces Border Return (R2 chunk 6)

Decided by the agent building gap-specs/nice_boys.json chunk 6 (Taylan's creative freedom; the design is docs/GAME.md "Form 27-B, in Triplicate").

- **Border Return is deleted:** `net/border_return.gd`, `border/`, the crossing hunks in `player/player.gd` (`play_throwback`, `set_crossing_view`, `set_crossing_bounds`, the lane-relative input and the bounds clamp), its strings, Q-010 and the `border_fail` trigger, `BORDER_CROSSING/STAGING/THROWBACK_SECONDS`, the `crossing_go` and `crossing_bonk` sounds, the Lane markers in `world.tscn`, and the leftover hooks in `ui/quip_player.gd` and `ui/ui_root.gd`. `Net.Zone.BORDER` now means Satellite Annex B. `camera_rig.set_active()` stays: `player.gd` turns the rig on for its owner with it and `player/seated.gd` turns it back on after a seat.
- **World/Annex** (`net/annex/annex.gd`, group `annex`, every peer): host-authoritative. Everything cosmetic is a pure function of (seed, begin time, ordered host overrides, server time): `annex_schedule.gd` generates each window's routine jointly (a window only goes off while another is open), with the never-wait guarantee checked by annex_test over 200 seeds. Overrides travel in the schedule's clock (seconds since the begin). Forms are plain data (`form_state.gd`) the host sends whole. The receipt is a pure function (`receipt_lines.gd`).
- **Other chunks only through guards:** the set, clerks, clipboard and slip (chunk 5) by `ResourceLoader.exists` + `load()`, with `annex_greybox.gd` / `annex_paper.gd` standing in under the same node names and calls; the Shuttle (chunk 4) by group `nice_shuttle` (without it, `world.on_ride_arrived(BORDER)` calls `begin()` and finishers go home by the host's teleport, and the old ride's van is hidden while the Annex is open); the Encounter (chunk 2) by group `nice_encounter` + `host_set_stage`; chunk 1's staged bodies, control locks, iris and caption stack by `has_method` / group.
- **Spawn markers under a zone:** `world.get_spawn_transform()` also finds a marker anywhere under the zone (the Annex's `AnnexCurb`, `AnnexSide`, `Forecourt`, `Threshold`), so `Net.move_player_to_zone(peer, BORDER, &"AnnexCurb")` works for the Shuttle too. A client's zone move arrives before `_rpc_begin`, so for BORDER it first asks the Annex to `ensure_set()` (the grey box would otherwise not exist yet there, and the client would land at Spawn0 in the middle of the room and slide out to the curb).
- **Reach and slack:** each spot's reach is measured to the feet locally (`ANNEX_COUNTER_REACH` 0.9 m is the spec's 0.6 m to the capsule edge); the host honours a client's ask up to `ANNEX_REACH_SLACK` (0.35 m) further, holds and the big stamp `ANNEX_HOLD_SLACK`, a put-back or a door `ANNEX_LOOSE_SLACK`, so 150 ms of travel never refuses someone standing on the spot. The spec's "host checks <= 0.6 m" is read as that capsule-edge distance plus the lag slack.
- **Finishing as a group:** riders through the turnstile wait (`ANNEX_FINISH_AS_GROUP`) until nobody is inside or walking in; the host re-checks every tick, so a last rider who disconnects or is put out at closing still releases them. A late joiner's snapshot carries the host's stages into `_rpc_begin_local` before any walk-in is queued, so only riders still walking are staged behind the Greeter. The punch card's visit count rides on the form (`form.visits`), since the crew save is the host's. Visitors go home with a relabel plus a host-to-owner placement (`_rpc_place`) at the door they used.
- **The turnstile** lets one player through by disabling its bar's shapes on that player's own peer only (each peer simulates only its own player). Collision exceptions weren't reliable here.
- **`player.carry_speed_scale()`** honours a `speed_share` meta set from outside (the big stamp's 70%).
- **Bodies dress from the name:** `player._dress_up()` waits (up to `DRESS_NAME_WAIT_SECONDS`) for the player's name before building the cartoon body, and dresses on `player_joined`, so every peer dresses a person alike (the 0.4.5 outfit mismatch at the Annex).
- **Save:** an additive `annex` section in `net/campaign.gd` (`visits` by player key, `best_s`), read with defaults; SAVE_VERSION stays 1. New Campaign API: `add_rescue`, `award_escape`, `note_annex_visit`, `annex_visits`, `note_annex_time`, `annex_best` (`add_rescue` awards `XP_RESCUE` once R2 chunk 2's constant exists).
- **Tests:** `--annex-test` (port 7830, `Extra = '--sim-lag=150'`); smoke's hazard probe is now the schedule fingerprint; playtest_tools runs every scenario that has a `_reached_<name>()`; the run-event soak checks every caught bot gets home and at least 80% finish before closing (`ANNEX_CAP_SECONDS_SOAK` 50 under `--run-event-at`); telemetry `mark_annex_start/finish`.
- **BUILD_VERSION:** needs a bump at the merge (new RPCs, the smoke probe RPCs renamed, the save section).

### 2026-11-03: R2 integration: where the catch, the Shuttle and Annex B meet (wave R2 merge)

Decided by the R2 integrator. Chunks 2, 4 and 6 were each built against stand-ins for the others; merged, these seams needed joining (all host-authoritative as before; no new RPC):
- **Crew modes follow the van.** The Courtesy Shuttle sets each NICE BOY's replicated `mode` from his place in the van (`shuttle._sync_crew_mode`): SEATED in a seat, HOP_OUT on the way out, NOISE on the curb (RECALL if the recall was called meanwhile), HOP_IN climbing back in or onto the bumper. `crew_member` adds nothing of the fallback van's own on top when staged on the Shuttle's anchors (no hop path, hop clock, hop gesture or hop thump, no fallback-seat staging). On the recall, crew whose hop hasn't started stay seated (`host_cancel_hops`). The director's departure counted nobody aboard before.
- **The director with a Shuttle:** `_rpc_legacy_ride` and the Rides/Van fallback are gone (chunk 2 asked for it once chunk 4 merged). Between raids `_step_quiet` lets a forced catch's van go once its riders sit (it departed every tick and resent QUIET) and walks stragglers to the creeping van. `host_board` with no raid van at the curb calls one up (they walk in once it parks); someone already aboard keeps the seat.
- **"All clear" on clients:** `director.cohort_id()` goes up on every catch on every peer (note_capture on the host, mark_caught from the Encounter's RPC on clients; carried in `_rpc_state`); run_cues compares it alone (`last_raid_caught()` is the host's count).
- **The van at Annex B** parks along the street with its door step on the `AnnexCurb` marker (`VAN_ANNEX_DOOR_TO_CURB`), not on the marker's facing (it rolled in nose-first through the Annex's front windows). Riders unbuckle and step down to the curb as at a drop-off; `Annex.begin` gets the time the last one is down, and the walk-in line starts as far up the walk as the deepest rider stands behind the Greeter, so nobody is staged inside the van's side. The van's heading looks `VAN_HEADING_LOOK` (one route step) either way, so swerves turn smoothly.
- **Annex with chunk 5's paper:** Form27B's setters (`set_after_hours`, `set_carbons_from`, `attach_auto`; hidden in the tray), and the static `ReceiptSlip.show()`. A bell rung while the clerk is shaking his head at your form (`PEER_FORM`) still sends him to his cabinet (chunk 5's bell sits on the counter).
- **Hiding with the R2 crew:** `hide_spot._seen_by_crew` takes chunk 2's `sees_point` when it says yes, else its own cone and a line that ignores the spot (sees_point's line ended on the spot's own surface, so a peek in plain view never raised suspicion).
- **NetSmoother** keeps both fixes: a snapshot counts when the body moved or turned (chunk 2), and one stop copy after it's unchanged for `STOP_AFTER_SECONDS` (chunk 3).
- **Name tags** fade aside while a NICE BOY barks (`CREW_TAG_HUSH_SECONDS`); the two printed over each other.
- **Tests:** encounter step 9 waits for the Shuttle to leave the yard's sight with the crew seated; shuttle_test's border step is the Annex hand-off; annex_test counts boarding the return van as going home; hide_test and the hiding scenarios take the crew off the van; playtest_tools expects cite_rescue and van_unbuckle to say they need a second player.
- **Not joined yet:** `crew_member.van_anchor()`'s Rides/Van branch, Encounter's SEATED-at-door fallback and `CREW_VAN_SEATS` are dead with the Shuttle present but kept; `camera_rig.set_active` still has callers.

### 2026-11-03: the toss through your own eyes, and the observer goes where the host goes (toss:polish)

- **Why:** build 0.4.8 recorded frame by frame: a first-person toss filled the screen with the bag for four frames (it rises from 0.4 m in front of your eyes), the carried bag was a sliver of rim under the held-item card, Chefty's Label3D (fixed world size) filled the top of the view from 2 m, and the observer stayed at the store (zone STORE) when a scenario moved the host to the yard.
- **First-person bag: a presentation layer on the look only.** `props/carry_view.gd`, a node net_prop.gd adds to a leaf bag the first time this peer holds it, runs after the prop (`process_physics_priority` 115 > 110) and moves only `Visual/Look`, only on the holder's own peer, only while their first-person camera is current. The bag's body, its `Visual` node (which the client's settle and catch code read back), the host's flight and every other peer's view are untouched, so prediction and the settle still agree and nothing on the wire changed. Held: framed in view space (`FP_BAG_RIM`, pitch followed partly), the wind-up dips it. Released (`net_prop.fp_carried()` false: the host let go, or a client is predicting or settling its own toss): the offset from where it was drawn eases out over `FP_THROW_EASE_SECONDS`, and while the bag's middle is up by the eye line its apparent width is capped (`FP_THROW_MAX_ANGLE`) by scaling it about its middle, which in perspective is the same picture as pushing it further away along the view ray but can never put it behind a wall. The cap is gone by `FP_THROW_SETTLE_DONE` of a settle, before the landing squash. `player/first_person_arms.gd` treats a bag no longer `fp_carried` as out of the hands (a client's `held_by` lags a round trip), adds a follow-through after a lob, stretches the arms to the framed rim, and leaves the wind-up shake to the bag.
- **Chefty's label:** `ui/near_label.gd` (a Label3D subclass) caps its on-screen height per line by drawing smaller pixels (a billboard ignores node scale) and fades it with `transparency` between `CHEFTY_LABEL_FULL_M` and `CHEFTY_LABEL_GONE_M`, measured from whichever camera draws. Used for Chefty in `chefty_drive.gd` and the legacy `ride.gd` (not the van's).
- **Eyes at rail height.** The bed's rails (1.65 m in bed space, about 1.55 m in the world) sit at a player's eye height (the first-person camera is at 1.58 m), so the bed is blind from anywhere on foot. Chefty is not resized (driving and art depend on its size); `ui/toss_arc.gd` draws the landing ring with no depth test when the toss is going in, and skips arc dots closer than `TOSS_ARC_NEAR_M` to the camera (the hop's climb past your face drew big blurry blobs).
- **Observer:** the host's `net/dev/remote_play.gd` (`_shadow_observers`) finds the observer by its name (`--name=Observer`, which `tools/play.ps1 -Observer` passes; dev only), keeps it in the host's zone with the game's own host-ordered teleport (LOT when the host is on the yard, else STORE: TRANSIT and BORDER have rosters of their own), puts it on the director's `_safe_until` list for good, and makes its body a ghost on the host (Visual hidden, collision off); the observer hides its own body too. It is also a **watcher** (`Net.set_watcher`, host-local, never on the wire, cleared when the peer leaves): `Net.players_in_zone` leaves watchers out, so it never boards Chefty home (`world._host_finish`'s `choose_party`), never keeps a raid rolling or the job board locked on its own, and `net_prop._long_toss_reaction` skips it as a witness (it would otherwise steal the thrower's brag from 20 m off at the lot spawn). Its own `Net.player_zone` still says LOT, which is what its HUD, cues and recorder read. Rejected: a name check in each roster (every new roster would have to know about the observer). Relabelling the zone without a teleport (`Net.set_player_zone`) was tried first and fails: player.gd keeps each player on its zone's pad and pulled the observer's body to the yard's spawn.
- **Networking, saves:** no RPC, replicated property, save format or yard/task generation change. The observer change uses existing RPCs (`move_player_to_zone`) from a dev-only node.
- **Test:** `--toss-test` gains H10: the host's bag was drawn in its hands, eight tosses and heaves left them, and no drawn jump over 0.35 m a tick. What the camera gets is measured off the drawn meshes, not off the cap's own formula (a review found the first version could not fail: it sampled only where the cap was fully on and re-derived the cap's angle from the assumed radius): `carry_view.gd seen_from` projects the look's mesh box through the first-person camera, cut at the near plane, every tick from release to landing at every height, as a share of the view. A thrown bag may never cover more than 1.1 times the screen one in your hands does (it leaves from there and rises a touch toward the middle of the view on the release ease), nor more than 0.2 of it once past the hand-off ease. Measured: held 0.44, thrown 0.44 to 0.45 (the release ticks), flight 0.13 on the host and 0.08 on the lagged client, which makes the same check of its own predicted tosses. With the cap switched off it fails on both (host thrown 1.0, in your face; client flight 0.22). The observer change was checked by hand: with `toss_close` and `toss` the observer is in LOT with its ghost body at the yard's spawn, its HUD is the yard's and its recording follows the host; with `caught` the head NICE BOY took the host, not the observer, and while the host rode the van the observer waited at the store and its camera kept following the host to the border.

### 2026-11-02: The windrow: a rake stroke gathers into a berm, as wide as the head (rakefeel:field, R1b)

- **Why:** R1 made the leaves fly, but watching it the integrator saw an eraser: one swing wiped a sharp ~3 m wedge almost at once, and the pushed leaves spread out instead of building up. Taylan: "I just want raking to be fun and satisfying." Real raking satisfies because you gather. Also closes OQ-R1-2 (the direction bias) and OQ-R1c (field width vs head).
- **The rule** (`LeafField._stroke_cells`, `_push_lane`, `_stroke_drag`; constants under `# ---- rakefeel` plus `RAKE_WIDTH` 3.2 -> 1.2, `HAND_RAKE_STRENGTH` 9 -> 0.8): each cell is on one lane parallel to the stroke's 8-way step. In each lane, the cells the tine line crossed (from the plant line `RAKE_FIELD_BACK` behind the origin, plus the drag, up to the bite's front, `RAKE_FIELD_BITE_FRONT`; full grip across the width, fading over `RAKE_FIELD_EDGE`, the edge catching only part of the lawn's litter and, over its inner half, all of a heap, `RAKE_FIELD_HEAP_GRIP`) give up all but a residue (at most 3, under `CLEAN_DENSITY`), front first, until the head holds `strength x RAKE_FIELD_LOAD` a lane; that load lands 60/30/10 in the cells just ahead of the tine line (`RAKE_FIELD_BERM`, the blower's `cells - RAKE_STROKE_CELLS` further), a full cell passing the rest on (`RAKE_FIELD_BERM_REACH`, as far in metres along a diagonal), and what still doesn't fit stays under the tines. Lanes never share a cell and each is read before it is written, so all 8 directions behave the same (0.4.5's in-place loop cascaded leaves across the disc toward +X/+Z only). Mass is conserved (off the grid the leaves leave the lot, as before).
- **The drag.** A stroke also sweeps the ground its tines crossed since the same raker's last stroke (`_stroke_drag`, up to `RAKE_FIELD_DRAG_MAX` 2.4 m; 0 after a turn, a step back, a side-step or a walk on farther than that), so the windrow rides along at any walking speed with no stripes, and standing still rakes nothing new. The host records it for every request it gets (refused ones too), the raking client for every request it sends (predicted or not), in the same order from the same float origins, so prediction and confirmation match with nothing new on the wire. Hired hands are tracked by actor. The pending ledger keeps `drag` and the rule's `bite` in each op's args; `--rake-net-test`'s reference copy tracks its own drag.
- **Bites:** a rake's plant bite (0) drives to 0.1 m past the origin and its mid-drive bite (1) to 0.45 m, so within a swing the berm moves with the tines. Hands, the blower and hired hands always push a whole drive (bite -1).
- **Tiers:** a better rake strokes wider by its head's ratio (`RAKE_HEAD_WIDTHS`, `LeafField.rake_head_scale` from the peer's unlocks, which every peer has); `Campaign.rake_multiplier` still raises strength, now the head's load.
- **Hired hands** walk `HIRE_STEP_AFTER_STROKE` (0.55 m, was 0.18 inline in `crew_hand.gd`) after each stroke so a pile still bags about 4,550 units (0.4.7: 4,430-4,550).
- **Leaf motion:** `LEAF_FLOW_MAX_DIST` 4.5 -> 6.5 m: a dragged stroke can carry a leaf from 2.65 m behind its point to its berm, and `--leaf-motion-test`'s synthetic load (six actors jumping 3 m between strokes) left those losses unmatched (765 units faded) at 4.5. `LeafFlow` already explains the rule exactly as line transport along the step; its comments still name `_apply_rake`.
- **Pace, measured** with `tools/rake_pace.gd` (new; a headless `--script` on a scratch field with the real rule, yards shaped like the lot generator's, seeds 777/1000/4242, x and z lanes; a scripted raker cleans to 92% in lanes from block edges into pile rows and bags them; lane spacing, block size, where to stop and bag spacing searched per rule; bag 1.5 s, hauling 4 s a bag; `--field=` and `--width=` run 0.4.7's rule from a copy for the baseline):

  | | 0.4.7 (3.2 m disc) | windrow (1.2 m) | ratio |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 2.5 m/s (bots crouch), rake + bag | 453 s | 739 s | 1.63 |
  | 2.5 m/s, with hauling | 822 s | 1,114 s | 1.36 |
  | 5 m/s (people walk), rake + bag | 422 s | 661 s | 1.57 |
  | 5 m/s, with hauling | 785 s | 995 s | 1.27 |
  | units a stroke, walking 2.5 / 5 m/s on a fresh lawn | 5,428 / 6,501 | 1,183 / 1,826 | |
  | one stroke standing, 8 ways (moved; mean carry) | 5,508-6,480; 2.3-2.8 m | 384 axis, 267 diagonal; 0.99 / 1.04 m | |
  | bare hands, units a second | 67 | 77 | |
  | a hired hand's pile | 4,434-4,554 | 4,561 (4,213-4,231 after the review's soft-edge fix; `HIRE_STEP_AFTER_STROKE` 0.45-0.65 all bag less) | |

  The integrator asked for the time to clean to stay within about 15%. It doesn't: raking a lot alone takes about 1.6x as long, a whole job about 1.3x. A stroke the width of the head cannot sweep the area of a 3.2 m disc at the same walking pace. Widening it doesn't get there either (`RAKE_WIDTH` 1.45, measured after the review fixes: 689 s / 1,003 s at 2.5 m/s, 542 s / 854 s at 5 m/s, so 1.52x / 1.22x and 1.29x / 1.09x: only the 5 m/s whole job is inside 15%), and it touches 2.45 m of lawn with a 0.95 m head, most of the way back to the eraser. The windrow at 1.2 m is kept and the pace is mirrored instead: `tools/balance/model.json` `leaf_rate_yard_per_min` is x0.79 on its rake share (the W4 rule), which fails four more tuning checks that `--baseline` excuses until balance:E. **This is not signed off:** the slower pace is a spec gap for Taylan or the integrator to accept before merging (GAME.md OQ-R1d), and balance:E owns the four tuning checks it fails (pressure_washer priced right at crew 1, days per tier at crew 4, pace Mulch Mule at crew 2, pace Hedge Hog at crew 3). Most of the extra time is bags, not strokes: the windrow's narrow piles bag about 3,500 units each at 1.2 m (`rake_s` is about 370 s of a 740 s job), so a bigger bag scoop or a pile-shaping change is the other lever besides `RAKE_WIDTH`.
- **Review fixes (same chunk):**
  - The drag is 0 when the raker moved more than `RAKE_FIELD_DRAG_MAX` forward since their last stroke (was: clamped to it). Walking on to bag, to the next lane, or sprinting (8 m/s puts 3.4 m between swings) lifts the head, so a first stroke after a pause never sweeps up to 1.15 m behind the raker's feet over ground the head never touched. Measured pace is unchanged (739.5 / 1,114.8 s and 660.4 / 993.7 s).
  - The soft edge pushes a heap with `grip x RAKE_FIELD_HEAP_GRIP` (2.0, at most whole), not wholly: the head's own berm still leaves no trail (the inner half of the edge pushes a heap whole), but a 200-unit strip 1 m off the line keeps 67% of itself instead of giving up 75%. `rake_field_rules.gd` checks it.
  - The berm passes on `RAKE_FIELD_BERM_REACH` cells along an axis and as far in metres along a diagonal (4 cells, 2.83 m; was 6 cells, 4.24 m), so the longest carry is 6.1 m along an axis and 5.9 m along a diagonal, inside `LEAF_FLOW_MAX_DIST` (6.5 m); before, a diagonal could carry 7.3 m and fade instead of fly.
  - `leaf_motion`'s `client_no_snaps`: the load step's synthetic rakers jump up to 7.5 m between strokes, which the old clamp turned into 2.4 m drags and long carries into berms that kept growing under homing leaves. With the drag fix alone, 1 of 3 runs beside three other tests still snapped once (2.2 cm, a hired-hand leaf homing 3.8 m onto a mound that kept growing); with the drag fix and the berm's reach in metres, 0 snaps in 9 runs at `-Jobs 2` beside rake_net, job and hire (and the rest of the chunk's tests). If it ever comes back, the mover side (`leaf_movers.gd`, rakefeel:look) is the place: a leaf still homing in its last `LATE_LAND_WINDOW` onto a slot that keeps rising. The golden is re-recorded for ops 37-38.
- **Tests:** `net/dev/rake_field_rules.gd` runs in `--rake-net-test` (port 7821, no new port) next to the golden: 8-way symmetry, the width, a 6 m run's berm growing every metre and riding just ahead of the tines with a clean lane behind and mass conserved, three runs making a pile you can dive into, one stroke through a drift capped at the head's load, a 16 m run's berm stopping growing, hands under 8% of the rake, the drag's rules. `net/dev/leaf_golden.gd` is re-recorded for the new rule on purpose, with ops 41-50 added (a walked run with both bites and the drag, The Leafinator's head, a weak hired hand filling its head); host, client and reference still agree byte for byte under 150 ms / 2% loss. `--job-test` rakes its pile in three lanes (one head-wide lane no longer clears a bag's radius).
- **Watched** with `tools/play.ps1` (`rake_fresh`, `rake_pile`, first person and observer, and `--sim-lag=150 --sim-loss=2`; no flags): a clean lane opens behind the rake and a heap builds in front of it as you walk; stopping leaves it as a pile.
- **Networking:** no RPC, replicated property, save format or yard/task generation changed (the drag and the bite-dependent reach come from requests both sides already have). The rule changed, so the golden changed.

### 2026-11-03: R1b integration (rakefeel field, look, sound)

- **Merged** in order: rakefeel:field (the windrow), rakefeel:look (knees, the planted lunge, the riding windrow), rakefeel:sound (sounds on the swing's own clock, the mix, the bag tie). Conflicts were only the append-only banners in `core/constants.gd`, `docs/GAME.md` and this file; both sides kept.
- **The planted back foot dragged.** After the look merge `--rake-stroke-test` failed its no-skating check every run (6.2 mm a tick against 3 mm), and the look worktree failed the same way, so it was not the merge: as the lunge came on, the back foot ran out of reach while the front foot was still finishing a shuffle step, and a foot could only start its step once both were down, so it dragged a tick. A foot about to be out of reach may now start its step once the other is `RAKE_STEP_OVERLAP` (0.75) through its own (`CartoonBody._stance_feet`). Measured slide 1.2-1.3 mm a tick over five runs.
- **The field's footprint vs the drawn head, left as is.** rakefeel:field placed its stroke 1.25..1.95 m out (the R1 head path) and said `RAKE_FIELD_BACK`/`RAKE_FIELD_BITE_FRONT` should follow if the head moved. rakefeel:look then drew the head 0.85..2.1 m out for others and 1.45..2.35 m in your own view (on purpose, to frame it over the hotbar). Moving the field to either path puts it off the other, and changes the golden, the pace and the balance mirror, so it stays between the two (comment in `constants.gd`). Taylan should judge it in first person and from the observer.
- **Sound on look's swing.** rakefeel:sound times every rake sound off `RAKE_SWING_PHASES`, so it takes rakefeel:look's longer drive (drive_end 0.74, flick_end 0.87) with no change; the merge kept look's phases, sound's `RAKE_CREW_BITES` [0.2] and sound's removal of `LEAF_SFX_FLICK_PHASE`. Two audio_test checks had the old phases baked in: `scrape_pitch` assumed a 0.6 s swing drives 0.3 s (it now reads the drive's share off the table), and `sync_ticks` read a play one tick late (it checks on the physics_frame signal, before that tick's flush), which only showed once the flick moved to 8.6 ticks out. The game's 0.6 s swing now plays the scrape at pitch 0.93 to fill its 0.32 s drive.
- **The hired hand's stroke** keeps both: sound's tempo on the event (0 works it out from skill) and field's drag since the hand's last stroke.
- **Watched together** (`tools/play.ps1 -Observer -Scenario rake_fresh`, holding the rake, then walking at full speed with it): no pop, snap or frozen-view flags in either view, even frame spacing. In first person a green patch opens under the tines while you stand, and a heap builds ahead of you as you walk on. From the observer a lane opens behind the raker with ragged edges, and the lunge and knees read. Rake sounds start 0-36 ms after the frame that shows them. From the observer, bite B's crackle and the flick still have no onset of their own (rakefeel:sound's known limit).
- **Full suite:** every R1b-touched test passes. encounter, shuttle, toss and drive failed under load (24 other Godot processes on the machine) with different checks each run. They failed the same way on the pre-R1b commit run side by side, and passed on rerun. The bot soak fails on voice packet loss (6.2-12.6% against 6%), and the pre-R1b commit fails it the same way (6.5-7.3%), so that is not R1b. No R1b chunk changed an RPC, a replicated property, the save format or yard/task generation, so `BUILD_VERSION` stays 0.4.9.

### 2026-11-03: The playtest tools judge the game, not themselves (R2b tools)

- **Why:** the R2 motion audit's recorders lost takes to the tools: a long take's `record_stop` stalled the host until the observer dropped ("Host left"), `audio.wav` ran up to 1.5 s short of its frames, `pile2_1p` became `rec_pile_2_1p`, scenarios started with the last moment's van seat, lines and stage shot, `cite_rescue` cited the parked observer or a friend the roaming crew had already taken, the lunge's wind-up came 3.9 s in, the spectator camera sat in trees, hedges, posts, the van and heads and lined the escorted pair up along its view, big `look` steps were flagged as camera snaps, and the agent's thinking time ate game time between commands.
- **Recorder** (`net/dev/recorder.gd`): each take is a `Take` object; frames go to disk on worker threads as before, and `stop()` only closes the take and hands it to a writer `Thread` (picture change via `Image.compute_image_metrics` on 64x36 copies made by the frame workers, the flags, the sheet pages on the worker pool, the filmstrip, events.json). `is_writing()` / `wait_written()`; remote play waits for the writer before it answers, while frames keep running. A new take may start while the last is written. Measured: a 224 s, 3,358-frame take was written 2.0 s after the stop with no peer dropped.
- **Audio:** an `AudioEffectCapture` on Master (4 s buffer) drained every frame and streamed to `audio.wav` as 32-bit float (format 3; `tools/playtest_audio.py` reads it and the old 16-bit files). The wav is kept on the wall clock: a deficit that lasts 0.4 s (an underrun: never mixed) is written as silence where it happened, frames the capture had to drop likewise, and mix that turns up after its gap was padded (only late) is dropped against that padding. `audio_padded_seconds` in the report. Measured: 223.84 s of audio for 223.79 s of frames.
- **Labels** keep their digits (`Recorder.clean_label`, not `to_snake_case`).
- **Scenarios** (`net/dev/scenarios.gd`): `reset()` acts for every peer (hiding spots, Chefty seats, the cohort), parks friends at the store unless the next moment needs them, holds the director's own raid rolls for 120 s (`_next_roll`), and clears every screen's leftovers: the host calls `clear_leftovers()`, and `Shuttle._rpc_dev_reset` (which already reaches every peer) calls the `scenario_listeners` group so the Scenarios node does the same on every client that runs it (remote play's windows, the playtest_tools test's client) (caption stack lines, the quip caption, StageCam shots cut, an open iris). Placements tell the window's recorder (`note_placement`: a `placed` event, no pop/snap for 3 frames). The friend of a two-player moment is a non-watcher who isn't named Observer (`tools\play.ps1 -Friend`); `cite_rescue` keeps both players safe through the setup, waits for the cite to hold, then stands you behind the NICE BOY with the friend on his far side; `lunge` waits for him to be out and winds him up 0.6 s after; `annex_heist` and `annex_bigstamp` stand you on the floor (the counter's lip, the desk's visitor spot); `van_unbuckle` waits until the friend is settled. The director's `_step_quiet` no longer departs a `dev_hold` van (van_seated holds as documented). Timers hold nodes by instance id (the "Lambda capture was freed" errors).
- **Remote play:** `look` over 100 px is eased over frames (smoothstep, 30 px a frame); new steps `until <field> <text> [timeout]`, `mark <text>`, `face`, `goto` (straight line, no pathfinding); `tools\play.ps1 -Script <file>` sends each instance its whole list at once. `-Start -Friend -Scenario <name>` holds the scenario back from the host's launch flags and sends it as a command once the friend (and observer) have joined, so the two-player moments find their friend. `until raid` knows all four director phases (DEPARTING too). In a remote-play session both ends give an ENet peer 20 s before dropping it (a client's first town-block build took 5 s on a loaded machine and dropped both clients).
- **The spectator:** line of sight by physics ray and by boxes of the drawn shapes near the subject (refreshed every 3 s; trees are a trunk box and a crown box; vans and Chefty boxed every frame); thin things (under 1.2 m across: posts, trunks) may cross the view but never hold the camera, wide things pull it in at once and it eases back out at 2.5 m/s; a sphere check keeps it out of colliders and a 0.45 m check out of people; it cranes up 1.5 or 3 m over low obstacles; a subject seated in a van or Chefty is filmed from outside that vehicle (its shell skipped, the camera kept past its box) with a cut on the way in or out; a NICE BOY or player within 2.4 m is a partner: the aim glides to the pair's middle, a yaw within 40 degrees of their axis turns side-on, and of the two sides the one with room wins (1 m of stickiness). Yaw eases at 3/s; a subject moving over 2 m in a frame is a cut, logged as cam_cut with a reason, as is a `follow`/`cam` step. `PLAYTEST_SPECTATOR_DEBUG=1` prints what holds it in.
- **CI:** playtest_tools now has a client (the friend): the two-player moments must reach their state, and the reset after each must leave the friend unseated, uncaught, with no caption lines; it also checks the label, stop() under 50 ms, the eased look, the side-on yaw, `until`'s fields, the lunge's wind-up within 1.2 s and the friend behind the NICE BOY. Both peers run the game's `--fixed-step=30` (`Extra`; a client without it runs flat out under `--fixed-fps` and its `--run-seconds` ends it early). The host waits up to 30 s for the friend and reports `friend`; CI (ci_local's Check and netcode.yml) fails a run without it rather than passing on the "needs a second player" refusals. The client runs `--playtest-tools-test` too (`Extra`): every host reset must reach it (at least 3) and leave its caption stack empty, so the client-side clear is exercised. Port 7820 unchanged.
- **Protocol:** none changed (no RPC, replicated property, save format or yard generation): the shuttle's reset RPC only gained a local group call, and every new path is dev-only.

### 2026-11-03: R2b stage: StageCam swings round a pivot, sweeps the world and the vehicles, and the host picks the two-shot (R2b chunk stage)

- **Why:** the 0.4.9 motion audit (audit/r2/audit.json, catch and e2e): the lunge's wind-up lasted 0.1 s (contact was sent `CREW_LUNGE_WINDUP` ahead, and the lunge took its last 0.25 s), his face filled the first-person view, the ease into the two-shot was a straight blend through his head that started at full speed, the two-shot stood the pair nose to nose, the escort camera whipped 13-16 m in one frame, boarding blended from the street through his head and the van wall into the cabin, StageCam ignored Chefty and the van, and an escort stood 6-9 s against the yard's hedge.
- **Wind-up timing:** `encounter.host_windup` sends contact at `CREW_LUNGE_WINDUP + CREW_LUNGE_SECONDS` (+ the lag stretch). The crouch and lunge poses and the whoosh are driven from the wind-up RPC's placement on every peer (`crew_member._step_lunge_beats`), not from the replicated mode.
- **StageCam (player/stage_cam.gd):** blends ease in and out (smoothstep; the cubic ease-out's first frame was a 0.7 m jump). New optional shot keys: `pivot` (a Vector3 or Node3D the shot is about) and `collide`. A blend into a shot with a pivot runs in the pivot's polar frame (the distance leads the angle: pull back, then swing round; a small lift), looking from the old look point to the new one, so it starts and ends exactly on the two views and never crosses the pivot. With a pivot the eye is clamped relative to the pivot's motion (`STAGE_CAM_MAX_SPEED`, `STAGE_CAM_MAX_TURN`); a swing that would go faster is paced along its own arc (`_paced` holds the blend back that frame) rather than clamped after the fact, which chased the arc point in straight lines and cut through the pivot. And the pivot's own motion carries the eye no faster than `STAGE_CAM_PIVOT_MAX_SPEED` (a pivot that jumps is glided after, at most the sum of the two, never followed in one frame), except behind a closed iris. `collide` sweeps a sphere (`STAGE_CAM_RADIUS`) from the pivot to the eye actually drawn, on every frame of the swing as well as at its end (a review found only the target was swept: the swing out of first person pulls back behind you first, so a player caught with their back to a hedge or Chefty got frames from inside it), through layer 1 and `VEHICLE_LAYER`, past players, crew and props (`player/stage_sweep.gd`), pulling in at once and easing back out; after the speed clamp a memoryless sweep at the camera's near-plane size keeps the clamped eye out of a wall too. Any other body within `STAGE_CAM_BODY_CLEAR` of the eye is hidden (CartoonBody.visible, local) while StageCam has the view. Shots without a pivot behave as before apart from the ease.
- **The two-shot:** the host picks the camera's direction (`_clear_side`: side on first, the yard side first, then swung 0.45 and 0.85 rad either way, the first with a clear sweep, else the most room) and sends it as `cam_yaw`, a new last argument of `Encounter._rpc_catch` (**RPC changed**), so every peer cheats both bodies toward the same camera (`_staging`, `CATCH_CHEAT_TURN`). The victim's body is staged when the hit-stop thaws, and the NICE BOY's `play_stand` gained a `delay`, so both hold still through it. A caught body's pose is `player/caught_pose.gd`, a local cosmetic node the Encounter adds under the CartoonBody on every peer (held / escorted, flinch, wriggle) and releases at the door or when freed.
- **Escort camera:** the 13-16 m whip came from the follow shot's first frames: its target sat 15-25 m off for about two frames, then came back (reproduced in `rec_b_escort_1p`, 15 m at frame 7). The likely cause, not proven, is the escort anchor's interpolated transform holding a stale pose for a tick after the crew's placement is released; `_anchor_point` takes the real position whenever the two disagree by over `ESCORT_ANCHOR_TRUST_M`. If a bad anchor pose still gets through, StageCam's pivot cap (`STAGE_CAM_PIVOT_MAX_SPEED`) turns it into a fast glide of at most 13 m/s rather than a one-frame jump: not a cure, so the cause stays open until a take catches it. The after-takes have no camera jump outside the boarding iris. The follow shot uses the pair as its pivot and `collide`, eases its turn (`ESCORT_CAM_TURN_EASE`), and blends `ESCORT_CAM_BLEND_IN` from the two-shot.
- **Escort route (host only):** `_plan_route` looks for a waypoint on rings round the door (`ESCORT_ROUTE_RADII`, every `ESCORT_ROUTE_STEP_DEG`) on the navmesh or in plain line, with a clear sweep on to the door, when the straight way is walled; progress is watched (`ESCORT_STUCK_*`): stuck, he replans; stuck `ESCORT_STUCK_HANDOFF` s in all, you're handed to the door from where you are (the Shuttle walks you in).
- **Boarding:** at BOARDING the local player's escort shot holds, the iris closes `BOARD_IRIS_DELAY` later, and once it's shut the stage shots pop with a cut (the Shuttle's seat shot takes over behind it) and the iris opens. Without an iris (or with no live shot) the old pop. Freed, unbuckled or reset before the cut, `_clear_peer` lets the iris go (`iris.release`: a close not started yet is dropped and never shows, one under way opens again); before a review caught it, the iris shut with nothing left to open it.
- **Crew beats:** `crew_member.landed_at` (host) and the brain's land look (`CREW_LAND_LOOK_SECONDS` before senses count after a hop-out); `spot_take` and `land_look` poses in `npcs/crew_gestures.gd`; `CREW_SPOT_FREEZE` 0.35 -> 0.6, `CREW_LUNGE_WINDUP` 0.35 -> 0.4, `CATCH_GOTCHA_GAP` 0.78 -> 1.05, `CATCH_CAM_BLEND` 0.35 -> 0.6, `CATCH_CAM_DISTANCE` 2.6 -> 3.0, `CATCH_CAM_FOV` 55 -> 40. The player's cap sits above the eyes (cartoon_body.gd).
- **Networking, saves:** `_rpc_catch` has a new argument (BUILD_VERSION needs a bump; left to the integrator). No replicated property, save format or yard/task generation change. `landed_at` is host-only.
- **Tests:** `--stage-test` gains `pivot`, two runs: the shot on the far side of someone 3 m ahead (a straight blend would pass 0.2 m from them; the swing never comes within 1 m), with a wide wall beyond them the eye settles in front of; and the gotcha's case, someone right in front of you, the shot to the side and a wall just behind you (the swing's pull-back went through it before the drawn eye was swept); neither run exceeds `STAGE_CAM_MAX_SPEED` or has a frame past the wall's face. It also gains `boarding release` (freed before the boarding iris starts closing: it never shows; freed mid-close: it opens again). `--encounter-test` gains "the host's contact is the full wind-up and the lunge away", "the gotcha stands them CATCH_GOTCHA_GAP apart, both turned toward the two-shot" and 8d (a wall across the way out: the route goes round, clear on to the door); the client's wind-up lead check is now `CREW_LUNGE_SECONDS + 0.05` (it was a flaky 0.25 against a 0.35 s lead under 2% loss; the lead is now 0.65-0.8 s), and its escorted-body check starts after `ESCORT_LINK_BLEND`.

### 2026-11-03: one cinematic HUD mode, one place for overhead text (R2b hud)

- **Why:** the R2 motion audit (audit/r2) found the yard HUD (job panel, hotbar hints, goal, prompts such as "[E] Pick up Rake" and "[E] Hide") up over every two-shot, the van ride and the Annex; every NICE BOY line on screen twice (bubble and subtitle); three or four caption lines stacked over the catch with quips under his lines; subtitles a beat late behind earlier lines; bubbles cut off at the screen's edges and by roofs, wrapping into one-word columns; name tags poking through the van's roof and stacked over two heads; and the CHASE flash tinting the whole frame.
- **Cinematic HUD mode:** `ui/hud_mode.gd` (static, read only) answers why the local player's screen belongs to the moment: a control lock (`player.is_control_locked`), a stage shot that isn't the player's own hide peek, a staged body, a Shuttle seat (`shuttle.seat_info`), zone BORDER (the Annex) or the director's cohort. UIRoot eases the yard pieces' top-level `modulate` (HudRows, TaskList, HotbarHud, HeatMeter, DayHud, ThrowMeter, and JobGoal times the raid dim) out and back (`R2B_HUD_*`), so each piece keeps its own logic and nothing else needs to know. The caption stack, the interaction prompt, quips, overheads and the run cues ask the same function. Rejected: hiding pieces with `visible` (they set it themselves every frame) and reparenting them under one Control (other code finds them by their UIRoot paths).
- **Overheads:** `ui/overheads.gd` (UIRoot child, `process_priority` 1000, after the crew, the players and the cameras) owns how bubbles and name tags read on this peer's screen. Tags fade through `GeometryInstance3D.transparency`, which nobody else sets, so it never fights the alpha the crew's own fade or the talking tint set. Tags and bubbles draw with `no_depth_test` and fade whole on a ray (layer 1) to the owner's head instead of being cut by geometry. A driving Shuttle's Solid is off every layer (`shuttle._set_solid`), so `Overheads.through_moving_van` also tests the segment against its five boxes; anything else drawn without a layer-1 collider (foliage) does not hide a tag. Rejected: a query-only layer on the van's Solid (a shuttle.gd change other chunks are editing). A bubble's world position is nudged along the camera's axes so its screen rect stays inside the safe margin. `Overheads.present()` picks bubble or subtitle once per line (crew_member.show_bark calls it; the old `_subtitle` is gone). Other speakers can join with the `overhead_speakers` group (the HUD test's stand-ins do; the director frees anything in `nice_crew` outside a raid).
- **Caption stack:** kind rank (subtitle above teammate above radio above quip) added to the caller's priority; two lines at most and a film-subtitle baseline in the cinematic mode; radio and quips cleared on entering it; quips wait under a subtitle; a `caption_blockers` group (the receipt slip) holds the radio and quips, while spoken and teammate lines still show at once and the stack moves up clear of the blocker's screen rect (a 3D blocker's mesh boxes through the camera); a review found holding every line made a bark or driver line during the ~7 s slip land seconds after its sound; line heights come from the font (`_text_height`), because on the push frame a Label's own minimum size is still one word a row and threw the stack up the screen for a frame. The soft plate is a `Panel` child drawn behind the Label, so the `caption_backdrop` setting's stylebox stays the settings test's contract.
- **Networking, saves:** no RPC, replicated property, save format or yard/task generation change. Everything here is local presentation reading state that already replicates.
- **Test:** `--hud-test` (port 7834, host and a Target client at 150 ms): the real catch over the network on the client (the yard HUD eased out within the fade, never back while held, no prompt on any held frame), spoken-line onset and stack rules on the held client, the HUD's return (waits, then eases, no pop), and on the host with stand-in NICE BOYS: bubble far / subtitle near and each line on screen once, a bubble at the screen's edge glides inside, a long line wraps to at most three rows, tags behind a wall, behind a nearer tag, in the van and behind a driving van (a layer-0 Solid). The held check reads the drawn prompt Label3D with a test prompt source offering one throughout (and requires it drawn on the free frames before the catch lands, so it can fail); a test caption blocker on the held client must hold the radio but not a spoken line, which shows clear of it.

### 2026-11-03: the seams between the beats: one size rule for floating words, prompts and tags wait with the HUD (R2c hud)

- **Why:** the second motion audit of 0.4.10 (audit/r2b) found three caption lines while held with one duplicated, the gotcha subtitle up to 1.5 s after its bark (older spoken lines held both slots: ties went to the oldest), bubbles printed over each other, bubbles and tags filling the frame through the two-shot's 40 degree lens or a metre away (a 'laude' tag clipped top-left for 5 s), the corner quip over the goal line, tags drawn through a hedge, prompts and tags popping on mid-swing, "[E] Grab Claude" over a friend being escorted, the HUD blinking out on a hedge climb-out, and Chefty's count through the Shuttle's window.
- **Caption stack** (`ui/caption_stack.gd`): spoken lines tie-break newest first and one that isn't chosen is over (`left = 0`); the drawn count (leaving lines included) is capped at `most_lines()`, cutting the oldest leaving line; a push of text already alive renews it; entering the cinematic mode also expires subtitles older than `R2C_HUD_STALE_SPOKEN`. Wrapped lines take `TextFit.balanced_width` (`ui/text_fit.gd`, shared with the corner quip): the narrowest width with the same row count, by bisection on the font's own wrap.
- **Overheads** (`ui/overheads.gd`): `fit_size` scales a Label3D's pixel size (from its base, kept in meta `r2c_base_px`) so one line stays within a share of the view's height for the drawing camera, for fixed-size and world-size labels alike; tags, bubbles and the props' floating counts use it. Bubbles are placed nearest first and `avoid_shift` moves a farther one off the nearer ones before the glide. Occlusion adds `through_hide_spot` (hiding spots' boxes, plus an `overhead_view_blockers` group for anything else without a layer-1 collider) and tests a parked Shuttle's boxes too (its Solid isn't on layer 1). Tag ranking has hysteresis (`R2C_HUD_TAG_KEEP_M`), a wider clearance (`R2C_HUD_TAG_CLEAR`, plus the pair's closing speed on screen times the fade, up to `R2C_HUD_TAG_CLEAR_MAX`), a snap to 0 when the rects really touch, and a dwell before coming back (`R2C_HUD_TAG_DWELL`); "drawn" is `is_visible_in_tree()` (a ghosted observer's tag neither shows nor pushes a real one aside). A bubble's form is still chosen once (`present`), but a showing bubble is re-checked every frame: it fades when its anchor leaves the view or comes within `R2C_HUD_BUBBLE_KEEP_M`, and on a camera cut (`_camera_cut`: another camera, or a jump over `R2C_HUD_CUT_MOVE_M` / a turn over `R2C_HUD_CUT_TURN` in a frame) it's gone at once unless it would be placed whole there. Rejected: handing the rest of a lost bubble's line to the subtitle stack (a line popping in late, already read). Rejected: depth-tested tags (a roof edge cuts a tag in half, R2b's reason) and per-label scripts (every owner would need editing).
- **When overlays may show:** `HudMode.overlays_held(tree, peer)` is true while `reason()` is set (the Annex aside), while the stage camera blends back from a shot that wasn't the player's own view, and for `R2C_HUD_OVERLAY_DELAY` (= the HUD's return delay) after; the prompt and the tags ask it, so they return with the yard HUD. Static vars per peer hold the last top shot and the last busy time. `hide_move` (the eye's way out of a hedge) joined `OWN_SHOTS`; being pulled out rides the same shot, so `reason()` checks `HideView.is_pulled()` first and returns `&"stage"` (review fix: the found pull-out had the whole HUD back up under his hands). The job panel's hidden dim only lifts while the HUD is free, and resets to 1 once the HUD is fully out.
- **Grabbing a player:** one predicate, `player.can_be_grabbed()`, read by the grabber's prompt (`_grabbable_player`) and the host (`brawl._handle_grab`): adds captured (director), staged, seated (Shuttle seat or any seat) and not drawn to the old hidden/asleep/carried/thrown checks. Host-authoritative as before; the host now refuses a grab on a captured, staged or seated friend, which it used to accept.
- **Recorder:** events.json frames gain `words` (caption lines with alpha and rect, overheads' bubbles and tags, the prompt with its alpha), read every frame: `hud` rescans its labels every 15 frames, which made a caption look up to 0.5 s late against sync.json. Remote play ghosts the observer on the friend's window too.
- **Networking, saves:** the only gameplay change is the host refusing grabs on captured, staged or seated players (same RPC, same replicated state). No RPC, replicated property, save format or yard/task generation change.
- **Test:** `--hud-test` (port 7834) gains `grab` (a caught friend can't be grabbed, a free one can), `apart` (two bubbles at once don't touch; caps through a 30 degree lens and at 1 m), `stack_seams` (spoken lines in a row: no crowded or doubled frame, newest on; a balanced wrap is the narrowest), `hedge` (a hiding spot's box hides a tag), and the `back` step checks a quip triggered while held waits and plays after (its line up in the corner caption). Review additions: `held_seams` (the same seams on the held client, cap 2, with a line on its way out while the next lands) and `bubble_gone` (a bubble whose head leaves the picture fades, comes back, and is gone on the first frame of a cut). The held-prompt check allows the prompt's 0.12 s fade-out counted in frame time, not wall time (a loaded machine's long frames failed it once), and its test source sits in front of the camera (off-screen prompts aren't drawn now). settings_test's corner-caption check accepts the caption lifted above the goal line.

### 2026-11-03: the Courtesy Shuttle finished: door timelines, a road that stays a road, drive-aways that never stop (R2b van)

- **Why:** the R2 motion audit (audit/r2, van and e2e) found the rider stepping out through the shut door at Annex B, the arriving van crossing the lawn and the sidewalk, the return van driving through a pine, a ride spent facing a wall and a water cooler, the van stopped dead 90 m down the street for anyone watching, and the drop-off van never leaving.
- **Door timelines.** `Trip.doors[which]` is a time-ordered list of `[open, at]` (the last `VAN_DOOR_EVENTS_KEPT`), not one slot: `_host_at_annex` sends the open and `_plan_leave`'s later shut back to back, and the shut overwrote the open, so the door never opened. `_apply_doors` plays the event in force each frame; sounds play per event; `_door_open` / `_door_fully_open` answer for a time. `_rpc_door`'s signature is unchanged; the snapshot's `doors` value is now lists (late joiners of the same build only). The rider's seat camera pops when their step starts, not when the unseat plan arrives. Host stats `unseats` / `unseats_shut_door` let shuttle_test prove nobody steps out before the door is open.
- **The road.** The van parks at the near curb (`VAN_CURB_Z` 24.05, lane 24.85: right-hand traffic, open side to the yard's sidewalk) instead of the far lane, so the door step and the exit points (`_exit_point`, `VAN_EXIT_OUT` past the step, on the ground) are on the sidewalk. Swerves are bounded to the carriageway (`VAN_SWERVE_RANGE` 5.0 out, 0.1 toward the curb; the eased swerve is clamped too) and go round people standing in the road (`_person_in`, also in the park box). The cause of the lawn crossing was `Surroundings`' scenery pines standing in the lot street: `Surroundings.dress` takes `clear_rects`, and world.gd keeps both legacy streets and their sidewalks clear (every random draw is still made, so the trees that stay are the same trees). A leaving route runs along the street's axis (snapped to x or z): a creep's slightly angled nose drifted a 100 m route onto the far sidewalk.
- **Drive-aways never brake.** DEPART, RETURN_DEPART and LEAVE coast (`_drive_segments(..., coast)`), and `_route_pose` carries a pose past a route's end straight on. The planned road still ends `VAN_HIDE_METERS` from everyone (planning 200 m in one host tick was a hitch); riders change zone during the drive once their iris is shut and the yard can't see the van. Each peer that still sees a van whose plan has moved on (a jump to the Annex, HIDDEN, the trip over) draws it on the drive leg it was on (`Trip.ghost_leg`) until it's `VAN_VANISH_METERS` from its camera, and plays none of the next stop's sounds from that copy. Rejected: a longer planned route (the host hitch), and extending HIDDEN's hold (it parked in plain sight).
- **Drop-off leaves:** `_host_drop` used `depart_planned`, already true on every return trip, so the leave never fired; it has its own flag. `_plan_leave` shuts the door from its `at` (it used to start 0.4 s in the past), blinks, then pulls.
- **Return boarding behind the iris** (`BOARD_IRIS`): `host_start_return` seats riders from wherever they stand at Annex B behind their own Daisy Seal iris (closed by their peer from the seat entry's time, opened in the seat) instead of a 0.6 s arc through the building.
- **The ride's camera:** `CabinCam` is unchanged; the door panels got tall windows and the cooler moved out of its line. The cab shot (`FreshenerCam`, between the front seats) starts `VAN_RIDE_MONTAGE_AT` after the pull with a `VAN_FRESHENER_BLEND` dolly; `_montage_at(leg)` is the one formula the rider's beats and the host's iris check share.
- **Engine and horn:** a local `engine_idle` loop per van, level and pitch from the leg's speed (no new sound); the arrival honk plays when the van is first seen within `VAN_HONK_NEAR`. tools/sfx_r2_van.py gave `van_pull` a clutch clunk on its first sample, `blinker_tick` a sharper click and level, and `van_door_slide` a latch click, so their attacks are measurable on time.
- **Harness:** `director._step_quiet` leaves a `dev_holding()` van alone (it departed `van_seated` two seconds after seating); `van_straggler` keeps its straggler out on the lawn (the shuttle doesn't recall a `dev_straggle`d NICE BOY) so the creep plays; `raid_warn` stands you on the front lawn looking up the street the van comes down.
- **Networking, saves:** no RPC signature or replicated property changed; the plan and snapshot dictionaries carry new values (door lists, seat mode 3), and the legacy lot's street trees and the van's parking lane changed (yard and task generation are untouched). Treat as a protocol change for BUILD_VERSION. No save change.
- **Review fixes (r2b:van review):**
  - **Only a full road coasts.** `_route` sets `_route_cut` when it ended short of its far end (`route_blocked_ends`, `route_ground_misses`). `_plan_route_from` and `_plan_leave` then use the braking profile, so a cut drive-away stops before whatever cut it instead of coasting through it.
  - **People never cut a route.** When no swerve inside `VAN_SWERVE_RANGE` is clear of people, `_route` looks for one that is clear of the static obstacles only (`_route_blocked(..., people = false)`). If the point itself is clear of them, the route keeps going. Otherwise an arrival cut short at a watcher would start its approach right in front of them.
  - **The crew stays aboard while anyone might still see the van.** `is_gone()` (DEPART and the HIDDEN after it) asks `_seen_by_yard_at` whether a yard player within `VAN_VANISH_METERS` has a line to where the ghost would be (`leg_pose` of the last DEPART leg, extrapolated). Before, it freed the seated NICE BOYS at 90 m while watchers still drew the van out to 200 m.
  - **The `doors` stat is judged when the step starts.** `_place_riders` (host) runs it on the frame each step down begins, after `_plan_leave`'s shut is planned, and also counts a door that starts shutting during the step. Checked by reverting `_door_state` to last-event-wins: shuttle_test `doors` failed (1 of 3).
  - **Ghost riders stay seated.** While a peer draws the ghost (`Trip.ghosting`), remote riders keep their seated pose and aren't unstaged until the ghost ends. Under 150 ms lag an observer saw the rider step out of the ghost mid-street on the yard road.
  - **One iris at closing time.** `annex._force_out` moves the straggler `ANNEX_DOOR_IRIS_WAIT` after the door iris, the way a visit goes in (`_forcing_out` guards the per-tick loop). It used to move on the same tick, so the forecourt showed before the door did.
  - **Iris hand-off.** `iris.gd` gained `close_count()` and `shape()`, and a close that carries on from a part-shut iris no longer swishes twice. The Annex's 0.6 s door-iris open is skipped when someone has closed the iris since. `BOARD_IRIS` takes over an iris that is already shutting (`close(now, ..., iris.shape())`) instead of reopening it to close its own seal.
  - **Tuning out of the code.** The engine loop's tuning moved to constants: `VAN_ENGINE_SILENT_DB`, `VAN_ENGINE_EASE_FLOOR_DB`, `VAN_ENGINE_SMOOTH` (a per-second exponential rate, no longer per frame) and `VAN_ENGINE_SHARE_MAX`. `_person_in`'s height window is `VAN_PERSON_HEIGHT`, and the engine's mount point is `nice_van.gd ENGINE_AT`.
- **Tests:** shuttle_test adds `road` and `road_out` (every route point near the yard keeps the van's body between the curbs), `doors` (nobody steps down before the side door is open) and `dropoff_curb` (the rider lands on the sidewalk, the van parked square, then pulls away on the road). The ride check measures on-screen speed over server time (a lagged client's clock easing stretched a tick and read 15.8 m/s from a 12 m/s van).

### 2026-11-03: hiding, the payoff (R2b hide)

- **Why:** the R2 motion audit (audit/r2/audit.json, HIDING) confirmed the found hider was left standing on top of the hedge through the gotcha (the stage took over mid-lift: `CREW_FOUND_CONTACT` 0.3 s against a 0.5 s lift), the lift raised the eye to 2.9 m and whipped round, the poke from inside was a man's crotch, the hedge's own outer mesh filled every turn, and more (13 findings).
- **The found timeline is host-timed and spelled out in constants:** `net/components/hide_spot.gd` `_pull` sends the found (`_rpc_inspect`), holds the hider inside for `HIDE_FOUND_REVEAL` (exits are refused meanwhile), then `_rpc_pull`s them to `found_landing()`: the ground just out from the face he looked in at, a step to the side away from him, checked for room. `CREW_FOUND_CONTACT` is now `HIDE_FOUND_REVEAL + HIDE_PULL_SECONDS + HIDE_FOUND_SETTLE`, so the gotcha starts after the landing. The landing rides on the player as meta `hide_pulled_to` (`[where, server time]`, set on the host), and `encounter.gd host_begin_catch` stands the victim there instead of wherever the host's lagged copy of the lift was; the pile does the same from `player.gd host_inspect_resolve`. Same RPCs, same arguments: only when they're sent changed.
- **Poses on other bodies go on in a late physics tick.** With physics interpolation on, only what a physics tick leaves behind is drawn, so the NICE BOY's peer pose (`props/hide_spot.gd peer_pose`, also used for piles) runs in `_physics_process` at `process_physics_priority` 150, after his body, his gestures and his crew Poser; `player/hide_view.gd` adds a `LateTick` child at the same priority for the pile's NICE BOY, the sinking body and the mound. Writing them in `_process` (the first try) drew nothing. Values are absolute and eased from what the body did that tick, so a hit-stop never accumulates them.
- **The hider's eye is carried by hide_view, not blended by the StageCam.** The first-person hide shot hangs off `HideEye`, a top-level Marker3D with interpolation off that `hide_view.gd` moves every frame: from the drawn view to the spot's eye on an eased path (position and pitch smoothstep, the turn starting a beat late), held there with the peek's rise and the pull toward his face, and on the way out from the drawn view to the base camera under a no-look `hide_move` shot that pops with no blend once they coincide. StageCam's own ease-out blend (a full-speed start) is left alone for everyone else.
- **Inside a spot is drawn locally.** On the hider's own machine only, the spot's `Look`, `Tells` and `Notices` (a shed: just `Look/Door`) stop being drawn while the first-person eye is settled inside, and `hide_view.make_shell()` draws the inside around the eye (a hedge's leaf tangle with an opening, a bin's tub). Everyone else sees the spot. A bin's eye faces the yard (`eye_side`: the side most toward the job lot's middle, the same on every peer); a shed's eye is just inside its door at the knot holes; a shed's two exit places are on the latch side of the doorway.
- **A find is per peer and bounded in time** (review fix). `_rpc_inspect` gained a fourth argument, the found peer (0 for every other phase), which `props/hide_spot.gd` keeps as `_found_peer` and returns from `inspect_view()`. `hide_view.is_found()` is true only for that peer (0 still means "this player", for the pile, which is per player) and only for `found_hold_seconds()` (`HIDE_FOUND_REVEAL + HIDE_PULL_SECONDS + HIDE_FOUND_SETTLE + HIDE_FOUND_HOLD_SLACK`) after the find; a spot forgets a finished find when the next hider enters, and a pile dive clears the last pile's poke. Before this, a spot's last find held whoever dived in next (no E, no peek, the inside drawn pulled open), held a shed's other occupant too, and left a re-dived pile's mound scooped flat. The hider's frame opening on a find closes again `HIDE_FOUND_SHUT_DELAY` after the pull, with the door.
- **Shared hide numbers live in constants:** the shed's door and knot-hole heights (`HIDE_SHED_WALL_SHARE`, `HIDE_SHED_DOOR_HEADROOM`, `HIDE_SHED_KNOT_DROP`, through `shed_door_height()` / `shed_knot_height()` on the base spot, used by both the model and the eye), the shed climb-out offsets, the shed landing factor, the gotcha's `HIDE_PULLED_TO_TRUST` window, the poke timings shared by the spot and the hider's view (`HIDE_POKE_*`), the found shut and clang, and the gesture tails.
- **Turn speed for tells is smoothed** (`HIDE_TREMBLE_SMOOTH`) on every peer and on the host's suspicion, so network-stepped yaw can't read as a jerk.
- **Networking, saves:** `_rpc_inspect` (net/components/hide_spot.gd) takes a fourth argument, the found peer; no other new RPC argument, replicated property or save field; no yard or task generation change. The found sequence's timing changed (when `_rpc_pull` is sent, `CREW_FOUND_CONTACT`), host and clients must run the same build for it to line up, and `player.gd host_inspect_cancel` now also sends the existing `_rpc_pile_inspect` with phase `&"cancel"`. Two new sound ids (`hide_grab`, `shed_creak`) and eight bark pools (`inspect_<kind>`, `near_miss_<kind>`).
- **Test:** `--hide-test` (port 7828) adds: the chase music holds "hide" while hidden; a slow, stepped look raises no rustle and a hard turn does; a found hider stays in through the reveal and lands on the ground off the spot; the gotcha's victim is on the ground beside the spot; the inspect and near-miss lines by kind; a find on a shed mate doesn't hold the other occupant; a hedge just emptied by a find lets the next hider out; an old pile find doesn't scoop the next mound.

### 2026-11-03: Annex B, the motion pass: one looks table, stamps timed to their ink (R2b annex)

- **One looks table.** `net/annex/annex.gd _run_looks()` builds {window: {icon, color}} from the run's seeded icons (`annex_schedule.gd window_icons`) and `Constants.ANNEX_ICON_COLORS` (a fixed color per pictogram), and the set (`annex_b.gd apply_run`, now given those looks instead of window letters), the floor lines (`FloorLine.apply_look`: paint, footprints, sticker) and the clipboard all draw from it. The badge is one CPU-drawn `ImageTexture` per pictogram/color pair (`OfficeProps.badge_texture`, cached), not a SubViewport, so it exists the frame it's asked for. A window's footprints moved from its baked mesh into its floor line (which recolors); the plaque board is the badge sprite.
- **Ink lands with its stamp.** A stamp's KA-CHUNK plays from the prop that makes it on the frame it hits (`ServiceWindow stamp_down`, `BigStamp slam`); the host sends the stamped form `ANNEX_INK_LEAD` early with the hit's server time as `at`, and `Form27B.set_progress` inks each box only from its `ink_at` (a gap apart for Window D and closing). `_rpc_form` no longer plays stamps itself (only a co-sign's distant one and a smudge). Serve: `serve_ink` before the hit, `serve_done` at the end. The approval at the desk, the big stamp and the self-stamp use the same pattern.
- **Once, not on a timer.** Wrong-window head shakes: the client asks until the host's `wrong_window` override for it arrives, then not until it steps off; the host also remembers (`_wrong_seen`, cleared when the body leaves that counter). Turnstile: the client asks until answered, once per approach; the host's rate limit is spent only on asks within reach. The prop's arms are driven by the nearest body in the passage (one clack per push), not by the unlock.
- **Cuts only behind a shut iris.** Closing's straggler and a leaving visitor: the iris closes at `at`, the host moves them at `at + ANNEX_DOOR_IRIS_WAIT` (a `_pending` item), the iris opens at `at + ANNEX_IRIS_OPEN_AT`, all server time.
- **The forecourt beat** (the seam with the Shuttle): `_maybe_return` holds a rider who is through for `ANNEX_FORECOURT_BEAT` (the receipt) before `shuttle.host_start_return`; the group waits for the last one's beat too. How the van takes them from there is the Shuttle's (r2b:van).
- **Closing's dark** is local and cosmetic: while the local player is inside, `_dim_room` scales the WorldEnvironment's sky light, the sun and `adjustment_brightness` per light row, and puts them back the moment they're out or the Annex ends.
- **Staff avoidance** is cosmetic (`npcs/clerk.gd _stand_off`): the host never reads staff bodies.
- **Networking, saves:** no RPC signature, replicated property or save format change, no yard/task generation change. Payload changes inside existing RPCs: a form dict may carry `carbon_idx` (FormState.carbon), a `volley` override carries `n`, and `_rpc_form`'s `at` can now be up to `ANNEX_INK_LEAD` in the future. Box counts (`ANNEX_BOXES_MIN` 4) and serve length (3.4 s) change the host's rules and timing, so host and client must run the same build.
- **Tests:** `--annex-test` checks the wrong window shakes his head only once while you stand there, and that a solo visit by the bot's rules averages inside `ANNEX_PACE_TARGET` (40-75 s); its home waits include `ANNEX_FORECOURT_BEAT`.
- **Review fixes (R2b annex).** The clerk who just served you is not a wrong window while you stay on his spot: `serve_ink` marks that window in `_wrong_seen` for the peer, and the client stops asking it (`_asked_wrong`) until it steps off; `--annex-test` stands on the counter through a whole serve plus 2 s and checks no head shake is booked. The turnstile's arms turn only for the players it was opened for (`open` args `who`, player node names) and only for its span (`seconds`, ANNEX_TURNSTILE_OPEN + PASS_SLACK; closing opens it for the roster until `_reset_set` closes it at the end). Opened for named players, the prop no longer drops its own bar on every peer: `_step_turnstile` owns that collider, per local player, so someone who wasn't let out can't walk through on their own peer. Closing's AFTER HOURS KA-CHUNK plays only on a form that closing approved (`after_hours`), and the hatches slam at least once per empty box on any such form. The receipt quip waits `ReceiptSlip.duration(lines)` (the slip's own row count) instead of a guessed line count.

### 2026-11-03: R2b integration (tools, stage, hud, van, hide, annex)

- **Merged** in order: r2b:tools, r2b:stage, r2b:hud, r2b:van, r2b:hide, r2b:annex. Most conflicts were the append-only banners in `core/constants.gd`, `docs/GAME.md` and this file; both sides kept. Three were code:
  - `net/run_events/director.gd` `_step_quiet`: tools and van both stopped the director departing a scenario's held van. Kept van's `shuttle.dev_holding()` (it reads the same `dev_hold` and also lets go once a departure is planned or the trip ended).
  - `net/dev/scenarios.gd`: tools' instance-id inspection beats (no lambda captures) carry hide's per-kind `inspect_bark` / `near_miss_bark` lines; hide's lawn-height NICE BOY sits under tools' `_placing`. `annex_heist` keeps tools' `HEIST_STAND` placement (annex's `_stand_by` did the same job; the playtest_tools test checks tools').
  - `net/annex/annex.gd`: van's review and annex both made closing time's force-out wait for the door iris. Kept one: annex's `_forcing` / `"force_out"` pending item (now also checks the peer is still here and the Annex active, as van's `_force_out_now` did) and annex's server-timed `_rpc_door_iris` (close at `at`, open at `at + ANNEX_IRIS_OPEN_AT`). Van's `close_count` guard isn't needed there: `iris.close()` already drops a pending open, so the ride home's boarding seal still takes the shut over.
- **Fixed in integration:** both bot soaks failed on `Camera3D.unproject_position` "p.d == 0" from `ui/overheads.gd` (r2b:hud). `is_position_behind` measures with the node's global transform and `unproject_position` with `get_camera_transform`, which physics interpolation can put a tick away; `Overheads.in_front` checks both and guards every unproject in overheads and the caption stack's blocker rect. Smoke's timeout now names the step it stalled on.
- **Load:** other worktrees ran 80-130 Godot processes during this merge. Smoke, hide, shuttle and encounter then failed with timeouts, dropped clients and frame hitches; an A/B of smoke against the pre-van merge under the same load failed the same way, and all of them passed once the load dropped. `encounter`'s client one-tick-turn check (step 2) is the flakiest under load (0.36-0.88 rad against 0.355); it fails on the pre-R2b base too.
- **BUILD_VERSION** 0.4.9 -> 0.4.10: stage's `_rpc_catch` `cam_yaw`, hide's `_rpc_inspect` found peer and find timing, van's trip plan/snapshot (door timelines, `BOARD_IRIS`), annex's form payloads and serve rules.


### 2026-11-03: The bot soaks' flakes: two crashes and a throttle fixed at the cause, and the checks judge the network's share (flakes:soak)

Every integration had to rerun the bot soaks, and the last two full suites failed them: voice loss over the 6% limit, a bot segfaulting in `tools/soak.sh`, the run-event soak's ~2% hitch check, smoke's exit code. Measured before changing anything: the bot soak (`-n 4 -s 45`) and the run-event soak (`-n 4 -s 170 -e 65`) together, the way `ci_local.ps1` runs them, on this 32-thread machine, which always had 7 to 38 other agents' Godot processes running; "loaded" adds 24 busy Godot processes of our own, "heavy" 48. What was found, and which kind of fix each got:

- **The segfault was a crash in the voice code, fixed at the cause.** The Application log held 155 Godot crashes in three days, every one at the same offset, and the four crash dumps read (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\CrashDumps`) show the audio thread reading freed memory inside `audio_stream_generator.cpp` while the main thread joins a thread at shutdown. `net/voice/voice.gd` gives every remote player's head an `AudioStreamGenerator` in every multiplayer session, voice or not, and `_release_output` stopped the player and dropped its stream. The generator's playback reads its generator through a plain pointer and stays in the mix for a fade after `stop()`, so the fade could read the freed generator: now and then a crash on quitting, on a player leaving, or on a respawn. Through Git Bash it shows as "Segmentation fault" (exit 139) with nothing in the log, since the crash is on the audio thread. A script freeing generators under a playing mix crashed 1 run in 11 without the fix, 0 in 11 with it (thin on its own; the dumps are the evidence). The fix: the playback holds its generator (`playback.set_meta(&"generator", generator)`), so the generator lives exactly as long as the mix needs it. Soak runs: 2 bot crashes in 26 before (one passed the old report, which ignored exit codes; a third crashed in an instrumented run), 0 in 26 after.
- **A client's ENet throttle collapsed a second after connecting: a real bug, fixed at the cause.** Logging ENet's statistics every frame showed each client's packet throttle limit toward the host dropping from 32 to 1 about a second after it connected (on a joiner, right as it finishes loading the yard) and coming back at ENet's next once-a-second bandwidth pass, so for about a second ENet threw away ~97% of the client's unreliable traffic: its movement and its voice, for every other player. The 2026-10-18 `throttle_configure` fix sets the throttle's acceleration and deceleration; this is the limit, which that doesn't touch. Reproduced with two bare `ENetMultiplayerPeer`s (limit 1 to 5 in every run, with or without a stall). Calling `bandwidth_limit(0, 0)` on the host's `ENetConnection` after `create_server` stops it: 32 in every run, also with 0.5 to 2 s stalls right after connecting and a 2 s stall 3 s in. `Net.host()` does that now. It's local to the host and nothing on the wire changes: an old client joining a new host gets the fix, a new client joining an old host doesn't.
- **The rest of the voice loss is the machine, so the measurement changed; the limit didn't.** With the throttle fixed, voice loss on the network is what the simulated 2% a hop predicts: 1.6 to 2.3% on the host (one lossy hop), 2.9 to 4.0% on clients (one hop from the host, two from each other client), in every run, alone, loaded or heavy. The rest comes in runs of 50 to 105 packets on a peer that wasn't reading: every client builds the yard for about 2 s right after joining, and a peer that isn't reading lets its socket's receive buffer overflow (Godot's ENet doesn't size the buffer). A bare ENet receiver measured here lost nothing to stalls up to 400 ms at 150 KB/s (more than the soak's busiest peer, the host at ~135 KB/s), 50 of 273 packets to an 800 ms stall and 159 to 1.6 s.
