So every peer now logs its stalls (frames over 50 ms, loading included, `net/dev/probe.gd`), the voice code logs each run of lost packets with the send times either side, and `tools/soak_report.py` sets one against the other: a run lost while the peer it was due at (the receiver, or the host relaying it) sat in a stall of 400 ms or more is the machine's, counted and printed apart (the table's `v loss` is raw, `v net` is judged). The limit is still 2.5 x the simulated loss + 1%. (First written as 250 ms; the review raised it to 400 ms, the longest stall measured to lose nothing, since nothing measured shows a 250 to 400 ms stall losing a packet.)
- **The hitch check, the same way.** Hitches are logged with when they ended, the gap, and the mover's peer; one that comes with a frame of 50 ms or more on the receiver, the mover or the host is the machine's (`+stall`). This replaces "inconclusive if this peer had over 20 long frames a minute", which excused nothing when the stall was on the host or the mover (the run-event soak that failed at 2.2% had 13 long frames a minute on the failing bot and 33 on the host) and excused everything on a peer over the line. The 2% limit is unchanged.
- **The run-event soak's engine error was a bug, fixed at the cause.** `props/receipt_slip.gd` posed a new slip, and played the printer's chatter at its global position, before the slip was in the tree, so a slip shown late (after a stall, or a receipt over a laggy link) logged `Condition "!is_inside_tree()" is true`, which fails the soak: 1 in 5 run-event soaks alone, 2 in 5 loaded. `show_for` poses it once it's parented.
- **Smoke's timeout counted a slow machine as a hang; changed to a progress timeout.** Smoke had 30 s from its first frame for everything, and needed 18.4 s alone (8.6 s of it the client process starting and building the world, 8 s Chefty's ride, which is game time). Loaded it needed 26 to 28 s, and with more agents running as well it timed out 12 runs in 12. It now fails as hung when nothing happens for 60 s before the client is in (the client's launch is the part that scales with load) or 30 s between later steps, with a 150 s ceiling, and says which steps it reached. After: 12 of 12 alone and 12 of 12 loaded pass; heavy, 3 of 4 pass and 1 fails because the client was disconnected (below).
- **Crashes on the way out fail the soak now.** `tools/soak.sh` writes each Godot's exit status (`exit_codes.txt`), and `soak_report.py` fails a peer that exited non-zero, even after writing its report.
- **Checked that it still fails a sick game** (`soak_report.py` on edited copies of a passing run): 8% more voice lost mid-session with no stall fails (11.0% against 6.0%); the same loss inside a 1 s stall passes; 5% of updates hitching with no stall fails; the same hitches each after a 120 ms host frame pass; a bot exiting 139 after its report fails.

Five runs each of both soaks together (bot soak / run-event soak), three heavy:

| | before | after |
| --- | --- | --- |
| alone | 4/5 and 3/4 pass (fails: Bot1 8.4% voice; the receipt error; a fifth run-event soak was cut short by an edit, and the last two ran with the receipt fix already in); 1 segfault on quit, passed | 5/5 and 5/5; raw voice loss up to 9.1%, network share at most 3.6%; hitches at most 0.7% |
| loaded | 3/5 and 3/5 (fails: Bot2 7.8% and 6.5% voice; the receipt error twice); 1 segfault on quit, passed | 5/5 and 5/5; raw voice up to 7.0%, network at most 3.8%; hitches at most 1.3%, with up to 96 long frames a minute |
| heavy | 0/3 and 0/3: 12.6% and 11.9% voice; the late joiner disconnected in 4 of 6 | 3/3 and 2/3 inconclusive (round trips of 230 to 270 ms with ~300 long frames a minute), 1 fail: the late joiner disconnected; network voice at most 4.0% |

- **Not fixed, found on the way:** under the heavy load a joining client's yard build (2.5 s on a quiet machine, `[Art] block built in ...`) stalls it past ENet's default timeout and the host drops it (the run-event soak's late joiner, one heavy smoke). A player on a slow machine would be dropped the same way. Raising the ENet peer timeout or building the block over several frames would fix it; neither is done here.
- **Not changed:** the limits; the round-trip, voice latency and playback, and run-event cue checks (still inconclusive on long frames); any RPC, replicated property or save format.
- **Revisit:** if Godot's ENet starts sizing its socket buffers, the join-time voice runs go away, and the 400 ms rule can be checked against what's left.
- **Review follow-up: a busy machine's stalls excuse the network's own problems by chance, so the judged share is scaled back up.** A reviewer added network-only hitches (gap 0.15 s, random times) to copies of the loaded run `after-load-4-soak` (65 to 96 long frames a minute): 45% of them were excused, because a 50 ms frame on any of three peers lands in most 0.4 s windows, and 3.5% injected (well over the 2% limit) passed 3 runs in 5. The old "inconclusive over half the run stalled" gate never tripped: those peers were stalled about 13% of the time. Now `soak_report.py` measures each peer's odds of a chance excuse directly: it puts a made-up hitch (a 0.12 s gap, the median measured) and a made-up lost voice packet (0.04 s of send times) at 2000 moments spread over the peer's session, the other peer taking turns, and runs each through the same stall rule. The network's share is the judged share / (1 - odds) (`h odds` and `v odds` in the table). Odds over 50% leave too little to scale from, so a check that fails then is inconclusive. Re-judging every "after" run: alone, hitch odds 1-5%, all pass; loaded, 3-47%, all 10 still pass; heavy, 97-100%, inconclusive as before. With 3.5% and 5% network-only hitches, or 8% lost voice packets, injected into each bot of every alone-1 and loaded run, 108 cases in 108 fail (after-load-4-soak's 3.5%: judged 3.0-3.6%).
- **Review follow-up: the throttle-limit fix is guarded.** The once-a-second `PEER_PACKET_THROTTLE` sample never saw the limit fall, so `net/dev/probe.gd` now reads `PEER_PACKET_THROTTLE_LIMIT` every frame toward every connected peer, loading included, and reports its minimum (`enet_throttle_limit_min`); `soak_report.py` fails a peer whose limit fell below 32. With `bandwidth_limit(0, 0)` commented out, the bot soak fails on Bot1 to Bot3 ("limit fell to 1 of 32") while its voice loss, 3.8 to 4.4%, would have passed; with it, every peer stays at 32.

### 2026-11-03: Flaky gameplay tests (flakes:play)

Five gameplay tests failed at random and cost every integrator reruns. Each was run 10 times before (the base commit, a copy in `build/base`) and after, with `ci_local -Jobs 1`, once on the shared machine as it was (20 to 40 other agents' Godots) and once loaded on top with 3 or 4 extra headless hosts beside the other copy's runs. Three of the five were the game; the rest were the tests or the machine.

- **toss H3 was the game: the aim help missed.** Its smallest nudge put the flight over the rail by a millimetre at a single tick (bottom at y 1.4002 against `BED_CATCH_RAIL_Y` 1.4); Jolt steps the real bag in single precision, it came by at 1.3992, missed the catch and bounced off the side wall. 30 helped 5 m tosses in a row: 29 missed. The help now aims for a sure catch (`bed_toss.gd sure_catch`: the flight goes in, and so do ones `TOSS_SURE_SHARE` (4%) softer and harder, the test the arc preview already used for gold, moved from `ui/toss_arc.gd SURE_SHARE` to `core/constants.gd`); a throw that would only scrape in is nudged too, and help that can't make it sure changes nothing. 30 of 30 now, across every wind-up it saves (0.48 to 0.52 s from 5 m; it used to claim 0.42 to 0.52). The client's prediction and the preview call the same function, so all three still agree.
- **The toss client's missing full bounce was the test.** H9's settle sweep (2,475 plans) ran in one frame: 5.5 s on a quiet machine, longer loaded, and ENet dropped the client during it (`[Net] peer ... left` right after H6), so it never heard the bounce or the finish. It now does `SWEEP_PLANS_PER_FRAME` (20) a frame.
- **The toss jump check was the test.** A 0.14 s frame moved a bag flying at 6 m/s 0.8 m, over `JUMP_LIMIT_M` (0.6); loaded, the host's E heave tripped it too (0.646). The check is now a speed: the step over the frame's own time, never less than 1/60 s and never more than the physics time Godot can step in one frame (`max_physics_steps_per_frame` + 1 ticks, 0.15 s at 60 Hz: a physics-driven bag can't go farther however long the frame runs). A 60 Hz frame is held to exactly the old 0.6 m, and no frame lets a bag move more than about 5 m; a shorter pop inside one long frame (3 m in a 0.1 s frame measures 0.5) is beyond this check, which is why `POP_LIMIT_M` watches the hand-offs where pops happen. `jump_at` records the step and the frame.
- **leaf_motion's snaps were the game.** A homing leaf whose spot moved in its last moments (a hand raking the pile out from under a leaf resting on it: the leaf 2.6 cm above its spot, closing at 0.36 m/s, when its 1.4 s ran out) jumped the rest. In its last `LATE_LAND_WINDOW` a homing leaf now closes the gap on the deadline, `dt / time left` of it each tick (`leaf_movers.gd`), so it arrives as its time runs out. Max handoff since: 1.97 to 2.0 cm (the late-landing tolerance), never a snap.
- **drive's prediction error was the game: a real rubber band.** The host ran one tick per frame-tick whatever the driver's inputs did: with the next input late (a long frame on either peer, then a burst) it drove on the last one, and those ticks stood for no input, so every later ack was that many ticks ahead of the driver's prediction and the driver's truck was yanked forward when the snapshot came (0.3 m on every quiet run from lost packets alone, 1.6 to 3.2 m loaded). Now every tick of a client's drive stands for one input in order (`chefty_drive.gd _sim_seq`, sent as the snapshot's ack): a late or lost input's tick is a stand-in, and the input, when it comes, only becomes the held one (`made_good`). Stand-ins go at most `CHEFTY_STAND_IN_MAX_SECONDS` (0.4) ahead; past that, or on the input timeout, the extra ticks stand for nothing, as before. No RPC changed: `_rpc_snap`'s ack keeps its type and meaning (the input the pose is after). The host's own driving and the autopilot are untouched. The probe reports `stood_in`, `made_good`, `forgiven` and, on the driver, `acks_ahead`.
- **brawl and gag: the test and the machine.** gag never failed (40 runs). brawl failed 5 times in 40, all loaded, all on the first punch: both peers build the block for 2 to 3 s right as the test starts, the client hadn't moved to its spot within `_place`'s 3 s, and the punch hit the spot it hadn't left (every later step passed). In two of the after-runs ENet then dropped the starved client outright. `_place` now waits up to `PLACE_WAIT_SECONDS` (10) for the target to arrive and says where it is when it doesn't. Arriving is being on the spot (0.3 m) or at rest near it (0.7 m, still for 0.3 s): at the 1.0 m grab spots the two bodies push apart and the target settles 0.49 m short for good, and waiting for the exact spot burned the full wait twice a run (a 36 s session against the 50 s ceiling). Now a session runs 16 to 18.5 s loaded, and the final code passed 10 of 10 loaded runs (3 extra busy hosts). drive_test finishes with a message instead of a script error when the client drops before it drives.
- **Measured, 10 runs each** (pass counts; the "after" loaded set ran under more load than the "before" one):

  | test | before, as is | after, as is | before, loaded | after, loaded |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | toss | 9 (H3) | 10 | 4 (client dropped in H9 x4, client jump 0.69/0.78 m, H3, host jump 0.646) | 8 (both: the client never loaded the world and was dropped) |
  | leaf_motion | 7 (snaps, to 3.5 cm) | 10 | 4 (up to 6 snaps, 3.8 cm) | 10 |
  | drive | 9 (1.56 m) | 10 (max 0.06 m) | 7 (2.97 m, 3.16 m, a dropped client) | 7 (max 0.28 m; the 3 failures had 15 to 55 comparable samples against the gate's 100: the driver lost physics ticks and fell behind real time) |
  | brawl | 10 | 10 | 8 (shove 0.00 x2) | 10 (final code, rerun; the first set was 7, all three failures before the longer wait) |
  | gag | 10 | 10 | 10 | 10 |

  Drive's prediction error on the quiet machine went from 0.29 to 0.32 m every run to 0.0 to 0.06 m. No limit was loosened; the jump check holds a 60 Hz frame to the same 0.6 m.
- **Still open: long main-thread stalls drop ENet peers.** Under heavy load a world or block build can stall a peer past ENet's timeout (about 5 s), and the session loses it (drive, toss and brawl each lost a client that way loaded). Real players would only see it on a badly overloaded machine; a longer ENet peer timeout, or building the block over frames, would cover it.
- **Still open: drive under parallel jobs fails on frame starvation, not prediction.** With `ci_local -Jobs 5` beside the other agents, drive failed once with the host's `drive_test.ok` true and the client's prediction error 0.0 m over 336 samples, on the client's `vehicle_view.max_gap_ms` (733.5 against the `< 400` gate): its fps fell to 5, and a 0.7 s frame is a 0.7 s gap in what it draws. The same starvation sinks the `> 100` samples gate (above). Both gates measure the machine there; they were left as they are, so an integrator who sees drive fail with a clean prediction error and a big gap should rerun it with fewer jobs.
- **Networking:** no RPC signature, replicated property, save format or yard/task generation change. The host's input stand-ins change which input `_rpc_snap`'s ack names (stand-in ticks count), host side only; a client of either build reads it the same way, so `BUILD_VERSION` needs no bump for this chunk.

Conflicts: `props/receipt_slip.gd` (R2b's `remember_rest()` already poses the slip once it's parented, which is flakes:soak's fix, so it stays with flakes:soak's comment), `net/smoke_test.gd` (R2b's timeout message and flakes:soak's progress timeout: the progress timeout stays and the message carries both), and the append-only ends of `core/constants.gd`, `docs/GAME.md` and this file (both sides kept; a duplicated R1b paragraph on the chunk side dropped).
