### 2026-11-03: Stowed looks and the draw are a local pose of replicated state; one glide for every hand-off (hotbar chunk D)

- **Stowed looks** (`props/prop_looks.gd` `STOWED`, `pose_stowed`): every peer poses a pocketed prop's look (the rake's `Pose`) in world space from its holder's animated `CartoonBody` part (`pose_part("torso")` / `"hips"`), so it bobs, leans and strides with them; `net_prop._physics_process` calls it instead of hiding `Visual`. Signs fan by their place in the stack (`stack_place`, the props in the same pocket slot by name). The owner's own copy goes on `CartoonBody.HEAD_LAYER` (`_own_layers`, remembering each part's built layers in meta `built_layers`), so the first-person camera skips it and every other camera sees it. It hides when the holder isn't drawn (`stowed_shown`: `is_body_drawn()`, `is_hidden()`, `is_sleeping()`, the body's `_sit_pose`), never by touching `Visual` (R2b hide's rule). `unstow` (from `net_prop`'s `pocketed_by` setter) puts layers and visibility back.
- **One glide for every hand-off** (`_blend_begin` / `_blend_put`): each pose names a mode (`hand:<peer>`, `stowed:<peer>`, `rest`); on a change the look glides from where it was last drawn (meta `pose_last`) to the new pose, over `HOTBAR_DRAW_SECONDS` into the hands and `HOTBAR_STOW_SECONDS` into a pocket, eased out and bowed `HOTBAR_DRAW_ARC` out from the holder and `HOTBAR_DRAW_RISE` up so it goes round the body. The start is kept in the holder's body frame when it came off a body (it walks with them) and in world space off the ground. Covered: pocket to hand, hand to pocket, ground to hand (a pickup no longer teleports), a set-up ladder to the shoulder, and pocket to ground (emptied pockets fly off the body). Not covered on purpose: hand to ground (drops and throws keep their own placement; the throw tests and the toss's own prediction own that path).
- **Clock:** `net_prop`'s `held_by` and `pocketed_by` setters stamp `drawn_msec` / `stowed_msec` on every peer (plus `drawn_from_pocket` / `stowed_from_hand`, from the other field or a 250 ms window, since the two fields can arrive in either order). A glide starts at a fresh stamp (`HOTBAR_STAMP_FRESH_MS`), so a pose that runs a tick late doesn't stretch it. `draw_dip(prop)` reads `drawn_msec`.
- **The rake** uses `RakeMotion.pose`'s `draw_dip` (as raking's contract asked): a negative dip lifts the head by `HOTBAR_RAKE_DRAW_LIFT`, easing to nothing, so it comes off the back lifted and plants; going away it is posed from the live swing lifted more and more, then glides to the back. `_carry_grips` moves its `grip_right` / `grip_left` metas with the glide, so both hands stay on the handle.
- **Hands go with it:** `grip_points` of a prop being put away (`is_stowing`) are its in-hand grips captured at the hand-off (meta `stow_grips`, in its own space) carried along, so `player.gd` (`_last_hand_prop`) and `first_person_arms.gd` (`_stowing`) keep the arms on it till it's away; while drawing (`is_drawing`) the first-person hands chase its grips at `HOTBAR_FP_DRAW_FOLLOW` instead of snapping, which reads as a reach down and a pull up.
- **Through your own eyes** a small tool comes up from (and goes down to) `HOTBAR_FP_DRAW_FROM` at the right hip, with `reset_physics_interpolation()` on the cut between the hip and the back (it's never on screen there; the stowed copy is on the head layer). The rake can't (its head is 1.5 m ahead, dropping it sinks it in the ground). Drawn, it takes the same path everyone sees, bowed wider and lower (`HOTBAR_FP_RAKE_ARC`, `HOTBAR_FP_RAKE_DROP`), coming in from the right. Put away, that path doesn't work: the handle slerps from planted to across the back through upright, so it rose through the middle of the view and vanished half way (review of `rec_r_1p` 61-64). Instead it keeps its planted angle and slides `HOTBAR_FP_RAKE_STOW_TO` (right, up, back) off the leaves and out past the right edge with no bow, then cuts onto the back on the head layer; re-recorded in `rec_r_1p` 61-66.
- **Fanned stacks ease:** a sign's place in the fan (offset and index) is eased toward the stack's current order (`_fan_shown`, `HOTBAR_FAN_SETTLE`), so when one is drawn or thrown the rest shuffle over in about 0.17 s instead of snapping 6 degrees and 2.5 cm in a tick (machine-gun sign throwing repeats it). A sign that wasn't worn a moment ago goes straight to its place (it glides in on its own).
- **Jangle:** `player._steps` plays `jangle` (at `HOTBAR_JANGLE_DB`) on every `HOTBAR_JANGLE_EVERY`-th footstep with `HOTBAR_JANGLE_AT` or more things pocketed. Local only, like the footsteps.
- **Networking:** none changed. No RPC, replicated property, save key, or yard/task generation; setters were added to the existing `held_by` and `pocketed_by` (they only stamp metas and restore the look). `BUILD_VERSION` needs no bump for this chunk.
- **Test:** `--hotbar-test` step 14 (port 7809): the stowed rake's model within 0.6 m of the holder's back, behind them and on the head layer; out of sight in a pile and back after; drawn by key 1, it glides into its in-hand pose within `HOTBAR_DRAW_SECONDS` + 0.1 s: after the first tick over 40% of the way is still to go, at least 3 samples lie part way, and no tick moves it more than half the length of the path it took (a pop is all of it in one tick; one over two ticks has a single sample part way). Steps are judged per physics tick, not scaled by real time; a take with a tick more than 1.6 ticks late can't be judged (a stall moves the msec-clocked glide further, like a pop), so it is put back and drawn again, 4 takes at most. Studio: `--subject=holds --stowed=1`, and `--walk=1` stops the bodies at full stride and holds them there.
- **Considered:** reparenting stowed looks under the body's bones (they belong to networked props; the look already lives under the prop and goes `top_level`), a separate rise-from-below dip for every kind (from the back a dip is a teleport for everyone else; the glide covers both), and hiding the owner's stowed copy with `visible` (the over-shoulder and stage cameras should still see it).

### 2026-11-03: Snacks are eaten from the snapshot; buffs are read where they act (shops chunk 6)

- **No new RPC.** A snack's eating, Dot's hand-over, the vending machine's drop and its stuck snack are all played on every peer from World/Shops' existing full snapshot (`_rpc_state`): the host records each player's last snack in `Shops.snacks` (`{peer: {item, buff, at}}`, `at` a server time) and the snapshot's `extra` carries it; the machine reads each item's stock going down (a sale: a snack of that item falls) and `Shops.vending_stuck` (the items it ate today, in order) getting longer (a snack of that item sticks). A peer that hears of a snack more than `SNACK_SHOW_WINDOW` (4 s) late doesn't play it, so a late joiner sees no stale meal. `snacks` and `vending_stuck` are not saved; `snacks` clears at sleep with the buffs, `vending_stuck` at the morning restock (a host who reloads mid-day finds the coils turned free, on every peer alike). **Protocol:** `_rpc_state`'s signature is unchanged, but its `extra` dictionary gained `snacks` and `vending_stuck`, so mixed builds would disagree on who's eating; the integrator should count it as a replicated-state change for `BUILD_VERSION`.
- **Watchers of the snapshot wait for it (review fix).** A client's `Shops` fields are defaults until its first `_rpc_state`, which needs a round trip; anything that plays *changes* in them (the machine) must not seed its "last seen" from those defaults, or the first real snapshot looks like a day of events (it replayed "the machine ate your money" to every joiner once the campaign's all-time `vending_eaten` was above 0). `Shops.synced` (local, not replicated: the host from its `_ready`, a client from its first snapshot) gates that: the machine builds its shelf, stuck snacks included, from the first real state with nothing played, and only reacts to changes after. A new `stock_day` refills it, so a stuck snack comes loose in the morning even when nothing sold that day.
- **One food, handed over (review fix).** The hand-over has a single food node, the buyer's `snack_eater`'s, on the server clock from the sale: it sits at the giver's point (`store_clerk.hand_point()`: Dot's prop hand, her arm aimed at the buyer; `vending_machine.tray_point()`), the buyer's hand reaches for it, and for the last `SNACK_PASS` seconds it travels into that hand (the first-person hand for your own view, the body's for everyone else's), then the same hand carries it to the mouth. Two copies swapped at a frame (Dot's and the buyer's) read as a teleport. The reaching is a new `CartoonBody` layer, `reach_at` / `reach_weight` ([right, left], laid over the walk, the gesture and the hold, with the hold's own aim-and-stretch), and `hands_off` lets a hand leave what it holds for a while (the hold fades out and back over `HOLD_BLEND_SECONDS`); the first-person arms have their own `reach_at`, and both views read the body's `snack_side` meta for which hand eats. Eating with a rake keeps it in the left hand; a one-handed hold in the right (gas can, sign, ladder) makes the left hand eat, mirrored in both views.
- **Buffs act where they act, from the same table.** `Shops.buff(peer, effect)` (server-time expiries, the same on every peer) is read by the owner's movement (`player.gd` `snack_buff`: walk and sprint speed, and the velocity cap, which Turbo lifts with it; the carry slowdown; the strain and faint times) and by the one rake tempo hook, `RakeStroke.cycle_seconds(peer)` (critic and raking.json: the R1b retune stays, a buff scales it). The swing's tempo already rides on each stroke event, so other peers and the leaf sounds follow a Turbo swing with no change. Hired hands' actor ids are never peers and take no buff.
- **The look of a buff is local presentation**, `player/snack_eater.gd`, a child of each player's CartoonBody so it runs after the body's own animation each tick (eyes, face, tint) and registers its gestures (`player/snack_gestures.gd`: eat, drink, belly_pat, turbo_shake, burp) through `CartoonBody.register_gesture`. The first-person arms read the same gesture clock and carry the food; the arms place it after they move the hand so it never trails by a frame. Your own buffs are HUD icons (`ui/buff_hud.gd`, a row in HudRows) drawn by the icon pipeline (`snack_<item>`, `buff_<id>` in `ui/icons.gd`), never text.
- **The food truck and the machine are local cosmetic nodes** built by `levels/dressing/shop_sites.gd` from `Constants.SHOP_SITES` (`props/vehicles.gd build_food_truck`, `props/vending_machine.gd`); the awning follows `Shops.is_open` on each peer like the hours sign. Dot's keeper spot moved inside the truck (`SHOP_SITES.lunch_money.keeper`, y 0.32: her feet below the box floor so the hatch frames her). The machine's front snacks are its own nodes; the rest of the cabinet is one cached mesh.
- **Considered:** an RPC per bite (rejected: the snapshot already arrives on every peer, and a separate message could arrive before the state it belongs to); a status line naming the buff (rejected: Taylan, no hand-holding text; the body and an icon carry it).
- **Dev:** scenarios `lunch_money`, `snak_o_matic`, `lunch_money_rake`, `snak_o_matic_can`, `snack_crash`, `snack_queasy` (`net/dev/scenarios.gd`), studio subjects `food_truck` and `vending`, and tour shots `lunch_money_hatch`, `lunch_money_truck`, `snak_o_matic`. `tools/play.ps1` now passes `--rank=` to the observer and friend too: the crew rank is crew-wide, and a watcher at a lower dev rank saw the truck shut that the host had open.

### 2026-11-03: Builds are spread over frames; the town streams its houses (driving chunk E)

- **The bug (F1, a real player-facing one):** a joining peer built the whole world in the frame its spawn arrived: Homie Depot, Satellite Annex B, the town or the legacy lot street, every walker, tree and grass chunk, plus the job yard and its block. Measured headless on this machine (loaded with other agents' runs): the client's longest frame in the join was 2466 ms (townmap), 2482 ms (toss), 3542 ms (bonk); windowed and on a slower PC it reached ENet's 5 s peer timeout, so the host dropped the player (F1 saw it in the soaks, smoke, toss and brawl).
- **Decision: a BuildQueue** (`levels/build_queue.gd`, one per world, process priority -100). Builders stay plain functions and hand work over with `BuildQueue.later(node, fn, label)`: queued when the tree has a queue, run at once otherwise (the studio, tools and tests that build by hand are unchanged). Each frame runs steps until `Constants.BUILD_MS_PER_FRAME` (8 ms) is spent, at least one. A step queued from inside a running step goes right after it (depth first), so things build in the order they always did and every rng is drawn in the same order (same scenery, same art hashes). A step whose node left the tree is skipped. `BuildQueue.finish(tree)` runs the rest now (Annex B's `ensure_set` uses it).
  - Steps are one tree, one walker (a cartoon body), one grass chunk, one doodle, one shop site, one Annex piece or clerk, one parcel plan, one parcel of a job's block (`BlockBuilder` async now goes through the queue instead of 2 parcels a frame; `ART_BLOCK_PARCELS_PER_FRAME` is gone), one town house's solids.
  - Not deferred: anything gameplay reads at once (colliders, the ground, the lot generator's yard, Chefty, the keepers' sites are deferred but their bodies come within a second). The town's colliders and the streamer's node are made in `_dress` itself; each house's solids are a step (the houses stand 380 m from the store).
  - **The host builds its world whole** (`world._ready` flushes the queue): nobody can be connected to it yet, and host-side tests and systems find the world as before. Joiners build over frames. Mid-session builds (a job's block, grass) are incremental on every peer.
  - **Prewarm:** `BuildQueue.prewarm()` before `join_enet` builds Chefty's truck meshes (cached by `props/vehicles.gd`), 150-200 ms that were in the join frame. Annex B's script is preloaded by `world.gd` so it compiles at boot, not mid-join.
  - **After, same machine, same load:** the client's longest join frame 370-600 ms headless (townmap 403-428, bonk 425, toss 571, work 585, art 372); no single queued step over ~160 ms headless under load (60 ms alone). The rest of that frame is the world scene, the systems' `_ready`s, the players' bodies and the lot generator. Windowed, driving Chefty from the store into Maple Row while the town streamed: longest frame 44 ms.
- **ENet timeout raised anyway:** `Net._open_throttle` sets every link to `ENET_TIMEOUT_LIMIT` 32, `ENET_TIMEOUT_MIN_MS` 12000, `ENET_TIMEOUT_MAX_MS` 30000 (ENet's minimum was 5 s). The join frame is now well under a second headless, but first-time shader compiles on a slow PC's first frames aren't ours to split; 12 s is the margin. A peer that really vanished is now noticed after 12-30 s instead of 5-30 s; a graceful quit is still immediate.
- **Check:** the probe reports `join_stall_ms` (the longest frame in the 30 s after a peer is first linked) and `build_queue` stats; the townmap CI step requires the client's `join_stall_ms` under 1500 ms (the old single-frame build was 2.4 s+), and townmap_test requires the world's queue to drain with no step over 400 ms.
- **The town streamer** (`levels/town/town_streamer.gd`): chunks of 3 slots along one side of a street (36 chunks). Every 0.25 s, by the distance from the camera to a chunk's nearest parcel: LOD 0 (`LotBuilder` "near": the art house and its yard without small clutter) under 110 m, LOD 1 ("far") under 260 m, a skyline (the chunk's `LotBuilder.skyline_boxes` merged into one mesh) under 420 m, nothing past that; 12 m of hysteresis. A level is built off the tree, one parcel a frame under `STREAM_BUILD_MS_PER_FRAME` (3 ms; a parcel is 7-36 ms, so in practice one a frame), nearest chunk first, added in one `add_child`, and cross-faded with the level it replaces over 0.6 s (`GeometryInstance3D.transparency`); the old level stays until the new one is in (never a pop to empty), and the neighboring level is kept. Headless peers keep the bookkeeping only. `settle_now(at)` builds everything for a screenshot (the capture tour).
  - **"Near", not "full", at LOD 0:** a full parcel took 37-113 ms to build; "near" 8-31 ms.
  - **Colliders on art's builders:** `TownColliders` uses `LotBuilder.colliders` with the same request as the visuals (`TownStreamer.parcel_request`: a yard slot is drawn as its job yard's house, `neighbor` false; a house across the street may be a neighbors-only archetype), so what you see is what you hit.
  - Satellite Annex B (BorderZone) is never streamed: it is its own always-loaded set.
  - **Perf, windowed, this machine under load:** 916 draw calls looking over the town square, up to 1565 on Maple Row; 181 parcel builds on the drive from the store, slowest 35.8 ms, longest frame while building 44.2 ms.
- **Considered:** building on worker threads (the builders share static mesh caches and add to in-tree parents: not thread safe); GDScript coroutines with an `await` budget in every builder (a coroutine's return value can't be used without `await`, so every builder that returns a node would change signature, the studio and tools included); visibility ranges for the LOD swap (per-mesh distances inside a chunk disagree, and art's trees already use their own ranges and visibility parents).
- **Revisit if:** the join frame needs to go below ~300 ms (the lot generator and the players' bodies are what's left), or the town grows past what one parcel a frame can keep up with at Chefty's top speed.
- **Where this departs from critic C24, on purpose (for the integrator to accept or turn into a chunk):** (1) LOD 0 is `LotBuilder` "near", not "full" (full is 37-113 ms a parcel, past any frame budget; building full over two frames would need `LotBuilder.build` split into resumable steps); (2) the roads are still `town_dressing`'s `neighborhood.road` strips, not `StreetBuilder.segment`, and are always built (BuildQueue steps), not streamed; (3) the swap is a 0.6 s transparency cross-fade between whole levels instead of visibility ranges (see Considered). Follow-up if wanted: a `driving:E2` chunk for StreetBuilder roads and a two-frame full LOD 0.
- **Review fixes (same day):**
  - **A job site placed or cleared mid-build:** `set_slot_hidden` only reaches built levels, so a slot hidden while its chunk's level was still being built off the tree kept the town's copy of the house standing in the job yard (or missing after). `_finish_job` now re-reads every slot's hidden state before the level goes in. `townmap_test` drives the build path directly (`build_level_now`, even headless) and lands the hide and the show mid-build; without the fix it fails.
  - **A yard's dressing is its own build step:** the parcel in one step, its leaves and sign in the next (a grown-back lawn's leaves are about 900 leaf cards, 7 ms headless), so one frame never pays for both. Windowed drive from the store (`build\play\rec_drive3_1p`): 217 builds, slowest 42.6 ms, longest frame while building 51.9 ms (this machine under load; 72 ms with leaves in the parcel's step).
  - **The observer camera and the town's one static body** (`net/dev/remote_play.gd`, a dev tool): a ray hit on `TownStatic` was judged thin or wide by the whole body, which holds the ground, so a yard trunk or a lamp post counted as a wall and pulled the camera in. Thinness is now judged by the shape the ray hit (`_thin_hit_shape`), and the ray goes on past it. The pull-ins left in `rec_drive3_3p` (#256, #378, #391) are its designed ones: a yard tree's crown box, a lamp at the camera's spot, a parked car, all from the hand-driven route cutting across lawns and sidewalks.
  - **Not fixed here (driving camera):** Chefty's chase camera (`player/vehicle_camera.gd`) has no occlusion at all, so driving across a lawn under a yard tree puts it inside the crown for a few frames (`rec_drive3_1p` #390). Art's trees now stand in the town's yards, so this shows where it couldn't before. Needs a chase-camera chunk (pull in on solids, fade crowns).


### 2026-11-03: W7 integration (icons:F, hotbar:D, shops:5, shops:6, driving:E)

- **Merged** in order: icons:F, hotbar:D, shops:5, shops:6, driving:E, each with `--no-ff` on top of F1. Conflicts, all resolved by keeping both sides: the append-only ends of `core/constants.gd`, `docs/DECISIONS.md` and `docs/PLAYTEST.md`; `props/prop_looks.gd` `pose_held` (shops:5's junk set-down ease and hotbar:D's `_note_rest` / new `_place_held(prop, look, holder, entry)` both kept); `player/player.gd`'s pickup hint (icons:F's `source` plus shops:5's `display_name`); Rusty's yard and gate classes beside Lunch Money's `HatchWatcher` in `levels/dressing/shop_sites.gd`; junk and food models in `props/shop_models.gd`; both sound sets (`audio/sound_bank.gd`, `tools/make_sfx.py`), studio subjects and scenarios. `Shops._restock` keeps shops:5's rule (a haggle sticks; only gone pieces are forgotten) and clears shops:6's `vending_stuck`. driving:E's `BuildQueue` now builds every shop site (Rusty's yard, the food truck and the Snak-o-Matic included) as one step each; the host flushes the queue in `_ready`, so host-side shop code finds them as before.
- **One integration fix:** in `--shop-test` the client bought its snack after Rusty's steps, but the host waits for the snack before it does Rusty's steps and cues the client; the client half now snacks first, then waits for Rusty's cue.
- **BUILD_VERSION 0.4.10 → 0.4.11:** shops:5 added RPCs (`Shops._rpc_request_sell`, `JunkBed._rpc_junk` / `_rpc_request_unload`, `net_prop._rpc_junk_land`) and junk on yards; shops:6 added `snacks` and `vending_stuck` to `_rpc_state`'s extra. icons:F, hotbar:D and driving:E changed no protocol.
- **Full suite** (`tools\ci_local.ps1`, PortOffset 0, with soaks): 43 of 43 pass on the first run, 449 s, the bot soaks 63 s and 188 s.
- **F1's open item is closed by driving:E:** a joiner's world is built over frames and each ENet link's timeout is raised (12-30 s), so a slow build no longer drops a peer.
### 2026-11-03: R2c stage: stale poses, your own body, the caught body, the boarding hand-over (R2c chunk stage)

- **Why:** the second motion audit of build 0.4.10 (audit/r2b/audit.json): the escort camera filmed an empty field about 1,400 m away (2 of 3 natural escorts, and one frame in the lunge take), your own head filled the swing out of first person, the caught body held one stiff pose, a rescue bonk had no reaction, a far hand-over slid the rider 18 m into the van, bystanders crowded the two-shot.
- **The 1,400 m escort, proven:** a debug print in StageCam caught it (build `rec_b_escort2_1p`, a second escort after a ride to Annex B): on the push frame `EscortFollow`'s interpolated transform was (-5.9, 2.2, 1011.9), Annex B, while its real one was (1001.5, 1.3, 4.7); the camera went to (-0.98, 2.17, 1006.97) on frame 134, the same point as the audit's `rec_e2e_raid2_1p` frame 1112. `Node3D.get_global_transform_interpolated()` returns the physics interpolation's cache from the last rendered frame, so a node moved by hand since then reads where it was. The follow marker had last followed the head NICE BOY to Annex B. On that frame `_keep_clear` swept from the Annex to the eye and pinned it there, and the speed clamp then glided it back at 7 m/s for the rest of the walk. R2b's `_anchor_point` guard covered the crew's anchor, not the marker the shot hangs off. **Fix:** the Encounter's own markers (TwoShot, EscortFollow, each hold) have physics interpolation off (`_placed_by_hand`); and every pose StageCam and StageBody read goes through `StageCam.drawn_pose()`, which takes the real transform for a node that isn't interpolated, or whose cache is more than `STAGE_CAM_STALE_METERS` off (logged as `stale_reads`). After: `rec_a_escort3_1p` (escort, ride to the Annex, escort again) stays 1.5-3 m behind the pair from frame 135.
- **Your own body (StageCam):** a shot whose outgoing eye starts within `STAGE_SELF_REVEAL_NEAR` of your head (`_from_inside`), or the blend back into it, culls your own body until the eye is that far away, then dissolves it in by `STAGE_SELF_REVEAL_FAR`. The dissolve is a screen-door `dissolve` instance uniform in `props/stylized.gd`'s shader (Bayer 4x4 discard), so it stays in the opaque pass: no sorting, no transparent pipeline, no halo of back faces. `StageCam.fade_geometry()` uses the dissolve on stylized meshes and `transparency` on anything else. The mask used to run in `_retop` before `_update` had placed the eye, so it judged from where the camera was left; it now runs only after the eye is placed.
- **Frame-out:** a shot may list `subjects` (the gotcha two-shot and the escort's follow shot: you and him) and `frame_out_held`; bodies in `STAGE_FRAME_OUT_GROUPS` that come into its frustum nearer than the subjects' far side plus `STAGE_FRAME_OUT_BEYOND`, and props a subject holds, dissolve out over `STAGE_FRAME_OUT_SECONDS`, and back in when the shot lets go. It is local (only the caught player's view). The in-frustum test tries each body's flanks too (`STAGE_FRAME_OUT_HALF_WIDTH`): a body at the edge, its middle just out, flickered in and out. **Teammates (review fix):** frame-out first dissolved anyone in the "players" group too, so a friend punching or whacking the NICE BOY off you (they must stand within `PUNCH_REACH` of him) was invisible in your own two-shot: he reeled from nobody. Now a player, and another caught pair (`"frame_out_partner"`: the Encounter's `_pair_partner` names a NICE BOY's captive and a captive's NICE BOY), only fades when `_team_blocks`: standing in the lens (flat, within `STAGE_FRAME_OUT_TEAM_LENS`) or in front of a subject (`STAGE_FRAME_OUT_TEAM_IN_FRONT` nearer the eye and overlapping on screen), with `STAGE_FRAME_OUT_TEAM_SLACK` of hysteresis once faded; a pair fades together. A proximity exemption ("within punch reach of the pair") was tried and dropped: a bystander the escort walked past then stood in front of the pair for a second (`rec_j_two_fr` 277-295). After: `rec_n_whack_fr` 108-127 (the captive's own view of a gnome whack: the rescuer on screen from 112, the stars and the clipboard), `rec_k_two_fr` 273-315 (another caught pair passing in front fades as one and comes back as one).
- **The caught body:** `player/caught_pose.gd` rewritten (local, cosmetic): held squirms, a visible tremble, `react()` (the bark), `flinch(down)` (the sticker), side-eye at `captor` (the face turns `FACE_AT_CAPTOR` of the way), a glance at `camera_point` (the two-shot's eye from `Encounter._two_shot_eye`, the same on every peer) every third squirm. Face and eyes are set in `_process`, after CharacterLife (an older child) picks its own glance. `CATCH_VICTIM_CHEAT_TURN` (0.85 rad) replaces `CATCH_CHEAT_TURN` for the caught player in `_staging` (his stays 0.5); `_staging` runs on every peer from the catch's args, so no RPC changes.
- **Staged bodies:** `set_staged(..., clear_of)` (player.gd, StageBody): the first blend goes round that node in its polar frame (never under `STAGE_BODY_CLEAR`); the escort link passes the NICE BOY.
- **Rescue (every peer, `_rpc_freed`):** for a bonk or a punch, the crew's Poser plays the `bonked` shot (npcs/crew_gestures.gd; a shot, so the CHASE mode's `hold("")` can't cut it), with `dizzy(RESCUE_DIZZY_SECONDS)`; the host gives him `RESCUE_KNOCKBACK_SPEED` away from the freed player (his stun slides it out; away from the thrower knocked him into the friend's face); the freed body is staged on its hold for `RESCUE_STEP_SECONDS`, `RESCUE_STEP_METERS` out of reach (swept against walls), facing the rescuer, and the freed player's shots pop over `RESCUE_CAM_BLEND`.
- **Escort follow:** `_move_follow` swings the follow marker by `ESCORT_CAM_BIASES` (eased at `ESCORT_CAM_BIAS_RATE`) to the nearest offset whose sweep from the pair to the eye is clear and that has `ESCORT_CAM_ROOM` round the eye; computed only while the local player is escorted or boarding. At the door itself the van's flank still fills the left of the frame for the last half second before the iris (`rec_v2_lunge_1p` 276-280): partly better, not fixed.
- **Boarding from afar:** at BOARDING, a rider further than `BOARD_NEAR_METERS` from the door step fades out on every peer (`_fade_body`, `BOARD_FADE_SECONDS`) and back in `BOARD_FADE_BACK` later. The host boards them with the Shuttle's existing `BOARD_IRIS` mode (`host_board(peer, behind_iris)`, a new optional argument), so they're seated behind their iris instead of stepping up from metres away. The local escort's iris then closes at once, without the `BOARD_IRIS_DELAY` hand-up. Seen before the fix in `rec_r_two_1p` (a catch with no NICE BOY free: 18 m in 14 frames, through a tree); after, the iris shuts at 6.0 s and the cut is at 6.4 s behind it. **Decided once (review fix):** each peer first judged "far" from its own lagged drawn body, and the 1.6 m line sits inside a normal door arrival (he stops within `CATCH_DOOR_REACH`, you're `ESCORT_SIDE_OFFSET` to his side), so under `--sim-lag=150` an observer dissolved a captive the host was stepping up (`rec_r_lagraid_3p` 296-298). The host decides in `_host_at_door` and sends it as `{"far": bool}` in BOARDING's args (the args Dictionary already existed, so the RPC signature is unchanged; a late joiner gets it in the snapshot's args); `_rpc_stage` reads it and never measures. After, under `--sim-lag=150 --sim-loss=2`: `rec_f_escortlag_3p` 330-336 (a door hand-over, the body solid as he hands it up), `rec_f_twofar_3p` 164-176 (scenario `two_caught_far`: the far one dissolves).
- **The itemized row:** `hotbar._lay_flat` gives a long item (longest side over `CATCH_ROW_LAY_FLAT_RATIO` times its shortest) an angular velocity that tips its long axis level across its flight.
- **Sound:** `windup_squeak` (tools/sfx_r2_crew.py, sound_bank) plays where he is when the wind-up starts. `lunge_whoosh` starts on a broadband fwip, with its swish starting at 1.5 kHz instead of 600 Hz.
- **The encounter_test step 2 flake: two measurement errors fixed, the flake still open.** The client sampled the drawn yaw of a NICE BOY (its Visual, placed by the smoother once per rendered frame) once per physics tick, at physics priority 100. Two things read as turning that was never drawn. (1) A frame that covered several ticks (under load the engine runs up to 8 a frame) showed all their turning between two samples. (2) A synchronizer update moves the body, the Visual's parent, before the smoother puts the Visual back in that frame's `_process`, and the sample could fall between the two. The client now samples once per rendered frame at `process_priority` 4096 (after every placement), and divides that frame's turn by the physics ticks the frame really ran, never by wall time (review fix: an earlier version of this chunk divided by wall time too, which softened exactly the one-frame snaps drawn after a stall). The limit (3 ticks of `CREW_TURN_RATE`) is unchanged. **Still failing at times, with a one-tick turn** (the reviewer's run: 0.628 rad against 0.355, Crew2 in INSPECT, over 1 tick). Not proven, but the likeliest cause: NetSmoother stamps each update by its arrival tick, so updates bunched by a host hitch or a resend are interpolated across one tick instead of the 2-6 they cover (0.628 rad is 5-6 ticks of `CREW_TURN_RATE`), and the host's own per-tick measure passes. Fixing it means stamping by send time (a replicated property, so a `BUILD_VERSION` bump) or de-jittering arrival stamps in NetSmoother, which every remote body shares; neither is in this chunk.
- **Networking, saves:** no RPC signature, replicated property, save format or yard/task generation change. BOARDING's stage args gained a `far` key (the args Dictionary already crossed the wire; an old peer would read it as absent, i.e. near). `Shuttle.host_board` gained an optional argument (host-local). New sound id `windup_squeak`.
- **Tests and tools:** scenarios `two_caught` (needs `-Friend`: two NICE BOYS, so two two-shots and two escorts at once; it raises the dev save's heat past `HEAT_PER_EXTRA_CREW` before the call) and `two_caught_far` (needs `-Friend`: no NICE BOY free for you, the long hand-over), both in playtest_tools_test's two-player list. encounter_test's client yaw sampling as above.
### 2026-11-03: the Courtesy Shuttle's seams (R2c van)

Decided by the agent after the second motion audit of 0.4.10 (audit\r2b, van and e2e findings). Recordings under `build\play*\rec_*` (git-ignored).

- **StageCam shots can ride a moving frame** (`ride`, a Node3D, optional; player/stage_cam.gd). A blend into such a shot keeps its start in that frame (`_from` is stored relative to it and re-read every frame), so a blend between two shots inside a moving van moves with the van. Root cause of `dolly-exits-van`: the seat-to-cab blend interpolated from a world-space start toward an anchor doing 12 m/s, so the eye trailed out of the van's back (`rec_ride_1p` 264-292 before, 250-290 after: inside the cabin throughout). The Shuttle passes `ride: trip.look` on the seat and cab shots.
- **The straggler is a staged timeline, not the crew brain's walk** (`CREW_RUN`, entry mode 4 in shuttle.gd's crew seats; `_host_send_stragglers`). When a raid van creeps, every NICE BOY on the lawn gets a sprint whose length is a pure function of where he stood, the van's planned pose and the back doors' timeline, so every peer draws the same run; the back doors shut as he arrives, he hops onto `RearStep`, and the creep ends (DEPART) only after the hop. Before, `_host_creep` waited for the recall walk to reach the bumper (e2e run 2: never, the creep timed out at 6 s), and the scenario's straggler tick turned his physics off, so his staging never ran (`rec_strag_1p` 112-340 before; 30-78 after).
- **Arrivals start out of sight only when someone could see them** (`_anyone_watching`): a real player in the arrival's zone (the yard and the town count as one street), not riding. Otherwise the drop-off arrival is timed to `RETURN_PULL_UP` and Annex B's is the short roll-in, so riders' irises aren't shut for an unseen drive. The playtest observer (`Net.is_watcher`) doesn't count: its spectator camera cuts with the rider it follows.
- **The Annex hands riders to the Shuttle one at a time** (`host_start_return(peers, to, hold)`, `host_release_return()`): `hold` says more of the cohort is still inside; the van goes `VAN_RETURN_GO` after its last rider sits once nothing holds it (`RETURN_WAIT` stays the flag-off wait). Closing time sends `_rpc_closing_iris` (a new RPC on World/Annex: a door iris with no open) and seats the rider behind it; the Shuttle's iris boarding carries that shut over and opens it on the seat. The receipt slip is held up in front of the StageCam when that's the local view (props/receipt_slip.gd).
- **The van's open side has a collider** (`VanLook/Doorway`, an AnimatableBody3D beside the five `Solid` boxes, toggled with them): walking can't take anyone into the aisle; staged moves set transforms and pass through it.
- **Annex B's street** is 380 m of road (`VAN_ANNEX_STREET_EXTRA`) over the surroundings' ground, and the ride home's ForecourtCurb stop is `VAN_RETURN_CURB_SHIFT` up the curb from the marker.
- **Networking:** one new RPC (`Annex._rpc_closing_iris`), and new values in existing ones: `_rpc_crew_seat` carries mode 4 (`CREW_RUN`) with a world position, and the return trip's info carries `hold`. A mixed build would disagree, so `BUILD_VERSION` needs a bump at integration. No replicated property, save format, or yard/task generation change.
- **Review fixes (same chunk, after the r2c:van review).**
  - **The straggler's run is a navmesh way the host plans once** (`_plan_straggler_run`, sent by a new RPC `_rpc_crew_run(trip, crew, at, path, run)`): `NavigationServer3D.map_get_path` on the crew's map from where he stands to just behind the bumper where the van will be when he arrives (aimed three times, since the van creeps meanwhile), then straight from the mesh's edge onto the street. He runs it at `VAN_STRAGGLER_RUN_SPEED` with `VAN_STRAGGLER_RAMP` s up to speed and down again, faces `VAN_STRAGGLER_LOOKAHEAD` m along it, and over the last 40 % is eased onto the van's real bumper should the creep be cut short. The creep lasts as long as the longest planned run plus the hop (runs capped at `VAN_STRAGGLER_RUN_MAX` = 10 s; beyond that he runs faster, still on the mesh). Before, the run was a straight lerp capped at 4.2 s: a straggler behind the house went through it at about 15 m/s. Seen in `van_straggler_far` (new scenario: the straggler placed behind the house), `rec_strfar_3p` 36-282 (round the house's side, the porch and across the lawn to the bumper) and `rec_strfar_1p` 240-345 (doors shut at 312, on the bumper at 330, it drives off at 343). `shuttle_test` checks `straggler_pace` (a navmesh way, at no more than the run speed).
  - **The return van parks clear and its door is a way in.** `VAN_RETURN_CURB_SHIFT` 4.5 → 9.0 m, so its nose is out of the walk from the turnstile (`rec_turnA_1p` 25-50: at the left edge of the view, then gone). A rider still in their forecourt beat who walks up within `VAN_RETURN_STEP_REACH` of its open door is seated there by a step, no iris (`Shuttle.at_return_door`, `host_start_return(..., step = true)`, polled by `Annex._maybe_return`): `rec_turnB2_1p` 90-124. The receipt slip now follows whichever of the player's views is current, frame to frame (`Slip.follow_view`): it hung in the doorway while the step's StageCam took over.
  - **Out of a seat by the aisle** (`_step_out`): a curve through `VAN_STEP_OUT_TURN_X` at the landing's place along the van, so the body (and the eyes) come out level with where they land; and an unbuckled rider lands `VAN_UNBUCKLE_BESIDE` = 1.15 m (from 0.9) from the rescuer. The friend's eyes pass the rescuer at 1.1 m (`rec_unb3_fr` 76-110; before, `rec_unb_fr` 118-122 went through his cap).
  - **The cab shot leans in between the front seats** (`FRESHENER_CAM` (0, 2.55, -1.25), looking at (0, 2.5, -4)): with a NICE BOY riding shotgun, `rec_cab2_1p` 297-340 shows both heads at the edges and the road and daisy in the middle (before: `rec_cab_1p` 306, two heads filling the lower half).
  - **The van's brakes are their own sound** (`van_brake`, tools/make_sfx.py: a 2 ms bite, then a 1.05 s squeal; bank `unit_size` `VAN_BRAKE_UNIT_SIZE` = 16 like the engine). Root cause of the missing onsets: the van borrowed Chefty's `skid`, normalized to a 0.45 peak with a 20 ms attack, played at -10 dB with the default 8 m unit size, which put it about 13 dB under its own engine at 20 m and level with the engine in a rider's seat (sync.json's onset needs +6 dB). Now `VAN_BRAKE_DB` 0 and the settle squeal -6 dB: `rec_brakewarn_1p` (curb) 16 ms and 14 ms, `rec_brakedrop_1p` (seat) 40 ms and 20 ms.
  - **`_anyone_watching` skips anyone inside Annex B** (walking in, at the windows, or a visitor in the lobby; `_indoors_at_annex`): the forecourt and the lobby are one zone, and a friend filling in forms kept the riders' iris shut for a 90 m arrival nobody could see.
  - **Networking:** one more new RPC (`Shuttle._rpc_crew_run`), which replaces `_rpc_crew_seat`'s use for `CREW_RUN`. Still no replicated property, save format, or yard/task generation change.
### 2026-11-03: hiding's seams: poses follow the body, sounds at your ears skip the muffle, late contact sounds still play (R2c hide)

- **Why:** the second motion audit (audit/r2b/audit.json, HIDING) found the beats working and the joins between them broken: a pile diver's head left floating at standing height on other screens (the head hangs off the player's `Visual/Head`, which no gesture moved), a remote hedge dive standing upright on the crown under lag (the dive gesture ran on the clock from the host's word, the body on the replicated arc), the find with no hands (he stood 1.2 m out, in the picket fence, and the haul raised his arms overhead), the 1P leaf frame dropped in one frame, the poke and peek sounds buried under the hider's own muffle, and a lagged observer missing both grab sounds (dropped as later than `BEAT_SKIP`).
- **Heads ride the torso during hide moves:** `player/hide_view.gd _carry_head` (in its `LateTick`, priority 150, after the body's own tick) puts `Visual/Head` on the torso's collar while a hide gesture plays, while the body is posed into a spot by `_pose_remote_dive`, or while it's sunk (`_apply_drop`), eased on and off over `HIDE_HEAD_CARRY_EASE`. Never for your own first-person camera, which lives in that head. The CartoonBody's sink into a pile, hedge or bin is one value, `_apply_drop`, written to `Visual/CartoonBody.position.y` only while it's non-zero or returning to zero.
- **Other peers pose a dive from the arc's progress, not time:** on a non-owner, `on_hidden` records where the drawn body was; `_pose_remote_dive` then calls the gesture's pose function itself (`_HideGestures._HideGesturePoses.hedge_dive` and kin) at a phase from how far the drawn `Visual` has come toward `inside_point`, and sinks it by `HIDE_HEDGE_CROUCH` / `HIDE_BIN_CROUCH`. `body_should_draw` hides it at `HIDE_REMOTE_DIVE_IN` of the way (or `HIDE_REMOTE_DIVE_SLACK` past the dive) and latches: the owner's climb-out reaches a peer before the host's word, and used to draw them for a frame at the hedge face. A climb-out or haul on other peers rises out of the same crouch as the drawn body clears the spot.
- **The NICE BOY's poke is fitted to where he stands:** `props/hide_spot.gd peer_pose` takes the hider's eye and, for a spot, bends only as far as needed to bring his face `HIDE_PEER_FACE_GAP` from it and drops his hips to its height; over a pile he squats and bends over (`HIDE_PEER_PILE_FACE_UP`). His arms aim at `grip_points()` (the collar in the gap, then the hider's shoulders as they come out) with the same shoulder-to-target turn the hold system uses; held props go to his belt and back (`_stow`, `release_props`). The host stands him `HIDE_INSPECT_REACH` out (`inspect_point`; over a pile `pile_inspect_point` stands him `HIDE_PILE_STAND` out from the hider's eye) and sets the found hider down `HIDE_FOUND_LAND_SIDE` along the face (a shed's `HIDE_FOUND_LAND_SHED` off the latch side; a pile's `HIDE_PILE_PULL_STANDOFF` from him).
- **Sounds at the hider's own ears skip the muffle:** `audio/sfx.gd play_clear(id, at, db)` plays a positional one-shot on the `SfxClear` bus (an effect; a bank entry on another bus keeps it). `SfxClear` sends to Master with no effects, so the hiding low-pass on Sfx never touches it, and follows Sfx's volume and mute (`audio/buses.gd FOLLOWS` / `sync_followers()`, called from `ensure()` and `core/settings.gd _apply_audio`), so the effects slider still sets it and the Chatter slider (babble, barks, `vo_*`, as critic.md's C2 says) doesn't. It extends critic.md's bus list by one bus with no settings key. It was Chatter at first, which put these effects under the wrong slider. `props/hide_spot.gd _sound` uses it for anything at the spot you're in; the pile's pats and the pile's snatch use it on the hider's machine. A new `hedge_poke` (tools/sfx_r2_hide.py: a hard twig crack on the first sample) replaces the soft rustle for his pushes.
- **Contact sounds play while their moment is on screen:** a find's snatch and open sound play if the find arrives up to `HIDE_FOUND_REVEAL + HIDE_PULL_SECONDS` late (it was `BEAT_SKIP`, 0.5 s); `encounter.gd` plays `gotcha_grab` up to `CATCH_HOLD_END` late. Visual snapping rules are unchanged.
- **Leaving a spot never drops a layer in one frame:** the shell and the 2D frame are swept open (`HIDE_SHELL_OPEN_SECONDS`, `HIDE_SHELL_OPEN_SPREAD`; a find tears them open over `HIDE_FOUND_TEAR_SHARE` of the reveal), and the spot's outside is drawn again only once the eye is `HIDE_SHELL_CLEAR` out of the spot's box.
- **The pile's eye and mound read the leaf view:** `leaves/leaf_renderer.gd surface_y` gives the heap's height; the mound sits `HIDE_PILE_MOUND_SEAT` into it and the eye walks out along the dive (up to `HIDE_PILE_FACE_MAX`) to where the heap is no taller than it.
- **Review fixes: the pile meets at one place.** The eye used to walk up to 2 m out, so on other screens the NICE BOY (posed to that eye) bent over bare leaves well off the mound. Now the owner's `_pile_landing` carries the cannonball up to `HIDE_PILE_DIVE_REACH` farther into a deep heap (so its face is `HIDE_PILE_FACE` ahead; a layer-1 ray keeps it out of walls and fences), the eye's walk stops at `HIDE_PILE_FACE_MAX` 0.85 m (inside the mound), and where the heap is still taller than the eye there, the eye rises to `HIDE_PILE_EYE_OVER` over its skin (`_eye_rise`; from under the skin the lawn beneath showed as a green band). His grips go to the eye itself, in the mound's face. The landing is measured once, when you dive: a heap still growing then (a pile raked a moment ago) leaves the eye peering over a taller skin.
- **Review fixes: the hider's own lens.** A new global shader parameter `leaf_clear` (project.godot; vec4 world xyz and radius) folds the leaf flakes (`leaves/leaf_shaders.gd` FLAKE, hero and mover) within its radius into their middles. Only `hide_view._present_lens_clear` sets it: the owner in first person in or around a pile, `HIDE_PILE_LENS_CLEAR` round the drawn camera, for 0.6 s after; zero otherwise. The pile's 3D shell rides the moving eye (yaw and pitch) and rises from `HIDE_PILE_SHELL_RISE` below over `HIDE_PILE_SHELL_RISE_SECONDS` instead of appearing; the eye on its way in stays `HIDE_PILE_EYE_OVER` over the heap's skin and never climbs back over a crest; the dive's burst keeps `HIDE_PILE_BURST_CLEAR` off the lens (`hide_spot.burst` takes a lens point).
- **Review fixes: legs inside a spot.** `_apply_drop` straightens the legs (eased to rest by `HIDE_LEGS_HANG_FROM` of the sink) whenever a body is sunk into a hedge or bin, after the gesture's tick, so another peer never sees the climb-out's kick or the dive's tuck push the shoes out of the hedge's foot. A pile's mound flattens over `HIDE_PILE_MOUND_FLATTEN` where it lay (reparented off the body) instead of being freed in one frame.
- **Tools:** `remote_play.gd` state has `music` (track; while hiding asks for it, `hide`, `at` and `in_hide`), so a recording's events.json shows whether the chase music is inside its "hide!" section. A `pile_found` scenario (seen diving into a raked pile; he walks over, pats and finds you).
- **Networking, saves:** no RPC, replicated property or save field changed; no yard or task generation change. Host behaviour changed in where a NICE BOY stands to poke (`inspect_point`, `pile_inspect_point`) and where a found hider lands (`found_landing`), so host and clients should run the same build for the poses to meet. One new sound id (`hedge_poke`).
- **Test:** `--hide-test` (port 7828) adds: the remote diver's head stays on their shoulders (within 0.12 m of the collar) on another peer; his right mitt is within 0.3 m of the hider's collar when he pulls, standing within reach of the gap; the pile's eye is within `HIDE_PILE_FACE_MAX` of the hider and he stands within `HIDE_PILE_FACE_MAX + HIDE_PILE_STAND`; `SfxClear` sends to Master, has no effects and follows Sfx's volume.
### 2026-11-03: Annex B's people: states walk home, stamps are puppets of hands, staff are solid (R2c annex)

- **A staff state that wants him seated walks him there first** (`npcs/clerk.gd` `_wants_home`, `_homing`): play() of a seated state (or the Head NICE BOY's approval, or a stretch) while the body is elsewhere routes him home at `CLERK_HOME_SPEED` and poses the state once there. Root cause of the empty-desk approval and the empty open window: DESK and OPEN played `counter_idle` with no walk, so a body that had walked away stayed where it stopped. A walk fitted to a deadline (`_fit_speed`: a break's walk out arrives `CLERK_BREAK_ARRIVE_SHARE` in; `hurry_back` is seated by the end of RETURN) uses annex.gd's `arrive_in` / `seconds` play args, which it computes from the schedule segment.
- **The host never reads bodies, so the schedule carries the walk times**: the host's desk approval waits `ANNEX_HEAD_SETTLE` into DESK (the walk back from the cooler), RETURN is "not looking" (he's not at his stamp), the Copier Jam's RETURN is `ANNEX_JAM_RETURN_WALK` and a bell during a jam passes the same as the override's `walk`. annex_test checks each clerk's routes against these budgets and `CLERK_RUSH_MAX`, and that no route passes another booth's stool.
- **Breaks are in a staff nook behind the counter** (levels/annex/annex_b.gd `NOOK_*`), routed along a corridor behind the stools (`CLERK_CORRIDOR_BACK`); cabinets moved back against the wall to clear it. Lobby walks from the staff door go round the desk's visitor side (`DESK_BYPASS`).
- **Puppeted props**: the big stamp in a `slam` with `{"puppet": true}` doesn't move itself; the Head NICE BOY's pose (`_approve_pose`) places it from his hands every physics tick, as a pure function of the state's time, and the stamp keeps only its sound cues. A window stamp can be `borrow()`ed by a Callable target (the self-stamp: your form; anyone else's: over their left hand, `ANNEX_BORROW_CARRY`) and drawn on top in first person; `give_back()` returns it.
- **First-person hands follow the world through one meta**: `fp_hands` on the player ({"left", "right"} world points, "stretch") overrides `player/first_person_arms.gd`'s targets; the Annex (a borrowed stamp, the big stamp) and the receipt slip set and clear it.
- **Staff bodies are solid**: an `AnimatableBody3D` capsule per staff member on `PAD_WALL_LAYER` (players mask it; thrown items and the world rays don't), `CLERK_SOLID_RADIUS` 0.45; the stand-off (`CLERK_STAND_OFF` 1.25, was 0.85) now also moves standing staff, and standing staff keep `CLERK_STAFF_GAP` apart. The position is `_base + _avoid` every tick, so it never accumulates.
- **Sound samples, not triggers, were late**: `stamp_kachunk`'s impact is its first sample (tools/sfx_r2_annex.py) and `iris_swish` is front-loaded and louder (tools/sfx_r2_stage.py); the cues already fired on their frames. Measured after: KA-CHUNK 11-25 ms (window, Window D, heist, desk; was 46-82), iris close -9 / -6 ms (was 107-255 or none).
- **A new scenario, `annex_desk`** (a full form at the desk, the Head NICE BOY 2 s from back), for recording the approval.
- **Networking:** no RPC signature or replicated property changed; no save format change. The Annex day's generation changed (the jam block's lengths, RETURN's looking flag; both peers compute it, so host and client must be on the same build: it rides the integrator's BUILD_VERSION bump). Host behavior changed (the desk settle gate, the wrong window only from a looking clerk).
- **Review fixes (same chunk).** The settle gate counts from the later of DESK's start and the end of the last SPOTTED override (`_head_seated_since`): an override that ends drops him back into DESK with the segment's old start, so he approved while still walking back from the stamp holder. Other players' hands on a borrowed stamp go through a second meta, `body_hands` on the player ({"right", "left"} world points), which `player/player.gd` feeds to `CartoonBody.set_hold` ahead of a held prop; the Annex sets it on every peer for a remote self-stamp while the stamp is `in_hand()`. A self-stamp hold also ends on the client when it walks out of the host's reach, and a NOTICE puts the stamp back (`stamp_rest`), since the host drops those holds without a message. A bell's keycap is offered only on screen and never with a stamp in your hand. Staff carry a second, wider solid at head height (`CLERK_SOLID_HEAD_RADIUS`), off while seated or at their desk so the Head NICE BOY's visitor spot stays reachable. The stand-off no longer flips sides: a walking staff member steps aside square to his way, to the side he chose when someone came near, and a standing one keeps to the side he has already stepped to (the push used to point from the player to his line, and flipped as the player crossed it: a 0.45 m pop through the player, `rec_bump3_1p` 31). A new friend scenario, `annex_heist_seen`, films someone else's self-stamp.
### 2026-11-03: The plain-text sweep: the goal line only says where, refusals have no words (x1:text)

- **Why:** Taylan: "Let's not hold our players hand through everything. Text like that screams it was made by AI. Our players are not idiots." The rule and the list of cuts are in docs/GAME.md, "Writing for players"; this entry is the code side.
- **The goal line (`ui/job_goal.gd`) answers one question: where is the job?** `goal()` returns "" on the yard, in the store without a picked job, at bedtime and once the yard's paid; in the connected town it is `UI.goal_drive_to` ("174 Maple Row · 220 m ↗") until you're within `CHEFTY_ARRIVE_RADIUS` of the curb, seated or not. The step-by-step yard logic, its helpers, `GOAL_WALK_METERS` and `ui_root.board_seen` are gone. The node, its placement and the raid dimming are unchanged, so `stage_test`'s goal step and the caption stack's obstacles still hold.
- **Refusals are a shake.** `hotbar_hud._on_refused` sets `shake_left` and no toast (`HOTBAR_TOAST_SECONDS` removed); the pickup prompt over an item with no pocket for it is `{}`. `hint_for(kind)` is only `UI.hint_<kind>` (flavor); `HINT_STAYS` is empty; the hands card's `hint` is "".
- **Supply notices:** only "Out of bags." is left (`player._supply_hint` for the rake, `rake.gd`'s refusal flash). The empty gas tool and empty can lines, and the sputter's flash in `net_prop._rpc_mower_fx`, are removed: the sputter and the gauge strip say it.
- **The first-time cue** is `rake.gd`'s `static var bagged_once`: set when this machine's player presses bag on a gold (baggable) pile label; until then the label is `UI.pile_value` ("$13 [Right click]"), after it `UI.pile_cash`. A grey label shows a pile on its way (it used to say "Pile's too thin to bag"). Static on purpose: once per run of the game, never saved.
- **World cue for getting paid:** `homeowner._beckon` (local, cosmetic, every peer for its own player): in WAITING with `chefty.bag_value() > 0`, a yard not yet paid, and this peer's player on the LOT between `TALK_REACH` and `HOMEOWNER_BECKON_METERS`, the body plays `wave` every `HOMEOWNER_BECKON_SECONDS`, the first with a babble. Reads only replicated state; sends nothing.
- **The free teammate's radio:** `quip_player._watch_annex` reads the Annex's public API (`is_active`, `roster`, `visitors`, `stage_of`, `elapsed`, `seed_value`): for a peer neither riding nor visiting it pushes `ANNEX_QUIPS.teammate_dispatch` lines on the caption stack as `&"radio"` with the radio crackle when `dispatch_due(elapsed, sent)` (`ANNEX_DISPATCH_FIRST/GAP/MAX`), naming the teammates still inside in turn, salted by the visit's seed so every free peer hears the same line and none repeats in a visit; and `teammate_out` once per teammate when their stage is "out" or the visit ends with them still inside (closing time lets everyone out without an "out" stage). No new RPC: everything it reads is already on every peer.
- **Tests:** `job_test` checks the goal line is quiet at every step of both rounds (`_expect_quiet`), that the homeowner doesn't wave with an empty truck and does wave from 12 m once Chefty has bags; `hotbar_test` 13b/13c/13e/13a expect no toast, a quiet goal line and no tool hint; `settings_test`'s rebinding checks read a phone button and the dip prompt instead of removed goal lines; `cast_test` X1 checks the dispatch schedule and that the new pools have 3+ lines; `FORMAT_ARGS` covers both pools. Same ports.
- **Networking:** no RPC, replicated property, save format or yard/task generation changed. `BUILD_VERSION` needs no bump for this chunk.
- **Review fixes (x1:text review, same day), overriding the bullets above where they differ:**
  - **The radio runs from when you start listening.** `_dispatch_visit` is the annex elapsed when this peer started listening, and `dispatch_due` takes seconds since then. Listening starts at the visit's start, on coming back out of the room as a visitor, on a late join, and for a rider whose own stage is "out" (a released rider is free again; `in_room` is `visitors.has(me)` or on the roster and not "out"). Before, the absolute schedule and a counter reset to 0 replayed every overdue dispatch on consecutive frames (a visitor leaving at 120 s got two stacked RADIO captions). Teammates already "out" when listening starts are marked heard without a line. `_dispatch_heard` counts lines over the whole visit (reset when `seed_value` changes or the Annex goes inactive), and it picks the salt and holds `ANNEX_DISPATCH_MAX`, so no line repeats across trips into the room (rec_x1fix_dispatch_lag showed the same line twice before this). With `ANNEX_FINISH_AS_GROUP` true a released rider can't yet coexist with a friend still inside; the rule is there for when it can.
  - **`bagged_once` is set by the field's "bag" stroke event** for this machine's player (`rake._on_stroke_event`, moved > 0), not by the key press, so a refused bag (out of bags, too thin by the time the host looks) keeps the cue. A client's predicted bag counts too; a prediction the host then refuses would drop the cue early, which the prediction's own checks make rare.
  - **The beckon backs off.** The first wave comes `HOMEOWNER_BECKON_NOTICE_SECONDS` after Chefty's value goes up (it counts a tossed bag in the air, before the thump), then the gaps go 6, 12, 24, 48 s (`HOMEOWNER_BECKON_BACKOFF`, `_MAX_SECONDS`); each new bag restarts the gaps and brings the next wave within 3 s.
  - **Prompts:** loose props show "Pick up" with no name (`UI.prompt_pick_up` removed); `player._grabbable_player` skips `Net.is_watcher` peers, so the dev observer is never "[E] Grab Observer".
  - **Tests:** `townmap_test` adds g1 to g4 for the goal line that's left: quiet at the store with nothing picked, "<address> · N m <arrow>" at the store with a pick, quiet once the clock closes, quiet on the street within `CHEFTY_ARRIVE_RADIUS` of the curb (TOWN, not the yard's LOT). `job_test`'s `_expect_quiet` checks stay as guards against yard lines coming back.

### 2026-11-03: R2c integration (r2c:stage, r2c:van, r2c:hide, r2c:annex, r2c:hud, x1:text)

- **Merged** in order on top of W7 (0.4.11): r2c:stage, r2c:van, r2c:hide, r2c:annex, r2c:hud, x1:text. Append-only ends of `core/constants.gd`, `docs/DECISIONS.md` and `docs/PLAYTEST.md` kept both sides; `net/dev/scenarios.gd` (NAMES, the doc list, FRIEND_SCENARIOS, the dispatch) and `net/dev/playtest_tools_test.gd` (TWO_PLAYER_SCENARIOS) carry every wave's scenarios (W7's shop ones, two_caught/two_caught_far, van_straggler_far, annex_turnstile, pile_found, annex_desk, annex_heist_seen).
- **Integration choices:**
  - `player/stage_cam.gd`: r2c:van's `ride` frame reads the van's pose through r2c:stage's `StageCam.drawn_pose()` like every other pose the stage camera reads.
  - `player/first_person_arms.gd`: r2c:annex's `fp_hands` block sits before W7 hotbar:D's return (which carries `follow`); its loop variable is `hand_side`, since the snack block above already declares `side`.
  - `props/receipt_slip.gd`: both r2c:van's `follow_view` (the slip follows whichever view is current) and r2c:annex's `holder` (your hands on its corners).
  - `ui/interaction_prompt.gd`: W7 icons:F had replaced the Label3D prompt with the screen-space pill, which r2c:hud's edits were written against. The pill stays; r2c:hud's rules move into `present()`: a hint's point must be on screen (`on_screen`, R2C_HUD_PROMPT_EDGE, through the camera `present` is given) and the pill must not land on the TaskList, DayHud or HudRows pieces (`_over_panels`, now measured with the pill's own size); `own_view` and `overlays_held` gate `current_hint` as r2c:hud wrote. `net/dev/recorder.gd` reads the prompt from the pill (`current_text`, the pill's alpha). hud_test keeps W7's `_prompt_drawn`/`_prompt_shown`.
  - `player/player.gd`: grabbing uses r2c:hud's `can_be_grabbed()` and x1:text's watcher exclusion; the climb-down and pick-up hints keep W7's `source` with x1:text's words ("Pick up", junk included: shops:5's "Pick up Toilet" follows the x1 rule).
  - `ui/strings.gd`: x1:text's rule applied to W7 text that was written before it: `prompt_unload_junk` "Unload", `prompt_vending` "Snack", `hud_out_of_bags` "Out of bags." (W7's "Homie Depot, the Snak-o-Matic, or your phone" cut), the out-of-gas lines removed. r2c:annex and x1:text both replaced the same first-visit quip; both new lines stay in the pool.
  - `docs/GAME.md`'s hotbar notes keep hotbar:D's "what you wear" lines and x1:text's note on the cut hints.
- **BUILD_VERSION 0.4.11 → 0.4.12:** r2c:van added `Annex._rpc_closing_iris` and `Shuttle._rpc_crew_run` and the CREW_RUN seat mode; r2c:annex changed Annex day generation (the jam block, RETURN no longer "looking") and host gates. r2c:stage's BOARDING `far` key, r2c:hide, r2c:hud and x1:text changed no protocol.
- **Full suite** (`tools\ci_local.ps1`, PortOffset 0, with soaks): 43 of 43 pass on the first run, 492 s, the bot soaks 67 s and 196 s. During the per-merge checks stage_test's `agree` step failed three times when run 4 at a time beside annex/encounter (worst 0.13-0.20 m against 0.05) and passed alone twice and in the full suite; it compares wall-clock samples across two processes, so it is load-sensitive. Watch it.
### 2026-11-03: Cosmetics: looks rebuilt in place, Chefty repainted while it stands still (shops chunk 7, W8)

- **Looks are World/Shops' replicated `looks` and `truck`** (shops chunk 2's save keys `shops.looks` by campaign key and `shops.truck`), changed only through the host: `request_equip(slot, item)` (a hat or shirt you own, "" for your own) and `request_apply_truck(item)` (a paint or decal the crew owns, "" peels the decal), answered "worn" / "applied" / "not_owned" / "unknown" on the existing `result` signal. Buying a cosmetic puts it on (shops chunk 2). Every peer draws from the snapshot; no per-look RPC beyond the two requests.
- **`CartoonBody.rebuild(head_parent, own_head, animate)`** (critic C20) swaps only the clothes' meshes: the torso's and the arms' sleeves, the vest, the hat's parts on the face. Every pivot, the head node, the arm pivots `set_hold` aims and anything hung on the body stay, so `player.gd _on_looks_changed` re-dresses (`dress_from_seed` + `Hats.wear`) and rebuilds in place; the first-person arms recolor their sleeves. Hats use blob detail ≤2 and no `rbox`; the test caps each at 1800 triangles with its hair (the biggest, the crown, is 1348).
- **The show is local presentation on every peer**: the hat drop, the old hat's flight, the shirt's puff, and two registered gestures (`player/dress_gestures.gd` hat_on, shirt_tug) that the first-person arms read on the same clock (`fp_amount`, `fp_tug`). A look that arrives with a client's first snapshot (`Shops.first_snapshot`, local) or within `DRESS_ANIMATE_AFTER_SECONDS` of a body being dressed goes straight on, so a late joiner never watches the whole crew re-dress. Your own cap is droppable like a bought hat (taking a hat off in the Closet); hair and the NICE BOYS' visor are not.
- **Chefty's look is `net/components/chefty_paint.gd`**, a child of either Chefty (drivable `chefty_drive.gd` or legacy `ride.gd`), each with `apply_paint(color, decal)` that rebuilds TruckLook via `vehicles.build_truck(parent, {color, decal, dents, interior})` (critic C21; decal in the cache key) and keeps the seats, the bed load and the chassis (critic C22). A new look waits until Chefty is below park speed / not riding, then plays a paint cloud and swaps inside it. **W8 fix:** a client's first snapshot only emits `truck_changed` when the truck differs from the defaults, so a peer that joined to a factory-red truck never learned its first look and put the next one on in one frame with no cloud (seen from the observer in rec_paint_3p); the node now asks Shops for the look until it has one. shop_test's `client_truck_look` checks a client shows its joined look before anyone paints.
- **Where you buy:** `SHOP_SITES.homie_depot.spots` (the rack, the cubby, the paint counter) count as Homie Depot's counter for `_near_site`. **W8 move:** the WIP had the rack, mirror and cubby on the entrance block's own face, which put the cubby and the mirror across the glass doors and the rack behind a canopy post; they're now a fitting nook (`WARDROBE_WALL_X` -12, the store's front wall south of the block; the mirror on the block's side, `MIRROR_AT`), the cubby on legs 0.27 m off the wall to clear a display window's sill. The paint counter's riser came down from 1.47 m to 1.13 m with the name board up at 2.25 m, since its sample cards hid the truck you were painting.
- **The mirror** (`props/mirror.gd`) is a SubViewport sharing the world, drawn only while a camera is within `MIRROR_RANGE` in front of it, with an off-axis frustum cut at the glass; it leaves out the first-person arms' layer (`FirstPersonArms.ARMS_LAYER` 3) and shows your body's head layer. Buying or wearing within `MIRROR_CLOSE_RANGE` of it, or a paint job within `PAINT_WATCH_RANGE` of Chefty, closes the shop window so you watch it happen.
- **Icons:** the hats, the folded shirt, the paint can and the decal cards are model icons from the icon studio (`build_cosmetic`, built from `player/hats.gd` and `props/cosmetic_models.gd`); "shirt" and "paint_swatch" are white and tinted by the caller.
- **Text cut (no hand-holding):** the Closet's "Wearing: Hard hat, Teal shirt dye" summary line (the greyed Wearing button already says it); no toast for "worn" / "applied" (the body and the truck show it).
- **Protocol:** new RPCs `Shops._rpc_request_equip` and `Shops._rpc_request_apply_truck`; no new replicated property, no save-format change (the looks and truck keys were shops chunk 2's), no yard or task generation change. The integrator should bump `BUILD_VERSION` for the RPCs.
- **Dev:** scenarios `hat_rack`, `mirror`, `paint_counter` (the last puts the drivable Chefty on its pad; `tools/play.ps1` gives it the connected town), studio subjects `hats`, `chefty_paints`, `wardrobe`, `dressing` (a hat or, with `--shirt=1`, a shirt going on, frozen at steps, front and profile).
